## Slice 2 — Bridge hardening

Status: verified 2026-09-22 (Appendix B item 1 signed by the operator once the bridge fix `7b85f04` was live — After the program); landed 2026-09-10 (commit `c2b7361` `Readiness S2: bridge token, request limits, worker pool, percent decoding, authenticated Stream Deck profile`, pushed; CI run 34487837771 recorded under \"CI (result)\" below by the follow-up commit `Readiness S2 (ledger): CI run on the branch recorded`, as in S0 and S1); every S2 lane and `npm run dev:check` green on the workstation; the hardware step is Appendix B item 1, which only the operator can walk. Closes F01 F06 F28. Depends on S0.

Scope (as landed): new `E/control_surface_http.rs` holds the bridge transport. `load_or_create_bridge_token(app_data_dir)` → `<app-data>/control-surface.token`, 64 hex characters from `getrandom` 0.3 (already in the lock file through the Tauri shell, so no crate was fetched), created on the first launch of an install and reused afterwards, `0600` on unix, a damaged file regenerated, `SSE_CONTROL_SURFACE_TOKEN` overriding it for lanes without touching the file; a token that cannot be written fails the bootstrap loudly instead of starting an unauthenticated bridge. `start_control_surface_bridge(db, log, port, token)`. `HttpRequest` carries lower-cased `headers`; `authorize(&request, token, port)` runs on every route before the body is parsed: any `Origin` → 403, `Host` outside {`127.0.0.1:<port>`, `localhost:<port>`} → 400, missing / wrong `Authorization: Bearer` (constant-time compare) → 401 with a `WWW-Authenticate: Bearer` challenge. `read_http_request` reads through a `RequestSource` (`TcpStream`; a `Cursor` in tests): 8 KiB header cap (431), 16 KiB body cap checked against the declared `Content-Length` before any body byte is read (413), POST without a usable `Content-Length` or any `Transfer-Encoding` → 411, conflicting `Content-Length` headers → 400, a 1 s deadline enforced through the socket read timeout and an `Instant` check → 408. `run_control_surface_bridge`: one acceptor, `sync_channel(16)`, four named workers; queue full → 503 written by the acceptor; a handler that panics is caught and logged, the worker carries on; refusals are logged at most once per status per minute; every response is followed by a write-shutdown and a bounded drain so a refused client reads its status instead of a reset. `query_parameter` percent-decodes names and values (`%XX`, `+`). Status texts 401 / 403 / 408 / 411 / 413 / 431 / 503. `E/control_surface.rs` keeps the deck handlers and `ControlSurfaceError` (seven new variants, `status_code` now `pub(crate)`) and shares its test fixtures as `test_support`. `E/bootstrap.rs`: `RuntimeContext.control_surface_token`, loaded before the bridge starts, never part of a snapshot. `E/exports.rs`: `generate_companion_config(base_url, surface, token)` walks the finished profile and sets the generic-http `header` option of every bridge action to `{"Authorization":"Bearer <token>"}` — keys, dials, per-action LCD refreshes and the 1 s LCD poll trigger. `scripts/native-control-surface-qualification.mjs`: reads the token file the packaged engine wrote, sends the header on every positive check, then proves the refusals — no token 401 with the challenge, wrong token 401, `Origin` 403 and foreign `Host` 400 over raw sockets, a 17 KiB body 413, `Content-Length: 99999999` 413, `Content-Length: 4000` with ten bytes held open → 408 with the first byte inside 1.5 s, `%2F` in `key` reaching the handler decoded, `%5F` resolving to the known key, and the refused `deleteProject` leaving the project count unchanged — and exports the profile through `exports.companion.export` to check that every bridge request in it carries the token. Docs: `docs/OPERATIONS.md` (the token paragraph under "Stream Deck Audio Surface", the 401 step under "bridge stops responding", the qualification section), `native/protocol/v1.md` (authentication, refusals, limits, decoding), `docs/RELEASE.md`, a CHANGELOG Changed bullet. `native/Cargo.toml`, `native/rust-engine/Cargo.toml`, `native/Cargo.lock`: the `getrandom` edge.
**Rescope:** four deviations, none silent. (1) The transport lives in a new module `E/control_surface_http.rs` instead of growing `E/control_surface.rs`: with its tests the single file would have passed the 2,000-line `file:health` guard — F26's own gate — and the split mirrors the existing `control_surface_audio.rs`; the deck handlers did not move. (2) The design put the header into `exports.rs::http_post` and `lcd_refreshes`; it landed as a walk over the finished profile (`apply_bridge_auth_header`), because those helpers have dozens of callers across `exports.rs` and `exports_audio.rs`, and the walk is what lets a test prove "every bridge request, and nothing else" — `companion_export_carries_the_bridge_token_on_every_request` counts the token once per bridge action in the serialized file. (3) The design's 408 lane case, `Content-Length: 99999999` with a short body, is a 413 under its own cap rule (a declared length over 16 KiB is refused before a body byte is read); the lane asserts exactly that, and drives the 408 with `Content-Length: 4000` held open — the stall the finding describes. (4) `worker_pool_returns_503_when_saturated` runs the real pool with one worker and one queue slot through `run_control_surface_bridge`'s parameters (production stays 4 / 16): saturating four workers and sixteen slots deterministically would need twenty idle sockets and a multi-second window in every test run.
Gates before: `native:bridge:win:verify` asserted that the bridge binds and answers the five routes; nothing asserted who may call it, how much it reads, or how many threads it starts; `query_parameter` replaced `%20` and nothing else; no test fed `read_http_request` a hand-built request, and `handle_control_surface_connection` spawned one thread per accepted connection with a 2 s read timeout and no body cap.
Tests added: `control_surface_http::tests` — `read_http_request_parses_headers_lower_cased_and_body`, `read_http_request_rejects_body_over_cap` (17 KiB and 99999999), `read_http_request_rejects_oversized_headers`, `read_http_request_rejects_post_without_content_length`, `read_http_request_rejects_chunked_transfer_encoding`, `read_http_request_times_out_on_short_body` (a stalling reader), `read_http_request_honours_the_deadline`, `read_http_request_rejects_a_truncated_body`, `authorize_rejects_missing_bearer`, `authorize_rejects_wrong_token` (wrong, truncated, extended, `Basic`, bare), `authorize_rejects_origin`, `authorize_rejects_foreign_host`, `authorize_accepts_loopback_hosts_with_the_token`, `authorization_precedes_body_parsing`, `query_parameter_decodes_percent`, `token_file_is_created_once_and_reused` (mode `0600` asserted on unix), `token_file_with_junk_is_regenerated`, `token_env_override_wins_and_leaves_the_file_alone`, `bridge_refuses_unauthenticated_requests_and_serves_authenticated_ones` (a real loopback bridge: 401 with the challenge, 401, 403, 400, 413, 408 on the deadline, 200 through `localhost`, `%2F` decoded, `%5F` served, no action stamped by any refused request), `worker_pool_returns_503_when_saturated` (503 from the acceptor while the pool is full, then a 200 once the idle connections time out); `exports::tests::companion_export_carries_the_bridge_token_on_every_request`. Tests changed (old → new → why): `exports::tests` — seven `generate_companion_config(base_url, surface)` calls → `generate_companion_config(base_url, surface, TEST_TOKEN)` → the export needs the token; no assertion changed. `control_surface::tests` — `TestDir` / `ready_audio_test_db` → `control_surface::test_support` (`pub(crate)`, plus a `path()` accessor) → shared with the new module's tests; no assertion changed. The four test `RuntimeContext` literals (`app.rs`, `app_state.rs`, `commissioning.rs`, `support.rs`) gain `control_surface_token`. The existing handler tests call the handlers directly and are unchanged.
Lanes: `npm run native:test`, `npm run rust:clippy`, `npm run scripts:test`, `npm run native:engine:build && npm run native:package:win:local && npm run native:bridge:win:verify`. Done when an unauthenticated POST → 401, the exported profile carries the header on every action and LCD poll, and the qualification is green including the refusals.
Validation (workstation, 2026-09-10): `cargo fmt` applied; `rust:clippy` clean under `-D warnings`; `native:test` 331 passed / 1 ignored (tauri-shell 12, engine 311 + 1 ignored hardware-bound, `contract.rs` 4, `end_to_end.rs` 4 — was 310 / 1; the twenty bridge tests take 2.6 s, the saturation case included); `scripts:test` 85 tests, 81 passed, 4 skipped (the `0.0.0.0` guard covers the new lane code); `format:check`, `lint`, `protocol:check` (contract unchanged) and `file:health` (`control_surface_http.rs` 1,346 lines, `control_surface.rs` 1,451) clean; `node scripts/check-operator-copy.mjs` 0 hits (the front-end did not change); `native:engine:build` → `native:package:win:local` (the packaged engine is the freshly built debug engine beside the 2026-09-04 shell exe; this lane does not exercise the shell) → `native:bridge:win:verify` passed in 1.7 s with the steps `packaged-engine-start`, `bridge-token-file`, `bridge-snapshot-contract`, `bridge-http-routes`, `bridge-request-refusals` (no token 401, wrong token 401, `Origin` 403, foreign `Host` 400, 17 KiB 413, 99999999 413, stalled body 408 with the first byte after 1,011 ms, `%2F` 400 naming the decoded key; project count 2 before and after the refused `deleteProject`) and `profile-carries-token` (272 bridge requests, 25 of them in the LCD poll trigger); `npm run dev:check` green end to end. The lane ran on the fixture data with no verified audio, so its audio branch was the gated-409 path, as before. Companion was running (the export bound the page-follow triggers to the physical deck); TotalMix FX untouched; nothing listened on 38201 (the lane reserves an ephemeral port).
Baselines: no operator-visible change; the visual lanes were not run.
Operator hands: **Appendix B item 1.** Companion still holds the profile exported before this slice, which carries no token; once this build runs on the workstation the app writes the token at its first start, and every request from the old profile is refused with 401 — the deck goes idle, the app itself is unaffected. Export the Stream Deck profile from Setup step 1, import it in Companion with Full Reset & Import, press one deck key and watch one LCD refresh, then sign item 1; S2 moves to `verified`. CHANGELOG and OPERATIONS say so in the operator's words ("Stream Deck profile"); the copy gate scans the front-end, which did not change, and holds at 0.
CI (result, 2026-09-10): run 34487837771 (https://github.com/Fikarn/sse-exed-studio-control/actions/runs/34487837771), event `push`, sha `c2b7361`, created within five seconds of the push; ten minutes end to end. Green: `lint` (0.5 min), `frontend-test` (0.5), `frontend-typecheck` (0.6), `format-protocol` (1.3), `rust` (2.2 — rustfmt, clippy, check, test with the twenty bridge tests and the export test on Linux, the two real-socket cases and the unix `0600` assertion included, plus `native:acceptance`), `tauri-foundation` (2.4), `qualification` (7.4 — the shell-started engines wrote their token files into the runners' temp app-data and both lanes passed as in S1). Red, expected: `frontend-e2e` — 94 failed / 321 passed in 8.7 min: the same 52 `visual-review` + 39 `storybook` linux snapshot failures as S0 and S1 (73 stale + 18 missing) and the same `tests/audio-arm-countdown.spec.ts:45` dwell case, plus two runner-timing cases in specs S2 did not touch: `tests/audio.spec.ts:1054` (the 48 V arm-then-apply — the second click landed after the dwell and `data-armed` read `false`; already on the S13 quarantine list) and `tests/lighting.spec.ts:721` (the rotate-handle drag landed at 0° instead of near 82°; a further S13 quarantine candidate, noted under Slice 13). S2 changed no rendered front-end source — `frontend-test` and `frontend-typecheck` are green and the failing titles differ from S1's run by exactly those two — so no new visual diff exists and the linux refresh stays Appendix B item 6.

## Slice 3 — Storage integrity + database backups

Status: verified 2026-09-10 (commit `e4c283d` `Readiness S3: integrity check at start, verified database backups, pre-migration copies, synchronous FULL, recovery copy`, pushed; CI run 34507456992 recorded under "CI (result)" below by the follow-up commit `Readiness S3 (ledger): CI run on the branch recorded`, as in S0–S2; every S3 lane and `npm run dev:check` green on the workstation; no hardware exposure — the backups accumulate on their own, and Appendix B item 4 is the operator's restore check once S7 lands the restore). Closes F02 F13 F21. Depends on S0.

Scope (as landed): `E/storage.rs` — `configure_connection` sets `synchronous = FULL`; a typed `StorageError { Corrupt { db_path, detail } | MigrationFailed { key, detail } }` travels boxed in `EngineResult` and is recovered by downcast; `initialize_database(db_path, backups_dir)` opens the file, runs `PRAGMA integrity_check(8)` and returns `StorageError::Corrupt` before any migration or default touches it when SQLite refuses the file (`SQLITE_NOTADB` / `SQLITE_CORRUPT` while opening or checking) or the check answers anything but `ok`. `E/storage_backups.rs` (new module) — `SnapshotReason { PreMigration, Daily, Shutdown, PreRestore }` (`as_str`, `retention` 5 / 14 / 3 / 5); `snapshot_database(db_path, backups_dir, reason)` → `VACUUM INTO backups/db-<YYYY-MM-DDTHH-MM-SS-mmmZ>-<reason>.sqlite3` on the live connection (a consistent, compacted copy; the UTC millisecond stamp is computed in-process so a corrupt database cannot stop the name, and the names sort in write order), then the copy is opened on its own, converted to `journal_mode = DELETE` (one self-contained file, no `-wal` / `-shm`), integrity-checked, flushed with `sync_all` (opened for write — `FlushFileBuffers` refuses a read-only handle on Windows), and deleted again if any of that fails; `prune_snapshots(backups_dir)` removes the oldest of each reason beyond its retention by name and leaves every other file alone (support archives included); `newest_snapshot(backups_dir)`. Back in `E/storage.rs`, `migrate_schema(connection, backups_dir)` takes the PreMigration copy on the same connection, after the idempotent base DDL and before the first migration step, whenever `0 < current_schema_version < STORAGE_SCHEMA_VERSION` — a brand-new database (version 0) has nothing to protect and gets none; `seed_lighting_palettes_v6` returns `StorageError::MigrationFailed { key: "app.lighting.editor.state" }` for unparsable JSON or a non-object value, so the v6 transaction rolls back and the schema version stays at 5. `E/bootstrap.rs` — `bootstrap_runtime` splits into `bootstrap_runtime_from_paths(RuntimePaths)` (testable without env or ports; a storage failure returns before the bridge starts); `StartupFailure { code: &'static str, message }` implements `Error`; `storage_startup_failure` maps `Corrupt` → `STORAGE_CORRUPT` and `MigrationFailed` → `STORAGE_MIGRATION_FAILED` (a code the design's error type implied but did not name) with a sentence that names the file, the newest database backup (or that none exists yet) and the way out ("Restore a backup from Setup / Support; the file itself was left untouched"), and writes an ERROR line to the engine log; `startup_failure_code(&dyn Error)` gives `main.rs` the code (`BOOTSTRAP_FAILED` for anything else). `E/main.rs` — the bootstrap failure event carries that code; `spawn_snapshot_scheduler` (named thread, first Daily copy 5 min after start, then every 24 h) and a Shutdown copy in the stdin-EOF path right after `app.shutdown()`; both log INFO / WARN and never fail the process. `FA/app/startup/startupHelpers.ts` — `getFailureTitle` answers "Saved data needs attention" and `formatFailureCode` "Saved data check failed" / "Saved data upgrade failed" for the two codes; the "Restore a backup from Setup / Support" sentence is the engine's, rendered verbatim as the display's sentence, so no other surface changed. `scripts/tauri-setup-support-qualification.mjs` — scenario `corrupt-db` as step 4/4: junk at `studio-control.sqlite3` in a fresh app-data → `lifecycle === "failed"` with `startupFailure.code === "STORAGE_CORRUPT"`, stage `bootstrap`, a sentence naming a backup, and the junk file byte-identical afterwards (evidence check `corrupt-db-reaches-recovery-surface`). Docs: CHANGELOG (Added), `docs/OPERATIONS.md` ("The app fails before the dashboard" with the manual database restore until S7; "Data Safety"), `native/protocol/v1.md` (the two codes in the error taxonomy). Test convenience: `storage::initialize_test_database(db_path)` (cfg(test)) derives the backups directory beside the database, as the runtime lays it out.
**Rescope:** two deviations, neither silent. (1) The design listed `E/storage.rs`, `E/bootstrap.rs`, `E/main.rs`, `FA/startup/startupHelpers.ts` and the lane script, on the premise that `main.rs` already emits `engine.startupFailed` with a code — true — and that the shell shows it. The first run of the new `corrupt-db` scenario showed the recovery surface reading `ENGINE_READY_TIMEOUT` while the engine's stderr carried the right sentence: `EC/transports/tauriTransport.ts` invoked `engine_start` without awaiting the `engine://event` listener registration, so an event the engine emits within milliseconds of its spawn was dropped (a Tauri event with no listener is not queued), and `EC/store/createShellStore.ts` would in any case have replaced a failure that arrived before the ready gate existed with a ready timeout ten seconds later. The existing recovery scenario never noticed because a blocked app-data directory fails inside the shell's `engine_start` itself; an engine-side `PROTOCOL_MISMATCH` would have been lost the same way. Both files are now in scope: the transport holds the registered listener before it starts the engine, and a failure the engine reported during the bootstrap short-circuits the ready gate and outranks whatever the bootstrap trips over afterwards. Guarded by `tauriTransport.test.ts` ("is listening for engine events before it starts the engine", "releases the listener when the shell refuses to start the engine") and the store test "keeps the engine-reported failure instead of a ready timeout"; the lane scenario proves the path end to end through the real shell. (2) `E/storage.rs` had reached 2,421 lines with the backup code and its tests — over the 2,000-line `file:health` guard, F26's own gate — so the backup code lives in a new module `E/storage_backups.rs` with its three tests, and the inline tests of `storage.rs` moved to `E/storage/tests.rs` (the `planning/tests.rs` / `audio/tests.rs` precedent); pure moves, no assertion changed: `storage.rs` 889 lines, `storage/tests.rs` 1,176, `storage_backups.rs` 410.
Gates before: `initialize_database` ran `PRAGMA integrity_check` after migrating and stored the answer in `StorageBootstrap.integrity_check` for the health summary; nothing acted on it, so a damaged database was opened, migrated in place and reported in a sentence. No test fed the storage layer a file that was not a database. `seed_lighting_palettes_v6` returned `Ok(())` on unparsable JSON and `migrate_schema_v3_to_v4_handles_malformed_editor_state_json` asserted exactly that. `configure_connection` set `synchronous = NORMAL` with no test on the pragma. No code path wrote a copy of the database; the only backups were the JSON support archives the operator exports by hand. The setup-support lane's recovery step covered a blocked app-data directory (`BOOTSTRAP_FAILED`, raised by the shell) only, and no test drove an engine-raised startup failure through the Tauri transport.
Tests added: `storage::tests` (`E/storage/tests.rs`) — `connection_uses_synchronous_full`, `initialize_refuses_corrupt_database` (a junk file and a database with a damaged second page, both left byte-identical), `migration_snapshots_before_upgrading_v5` (the copy holds version 5 without palettes; a second start writes no further copy), `blob_migration_failure_rolls_back` (`MigrationFailed` with the key, version stays 5, value untouched, the pre-migration copy present, the repaired database upgrades); `storage_backups::tests` — `snapshot_writes_verified_copy` (name, reason, `journal_mode = delete`, no `-wal` / `-shm`, integrity `ok`, the data and schema version present, the live database still in WAL, `newest_snapshot`), `prune_keeps_retention` (retention per reason, oldest removed by name, four bystander files untouched), `file_timestamp_is_utc_to_the_millisecond`; `bootstrap::tests` — `corrupt_db_yields_storage_corrupt` (code, the file and the newest backup in the sentence, the ERROR log line, no bridge started), `migration_failure_maps_to_its_own_code` (and a plain error stays `BOOTSTRAP_FAILED`); `frontend/app` `startupHelpers.test.ts` — `formats STORAGE_CORRUPT`, `formats STORAGE_MIGRATION_FAILED`, the generic title for other codes; `engine-client` `tauriTransport.test.ts` (2 cases) and `createShellStore.test.ts` "keeps the engine-reported failure instead of a ready timeout"; setup-support lane scenario `corrupt-db`. Tests changed (old → new → why): `migrate_schema_v3_to_v4_handles_malformed_editor_state_json` → `migrate_schema_refuses_malformed_editor_state_json_at_the_blob_step` — old: the whole migration succeeds despite the bad JSON and version 4 is recorded; new: versions 4 and 5 commit, the v6 blob step returns `MigrationFailed` for `app.lighting.editor.state`, the value is untouched and a pre-migration backup exists — because silently skipping an unreadable value is the F13 finding. `migrate_schema_is_idempotent` passes the backups directory (signature). `initialize_database_applies_planning_schema_and_defaults` gains two assertions (integrity `ok`; no backup for a brand-new database). The 72 test call sites of `initialize_database(db_path)` in eleven files became `initialize_test_database(db_path)`; no assertion changed.
Lanes: `npm run native:test`, `npm run frontend:test`, `npm run tauri:setup-support:qualify`. Done when a corrupt DB reaches the recovery surface, upgrading a v5 DB leaves `backups/db-*-pre-migration.sqlite3`, and `PRAGMA synchronous` reads 2. Operator copy says "backup", never "snapshot" (copy gate). Open risk (unchanged): if metering-thread writes stay frequent after S10, rescope F21 to `NORMAL` + more frequent backups.
Validation (workstation, 2026-09-10): `cargo fmt` applied; `rust:clippy` clean under `-D warnings`; `native:test` 340 passed / 1 ignored (tauri-shell 12, engine 320 + 1 ignored hardware-bound, `contract.rs` 4, `end_to_end.rs` 4 — was 331 / 1; the four end-to-end cases each exit through the new shutdown backup); `frontend:test` — `frontend/app` 62 tests in 10 files, `engine-client` 7 tests in 3 files (the transport file new); `frontend:typecheck` clean; `node scripts/check-operator-copy.mjs` 0 hits; prettier and eslint clean on every changed file. `tauri:setup-support:qualify` ran three times: the first run, on the design's file list alone, failed its new step 4 with `ENGINE_READY_TIMEOUT` while the engine's stderr carried the right sentence (the delivery gap recorded under **Rescope:**); after the transport and store fixes it passed all four steps, and it passed again on the final, split engine code — steps 1–3 as before (`clean-startup-routes-to-commissioning`, `support-section-toggle-persists`, `commissioning-publish-unlocks-dashboard`, `backup-export-creates-archive`, `diagnostics-export-writes-report`, `backup-restore-round-trips-native-support-backup`, `persisted-restart-restores-dashboard-state`, `bootstrap-failure-remains-operator-visible`) plus `corrupt-db-reaches-recovery-surface` (code `STORAGE_CORRUPT`, stage `bootstrap`, the sentence naming the file and that no backup exists yet, the junk file byte-identical afterwards); about ninety seconds per run, the JSON `backupCount` checks unaffected by the new `.sqlite3` files because the support listing counts `.json` archives only. `npm run dev:check` green end to end (format, lint, `scripts:test`, `file:health` — `storage.rs` 889 lines, `storage/tests.rs` 1,176, `storage_backups.rs` 410 — rustfmt, clippy, `protocol:check` with the contract unchanged, `frontend:typecheck`, `frontend:test`, `native:check`, `native:test`). TotalMix FX and Companion untouched; nothing listened on 38201 or 4173.
Baselines: no operator-visible layout change — the recovery display prints the new title in its existing word slot and the engine's sentence in the sentence slot; no fixture board carries a `STORAGE_CORRUPT` failure, so no ratchet or visual baseline moves; the visual lanes were not run.
Operator hands: none for this slice. The database backups accumulate under `<app-data>/backups` on their own; until S7 lands the Support surface's restore of a `.sqlite3` backup, `docs/OPERATIONS.md` ("The app fails before the dashboard") carries the manual restore, and Appendix B item 4 (after S7) is the operator's restore check.
CI (result, 2026-09-10): run 34507456992 (https://github.com/Fikarn/sse-exed-studio-control/actions/runs/34507456992), event `push`, sha `e4c283d`, created within five seconds of the push; ten minutes end to end. Green: `lint` (0.4 min), `frontend-test` (0.4 — the three new suites among them), `frontend-typecheck` (0.5), `format-protocol` (1.2 — `file:health` with `storage.rs` at 889 lines), `tauri-foundation` (2.0), `rust` (2.5 — rustfmt, clippy, check, the 340 tests on Linux with the corrupt-file, damaged-page, `VACUUM INTO`, write-handle `sync_all` and retention cases included, plus `native:acceptance`, whose engines now write shutdown backups), `qualification` (7.1 — the setup-support lane's four steps under xvfb, `corrupt-db` included, and the workspace lane). Red, expected: `frontend-e2e` — 94 failed / 321 passed in 8.8 min: the same 52 `visual-review` + 39 `storybook` linux snapshot failures as S0–S2 (73 stale + 18 missing), the same `tests/audio-arm-countdown.spec.ts:45` dwell case and the same `tests/audio.spec.ts:1054` 48 V arm-then-apply case (S13 quarantine list), plus one runner-timing case that differs from S2's run: `tests/audio-talkback.spec.ts:64` (`keyboard.down("t")` never read as pressed within 5 s under the headless runner — a focus/timing case in a spec S3 did not touch; noted under Slice 13 as a further quarantine candidate), while S2's `tests/lighting.spec.ts:721` rotate-handle case passed this time. S3 changed no rendered front-end source and no fixture path — the store change reacts only to an `engine.startupFailed` event, which no Playwright fixture emits — so no new visual diff exists and the linux refresh stays Appendix B item 6.

## Slice 4 — Shell hardening

Status: verified 2026-09-10 (commit `f3db990` `Readiness S4: async shell commands, duplicate-id refusal, content security policy, folder-bound open and export`, pushed; CI run 34514042594 recorded under "CI (result)" below by the follow-up commit `Readiness S4 (ledger): CI run on the branch recorded`, as in S0–S3; every S4 lane and `npm run dev:check` green on the workstation; no hardware exposure, so the slice goes straight to `verified` per the status vocabulary). Closes F07 F08 F15. Depends on S0.

Scope (as landed): `T/main.rs` — `EngineState.bridge` is an `Arc<EngineBridge>`; `off_main_thread(task)` awaits `tauri::async_runtime::spawn_blocking`, and the seven commands `engine_start`, `engine_request`, `engine_stop`, `engine_summary`, `shell_confirm_close`, `shell_open_path` and `shell_export_diagnostics` are `async fn` commands that hand their work to it (Tauri runs an `async fn` command on its async runtime, never on the thread that paints the window and dispatches the next IPC call; the blocking pool takes the wait). `shell_open_path`: `allowed_open_roots()` = the app-data directory (which holds `backups` and `exports`), the logs directory (it may sit elsewhere through `SSE_LOG_DIR`) and `SSE_UPDATE_REPOSITORY_PATH` when set; `resolve_open_path(target, roots)` canonicalises the target (symlinks, junctions, `..`) and requires it to sit under a canonicalised root — a root that does not exist admits nothing — answering `PATH_OUTSIDE_APP_DATA: <path> is outside the app data, logs and update folders, so it was not opened.` or `PATH_NOT_FOUND: …`; the original spelling goes to the platform opener because `canonicalize` yields a `\\?\` path on Windows that Explorer refuses. `shell_export_diagnostics(report)`: `write_diagnostics_report(<app-data>/exports, report)` names the file `diagnostics-<YYYY-MM-DDTHH-MM-SS-mmmZ>.json` (`file_timestamp` / `civil_from_days` mirrored from `storage_backups.rs`, so backups and exports sort together; a taken name waits 1 ms and retries, so two exports in one millisecond get two files) — no other directory, and no temp-directory fallback. `#[cfg(feature = "test-bridge")] shell_test_bridge_export_diagnostics_to(report, directory)` serves the lanes and is registered in the test-bridge handler list only. `T/engine.rs` — `EngineBridge::request` goes through the pending map's `entry`: an occupied id is answered with `error_response(id, "DUPLICATE_REQUEST_ID", …)` — a protocol error envelope, so the front-end throws its message — before anything is written, and the original waiter keeps its slot; `EXPORTS_DIR_NAME`, `exports_dir_for(app_data_dir)`; `start` creates `<app-data>/exports` beside the logs directory so the Diagnostics key can open it before the first export. `native/tauri-shell/tauri.conf.json` — `app.security.csp` exactly as designed; `app.security.devCsp` is the same policy widened for the Vite document (`http://127.0.0.1:4173` in `default-src` / `script-src` / `style-src`, `'unsafe-inline'` in `script-src` for the React refresh preamble, `http://127.0.0.1:4173` and `ws://127.0.0.1:4173` in `connect-src` for HMR). `scripts/tauri-smoke.mjs` — `verifyContentSecurityPolicy(config)`: `csp` present; `default-src 'self'`; `script-src 'self'` without `'unsafe-inline'`, `'unsafe-eval'`, `*`, `http:` or `https:`; `connect-src` with `ipc:` and `http://ipc.localhost`; no `*` in any directive; `devCsp` present with the IPC origins and the HMR socket. `EC/transports/tauriTransport.ts` — `createSessionNonce()` (12 hex characters from `crypto.randomUUID`, a base-36 fallback), a `sequence` per instance, `id = ${method}:${sequence}:${sessionNonce}`. `FA/app/shellCommands.ts` — `exportShellDiagnostics(report)` takes no directory (fixture path `diagnostics-fixture.json`); `openShellPath` and `exportShellDiagnostics` wrap a refused command's plain-string rejection into an `Error`, so the surfaces show the shell's sentence with its code instead of their generic line. `FA/app/tauriShellTestBridge.ts` — an `exportShellDiagnostics` command with a `directory` invokes `shell_test_bridge_export_diagnostics_to`, one without it the production command; `testBridge.heartbeat` counts up every 250 ms and rewrites the status file whatever the shell state does (every write is an IPC call the shell answers on its main thread); `testBridge.cspViolations` keeps the last twenty `securitypolicyviolation` events (blocked URI, directive, source file, line). `FA/app/setup/SetupSupportPilot.tsx`, `FA/app/setup/SetupRecoverySurface.tsx` — the export call drops its `logsDir` argument; the Diagnostics key opens `runtimePaths.exportsDir ?? appDataDir` (was `logsDir ?? appDataDir`). `E/bootstrap.rs` `EXPORTS_DIR_NAME`; `E/app_state.rs`, `E/app.rs`, `E/main.rs` — `runtime.paths.exportsDir` (`<app-data>/exports`) in the app snapshot, the health snapshot and both startup-failure payloads. `scripts/tauri-setup-support-qualification.mjs` — step 1 gains, before the publish, `pickUnroutedBridgeAddress()` (the first of `203.0.113.113`, `198.51.100.113`, `192.0.2.113`, `100.127.255.113` whose port-80 connect from Node is still pending after 1.2 s — RFC 5737 / RFC 6598 addresses no LAN or CI network hosts; if none stalls the lane fails with a sentence, it never skips) and `runStalledRequestCheck` (writes a `runCommissioningCheck` lighting probe against it, samples the status file every 50 ms until the command lands, then asserts the probe took ≥ 1 s and failed as unreachable, that ≥ 2 heartbeats landed while it was in flight, and that the longest gap between status updates — command write, each heartbeat change, command completion — stayed under 1 s), followed by a lighting probe against `127.0.0.1` (a refused connect counts as reachable) so the persisted bridge address points back at loopback; after the directory export a default export is asserted to land in `<app-data>/exports` as `diagnostics-<UTC ms>.json` (evidence checks `shell-stays-responsive-during-stalled-request` with `bridgeIp` / `probeMs` / `heartbeats` / `maxGapMs`, and `diagnostics-export-defaults-to-app-data-exports`). Docs: `native/tauri-shell/README.md` (a hardening paragraph), `native/protocol/v1.md` (`exportsDir`), `docs/OPERATIONS.md` (where the export lands), `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md` (the lane's coverage), a CHANGELOG Changed bullet.
**Rescope:** five deviations, none silent. (1) The seven commands are `async fn`s that await `spawn_blocking`, not sync functions under `#[tauri::command(async)]`: the macro runs a sync body marked `async` inside `async_runtime::spawn` (`sync_threadpool` in `tauri-macros`), i.e. on one of the runtime's few worker threads, so the 10 s wait in `EngineBridge::request` would pin a worker per stalled request — a store refresh fans several out (F11), enough to pin every worker and stall the remaining commands; `spawn_blocking` uses the blocking pool, which exists for exactly that. `State<'_, EngineState>` stays; the bridge sits in an `Arc` so the closure can own it. (2) The allowed roots include the update repository (`SSE_UPDATE_REPOSITORY_PATH`): both Support surfaces carry an "Update folder" key that opens it, which the design's list (app-data, logs, backups, exports) would have broken; backups and exports sit inside app-data, so the allowlist is app-data, logs and the update repository. (3) The Diagnostics keys had to follow the file: both surfaces opened `logsDir` as "Diagnostics" because the pilot passed `logsDir` as the export directory, and with exports moving to `<app-data>/exports` the engine now reports `runtime.paths.exportsDir` (four payload sites) and the keys open it, the shell creating the folder at engine start. Files the design did not list: `E/bootstrap.rs`, `E/app_state.rs`, `E/app.rs`, `E/main.rs`, `FA/app/setup/SetupSupportPilot.tsx`, `FA/app/setup/SetupRecoverySurface.tsx`, `scripts/tauri-smoke.mjs`, `scripts/tauri-setup-support-qualification.mjs`. The fixture transport was left alone — its recovery boards would otherwise gain a row and move a baseline — and can carry `exportsDir` when S7 lists exports. (4) `tauri:smoke` is not a webview: `scripts/tauri-smoke.mjs` spawns the engine over stdin/stdout and reads the config files; it never launches the shell and cannot see a blank webview. It now asserts the policy's presence and strictness, the durable guard the traceability row names; the behavioural proof for the packaged build was a one-off launch on the workstation (under Validation) of a release shell built with `--features test-bridge`, because only a release build serves the embedded assets under the policy: `is_dev()` is "no `custom-protocol` feature", `tauri dev` loads Vite's document from `devUrl`, and Tauri 2.11.4 injects a policy only through `get_asset` (the `tauri://` protocol) — `manager::csp()` is consulted nowhere else on desktop. The design's "one dev launch" therefore proves nothing about the policy; the qualification lane's dev launches prove the dev shell is unaffected, and `devCsp` is set so the dev document keeps a working policy should a Tauri update start injecting one. (5) The proof build and the production build share the release exe; the production `tauri:build` ran last, and a 12 s launch of that exe with the test-bridge environment wrote no status file, so the shipped shell carries no test bridge.
Gates before: nothing asserted which thread answered a command — `engine_request` sat in `recv_timeout(10 s)` on the main thread, so every IPC call, status write and window event queued behind a stalled engine reply (the previous shell froze the status file for 1,558 ms of a 1,995 ms probe when the new check ran against it); no test built two requests with one id (`tauriTransport.test.ts` held the two S3 cases only); `shell_open_path` handed any existing path to the platform opener and `shell_export_diagnostics` wrote to any absolute directory the webview named (a temp directory when no runtime paths resolved); `tauri.conf.json` had no `csp`; `tauri:smoke` checked the window count and the parity-fixture list only.
Tests added: `engine::tests::request_refuses_duplicate_pending_id` (the duplicate is answered with the code and the method, the original waiter still receives a reply routed by id, a fresh id fails on the write with no engine and leaves no slot); `shell_path_policy_tests::open_path_rejects_outside_app_data` (the roots, files beneath them and an update repository open; a sibling directory, a `..` climb, the temp root, `C:\Windows`, a missing path and an empty root list are refused with their codes), `diagnostics_report_lands_with_a_utc_stamp_and_never_overwrites`, `file_timestamp_is_utc_to_the_millisecond`; `tauriTransport.test.ts` "ids unique within one millisecond" (200 requests in one tick: all distinct, sequence 1…200, one nonce) and "a second transport instance carries its own session nonce"; `tauri:smoke` `verifyContentSecurityPolicy`; lane checks `shell-stays-responsive-during-stalled-request` and `diagnostics-export-defaults-to-app-data-exports`. Tests changed (old → new → why): `tauriTransport.test.ts`'s hoisted `invoke` mock → also records `engine_request` ids and answers an `ok` envelope → the new cases drive `request()`; the two S3 cases are unchanged. The lane's existing `exportShellDiagnostics` step keeps its `directory` and now reaches the shell through `shell_test_bridge_export_diagnostics_to`; its assertion is unchanged.
Lanes: `npm run native:test`, `npm run frontend:test`, `npm run native:engine:build && npm run tauri:build && npm run tauri:smoke`, `npm run tauri:setup-support:qualify`. Done when the status bridge keeps writing during a 1.5 s probe, duplicate ids are impossible, the CSP is present, and `shell_open_path("C:\\Windows")` errors — all four met (307 ms longest gap over a 1,933 ms probe; sequence + nonce ids and the shell-side refusal; policy present, asserted by the smoke and proved on the packaged build; the unit test refuses `C:\Windows`).
Validation (workstation, 2026-09-10): `cargo fmt` applied; `rust:fmt:check` clean; `rust:clippy` clean under `-D warnings`, and `cargo clippy -p sse-exed-tauri-shell --all-targets --features test-bridge -- -D warnings` clean; `native:check` clean; `native:test` 344 passed / 1 ignored (tauri-shell 16 — was 12 — engine 320 + 1 ignored hardware-bound, `contract.rs` 4, `end_to_end.rs` 4; was 340 / 1); `frontend:typecheck` clean (27 fixture scenarios); `frontend:test` — app 62 tests in 10 files, design-system 175 in 24, engine-client 9 in 3 (was 7), tokens 15 in 1; eslint and prettier clean on every changed file; `node scripts/check-operator-copy.mjs` 0 hits; `file:health` passed (`main.rs` 1,643 lines, `engine.rs` 722). `native:engine:build && tauri:build && tauri:smoke` passed (the release shell in 18 s on warm caches; the smoke's CSP assertion included). Packaged CSP proof: a release shell built with `--features test-bridge` (34 s) launched against a fresh app-data with the test-bridge environment reached `lifecycle` `ready` — the bundle executed and the IPC answered under the policy served on `http://tauri.localhost` — reported `runtime.paths.exportsDir` (present at engine start), published commissioning with the override, showed Lighting, Audio and Planning, wrote `exports/diagnostics-2026-09-10T18-16-19-854Z.json`, kept its heartbeat advancing (23 → 27 over one second) and reported `cspViolations` `[]`; the proof runtime held no scenes, so the `data:` thumbnails themselves were not drawn — the policy allows `data:` and the smoke asserts it stays. Then the production `tauri:build` (18 s) and the 12 s no-test-bridge launch of that exe. `tauri:setup-support:qualify` passed all four steps in 42 s with the pre-built debug shell: `shell-stays-responsive-during-stalled-request` (`bridgeIp` 203.0.113.113 — the first candidate stalled — `probeMs` 1933, `heartbeats` 9, `maxGapMs` 307), `diagnostics-export-defaults-to-app-data-exports` (`diagnostics-2026-09-10T18-18-14-224Z.json`) and the nine S1–S3 checks unchanged; run once more against the previous `main.rs` (the sync commands, byte-for-byte from `011fe44`) to prove the guard, the lane failed at the new check — "The shell's status file stopped updating for 1558 ms while a 1995 ms lighting probe was in flight" — and the new file was restored byte-identical and rebuilt before anything else ran. `npm run dev:check` green end to end. TotalMix FX and Companion untouched — Companion's LCD poll reached the proof engines' bridge on 38201 and was refused with 401 as S2 intends (Appendix B item 1 stays open); nothing listened on 4173 between lanes; the lane's temp runtime pointed its sACN target at the unrouted address for the two seconds between the two probes and at loopback afterwards, with no fixtures to render.
Baselines: no operator-visible layout change — the Diagnostics keys keep their labels and slots and only their target folder changed; the recovery display's reference list is data-driven and gains an `exportsDir` row on a live engine, but no fixture carries the key, so no board, ratchet or baseline moves; the visual lanes were not run.
Operator hands: none.
CI (result, 2026-09-10): run 34514042594 (https://github.com/Fikarn/sse-exed-studio-control/actions/runs/34514042594), event `push`, sha `f3db990`, created within five seconds of the push; ten and a half minutes end to end on the first attempt. Green on the first attempt: `lint` (0.4 min), `frontend-typecheck` (0.5), `frontend-test` (0.6 — the two new transport cases among them), `format-protocol` (1.0), `tauri-foundation` (1.9 — `tauri:smoke` with `verifyContentSecurityPolicy` on the Linux build), and `qualification` (7.1 — the setup-support lane's four steps under xvfb with the two new checks: `203.0.113.113` stalled on the runner too, the status file kept updating through the stalled probe and the default diagnostics export landed in `exports/`; the workspace lane as in S1–S3). Red on the first attempt, not expected: `rust` (3.1) — 319 passed / 1 failed / 1 ignored in the engine suite: `audio::tests_console_link::console_pull_that_never_goes_quiet_is_incomplete` (audit-remediation Slice 3; a fake TotalMix dump must still be streaming when a 500 ms pull times out, judged by a 150 ms quiet window) reached its `expect_err`, so the dump went quiet for the window on a runner where the whole engine suite took 28.65 s instead of the 8 s of the re-run; S4 touched no audio code, and the shell crate's 16 tests, `contract.rs` and `end_to_end.rs` passed on both attempts. The failed jobs were re-run on the same sha (`gh run rerun --failed`, attempt 2): `rust` green in 2.4 min (rustfmt, clippy, check, the 344 tests with the new shell cases on Linux — engine suite 8.14 s — and `native:acceptance`). Red, expected: `frontend-e2e` — attempt 1: 94 failed / 321 passed in 9.0 min, the same 52 `visual-review` + 39 `storybook` linux snapshot failures as S0–S3 (73 stale + 18 missing), the same `tests/audio-arm-countdown.spec.ts:45` dwell case and `tests/audio.spec.ts:1054` 48 V arm-then-apply case (S13 quarantine list), plus one runner-timing case not seen before, `tests/audio.spec.ts:702` (the simulated-metering movement mark; a fixture-mode spec S4 does not touch); attempt 2: 93 failed / 322 passed in 8.6 min — the 91 snapshot cases and the two listed cases only, `audio.spec.ts:702` green again (noted under Slice 13 as a further quarantine candidate, together with the Rust timing case). S4 changed no rendered front-end source under the fixture transport — the Diagnostics keys keep their labels and no board renders the export path — so no new visual diff exists and the linux refresh stays Appendix B item 6.

## Slice 5 — Engine supervision + single instance

Status: landed (operator verification pending) 2026-09-11 (commit `f1dc897` `Readiness S5: engine exit watcher, automatic restart, single instance` plus the lane follow-up `c4d849f` `Readiness S5 (lane): the second-instance refusal waits for the Linux shell's start-up`, pushed; both CI runs (34572635978 and 34574043349) are recorded under "CI (result)" below, the second by the follow-up commit `Readiness S5 (ledger): CI run on the branch recorded`, as in S0–S4; every S5 lane and `npm run dev:check` green on the workstation; the hardware step is Appendix B item 2, which only the operator can walk). Closes F09 F19. Depends on S4.

Scope (as landed): `T/engine.rs` — `EngineBridge.process` is an `Arc<Mutex<Option<EngineProcess>>>` and the bridge counts launches (`generations`); `EngineProcess` carries `generation`, `pid`, `expected_exit` and an `EventSink` (`Arc<dyn Fn(Value)>` — production wraps `app.emit("engine://event")`, the tests a channel), and its stdin is an `Option` so a stop can close the pipe while the process stays in the slot; `launch(command, binary_path, sink)` does the spawn (`start` resolves the binary and the directories, then calls it) and starts `spawn_exit_watcher(process, pending, generation)`: a named thread that sleeps `ENGINE_EXIT_WATCH_INTERVAL` (250 ms), locks the slot only for `child.try_wait()`, returns silently when the slot is empty or holds a newer launch, and on an exit takes the process out and calls `report_engine_exit` — which reaps the child, drains `pending` answering every waiter `error_response(id, "ENGINE_EXITED", "The hardware link stopped (exit status N) before it answered this request.")`, and emits `{type:"event",event:"engine.exited",payload:{status,graceful,generation,pid}}`; `stop()` = `stop_with_grace(ENGINE_STOP_GRACE)`: under the lock it sets `expected_exit` and drops stdin, then polls `try_wait` every 25 ms under short locks, kills after the grace, and whichever of it and the watcher takes the process out reports it — an exit is reported exactly once, and a confirmed close or a restart reports `graceful: true`; `write_request` answers "Engine is stopping" once stdin is closed; `EngineBootstrapSummary` gains `pid` and `generation` (`engine_start` and `engine_summary` answer them). `T/main.rs` — `tauri_plugin_single_instance::init(|app, _argv, _cwd| focus_main_window(app))` is the first builder call, before `manage` and `setup`; `focus_main_window` unminimises, shows and focuses the main window. `native/tauri-shell/Cargo.toml` — `tauri-plugin-single-instance = "2.4.4"` (crates.io was reachable; `native/Cargo.lock` grew by its tree — zbus for Linux, windows-sys for Windows). Protocol — `native/protocol/v1.contract.json` `events += "engine.exited"`, `EVENT_ENGINE_EXITED` in `native/protocol/rust/src/lib.rs` and `EVENT_NAMES`; `npm run protocol:generate` regenerated `native/protocol/generated/v1.schema.json` and `EC/generated/protocol.ts` (the ts-rs snapshot bindings are unchanged); `native/protocol/v1.md` documents the event and the codes `ENGINE_EXITED` / `ENGINE_ALREADY_RUNNING`. `E/bootstrap.rs` — `STARTUP_CODE_ENGINE_ALREADY_RUNNING`, `INSTANCE_LOCK_FILE_NAME` (`engine.lock`), `acquire_instance_lock(app_data_dir) -> Result<File, …>` (`OpenOptions` read + write + create, `File::try_lock`; `WouldBlock` → `StartupFailure { ENGINE_ALREADY_RUNNING, "Studio Control is already open on this workstation: another copy holds <path>. Close the other copy, then start again." }`; a lock error is a plain bootstrap failure, never a silent pass); `bootstrap_runtime_from_paths` takes the lock right after the directories exist and before `initialize_database`, logs `Instance lock refused (ENGINE_ALREADY_RUNNING): …` on refusal, and keeps the handle in `static INSTANCE_LOCK: Mutex<Option<(PathBuf, File)>>` for the life of the process (`release_instance_lock_for` drops an earlier hold on the same directory so the engine's own tests can bootstrap one directory twice; a real engine bootstraps once); `E/main.rs` needed no change — the failure flows through `startup_failure_code` into `engine.startupFailed {stage:"bootstrap", code:"ENGINE_ALREADY_RUNNING"}` and the engine exits non-zero. `EC/types.ts` — `EngineLaunchInfo {generation, pid}`, `BackgroundFailure {at, context, message}`, `EngineTransport.initialize?(): Promise<void | EngineLaunchInfo>`, `ShellState.backgroundFailures`, `ShellStore.reportBackgroundFailure(error, context?)`; `EC/index.ts` exports the two types. `EC/transports/tauriTransport.ts` — `initialize()` resolves with `{generation, pid}` from the `engine_start` summary (the fixture transport is untouched and resolves with nothing). `EC/store/createShellStore.ts` — `engineGeneration` per bootstrap from the launch info; `engine.exited` is handled before the refresh path and ignored when `graceful`, when its `generation` differs from the current one, or when the engine already reported a start-up failure for this bootstrap (the exit that follows `ENGINE_ALREADY_RUNNING` or `STORAGE_CORRUPT` says nothing new and must not restart an engine that would only refuse again); otherwise `lifecycle: "failed"`, `recovery: "recovery"`, `startupFailure {code:"ENGINE_EXITED", stage:"runtime", message}` — "The hardware link stopped unexpectedly (exit status N)." followed by the restart plan — the ready gate is rejected and the meter frame cleared; `scheduleAutomaticRestart`: at most `AUTOMATIC_RESTART_LIMIT` (3) automatic restarts per `AUTOMATIC_RESTART_WINDOW_MS` (5 min) with `AUTOMATIC_RESTART_BACKOFF_MS` 1 / 2 / 4 s ("Studio Control restarts it on its own in N s (attempt K of 3)."), then "It stopped 3 times within five minutes, so it is not restarted again on its own; use Retry startup once the desk and the rig are ready."; `restartEngine()` (shared by `restart()` and the timer) cancels a pending automatic restart, as do `dispose()` and every bootstrap; `recordBackgroundFailure` keeps the last `BACKGROUND_FAILURE_LIMIT` (20) `{at, context, message}` in `state.backgroundFailures`, which survives a restart; the six `void refreshDomain(..)` / `void refreshAudioSnapshot(..)` sites carry `.catch(inBackground("<context>"))`. `FA/main.tsx` — creates the environment, forwards `window` `error` and `unhandledrejection` to `store.reportBackgroundFailure`, and passes the environment to `OperatorShell` (`FA/app/OperatorShell.tsx` takes an optional `environment` prop and creates its own otherwise, as the stories do). `FA/app/startup/startupHelpers.ts` — `getFailureTitle`: "The hardware link stopped" / "Studio Control is already open"; `formatFailureCode`: "Hardware link stopped" / "Already open"; `formatFailureStage("runtime")` → "running". `FA/app/tauriShellTestBridge.ts` — `testBridge.enginePid` / `engineGeneration` from the summary; the diagnostics report gains `backgroundFailures`. `scripts/tauri-setup-support-qualification.mjs` — steps 5 and 6: `engine-crash` (a fresh runtime; `taskkill /PID <enginePid> /F` on win32, `SIGKILL` elsewhere; `startupFailure.code === "ENGINE_EXITED"` with stage `runtime` and the restart sentence within `ENGINE_CRASH_DETECT_MS` = 2 s; then `lifecycle === "ready"` with a new `enginePid` and `engineGeneration + 1`; evidence `engine-crash-reaches-recovery-and-restarts` with `detectMs`, `restartMs` and both pids) and `second-instance` on the same running shell (the debug shell binary `native/target/debug/sse-exed-tauri-shell[.exe]` launched directly with the same environment and its own status file; either it exits with code 0 within `SECOND_INSTANCE_EXIT_MS` = 5 s — `refusedBy: "shell-single-instance"`, required on win32 and darwin — or, on linux only, it stays up and its status reads `ENGINE_ALREADY_RUNNING` — `refusedBy: "engine-lock"`; the first shell must still be ready with the same engine and a moving heartbeat; evidence `second-instance-is-refused`). Docs: `native/tauri-shell/README.md` (a supervision paragraph), `native/protocol/v1.md`, `docs/OPERATIONS.md` (Restart / recovery bullets; "The app fails before the dashboard" step 4), `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md` (the lane's coverage), a CHANGELOG Added bullet.
**Rescope:** three deviations, none silent. (1) The exit is reported by whichever of the watcher and `stop()` takes the process out of the slot, not by the watcher alone: `stop()` has to close stdin, wait and kill anyway, and a watcher-only report would have added up to one poll interval to every close and restart and needed a fallback for a slot with no watcher; `expected_exit` still goes up before stdin closes, exactly as designed, so the watcher reports `graceful: true` when it wins the race. The audit Slice 11 helper `wait_for_exit` and its test went with it (Tests changed). (2) The store learns the shell's generation from `initialize()` resolving with the `engine_start` summary — the design's first option; the fixture transport is untouched and resolves with nothing, and a store without a generation still handles `engine.exited` (only a known, different generation is ignored). (3) The `second-instance` scenario accepts two refusals — the plugin's (the second shell exits within 5 s, required on Windows and macOS) and the engine lock's (the second shell reaches the recovery surface as `ENGINE_ALREADY_RUNNING`, accepted on Linux only) — because `tauri-plugin-single-instance` 2.4.4 on Linux registers a D-Bus name and, when the session bus cannot be reached, ignores the error and runs unprotected (the `_ => {}` arm of its `platform_impl/linux.rs`); the CI qualification job runs under `xvfb-run` with no session bus of its own, so the engine lock is the guard there — the guard the design named for the double use of the database and the light outputs. The evidence records which refusal happened. The plugin fetch succeeded (crates.io reachable), so the design's lock-file-only rescope was not needed. Files the design did not list: `EC/types.ts`, `EC/index.ts`, `EC/transports/tauriTransport.ts` (+ test), `FA/app/OperatorShell.tsx`, `FA/app/tauriShellTestBridge.ts`, `native/rust-engine/tests/end_to_end.rs`, `native/Cargo.lock`, the docs. (4) After the first CI run: the 5 s bound on the second shell's refusal holds on Windows and macOS only. On the Linux runner the second copy was refused by the plugin — it exited with code 0 — but about 30 s after launch, because a Tauri shell on Linux goes through GTK's start-up before any plugin runs, and under xvfb that start-up waits ~30 s on the AT-SPI bus lookup (the first shell of every lane step shows the same delay); the 5 s bound sent the lane into its engine-lock branch, which then reported the exit as "exited early". The lane follow-up commit makes the Linux bound the lane's wait timeout (`SSE_TAURI_QUALIFICATION_TIMEOUT_MS`, 180 s on CI, 40 s by default) and polls for whichever refusal comes first — the plugin's clean exit or `ENGINE_ALREADY_RUNNING` in the second shell's status file — recording which in the evidence; the Windows and macOS bound stays 5 s, and on those platforms an engine-lock refusal fails the lane. `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md` says so.
Gates before: nothing watched the engine process — a killed engine left every `engine_request` waiting for its ten-second timeout, `engine_summary` reporting `running: true` for a process that was gone, and the front-end painting the dashboard; `engine_start` reported no `pid` for a lane to end; no test or lane started two engines against one directory, and nothing stopped a second shell; the store's background refreshes were `void` promises whose rejections vanished; `initialize()` discarded the `engine_start` result.
Tests added: `engine::tests::exit_watcher_fails_pending_and_emits_event` (a short-lived process; the waiter gets `ENGINE_EXITED`, the event carries status 0 / graceful false / generation 1 / the pid, the slot and the pending map are empty afterwards, the report is single), `engine::tests::stop_reports_a_graceful_exit_and_kills_a_lingering_engine` (a `sort` that exits on stdin EOF is reported graceful well inside a 10 s grace; a `ping` that ignores stdin is killed after a 200 ms grace and reported once); `bootstrap::tests::second_lock_holder_is_refused`, `bootstrap::tests::bootstrap_refuses_a_held_app_data_dir` (refused before the database exists, `ERROR … ENGINE_ALREADY_RUNNING` in the log); `end_to_end::second_engine_on_the_same_app_data_dir_is_refused` (a second engine on the first's directory emits `engine.startupFailed` `ENGINE_ALREADY_RUNNING` / `bootstrap` and exits non-zero; after the first shuts down a third starts on the same directory); `createShellStore.test.ts` "engine.exited → recovery + bounded restart" (fake timers: 1 / 2 / 4 s, four launches, the fourth stop stays failed for a further 60 s, `restart()` by hand still works), "stale generation ignored" (generation 0, a graceful exit and an exit after `ENGINE_ALREADY_RUNNING` change nothing and start nothing), "keeps the last twenty background failures without leaving ready"; `tauriTransport.test.ts` "resolves initialize with the launch the shell reported"; `startupHelpers.test.ts` "formats ENGINE_EXITED", "formats ENGINE_ALREADY_RUNNING"; lane checks `engine-crash-reaches-recovery-and-restarts` and `second-instance-is-refused`; `tests/contract.rs` green with the new event. Tests changed (old → new → why): `engine::tests::wait_for_exit_sees_a_prompt_exit_and_gives_up_on_a_lingering_child` (audit Slice 11) → removed with `wait_for_exit`, its two behaviours asserted through `stop_with_grace` in the new stop test → the stop no longer waits on the child through that helper; `tauriTransport.test.ts`'s `invoke` mock → answers `engine_start` with a summary (`pid` 4242, `generation` 3) → the new case reads it; the S3 and S4 cases are unchanged.
Lanes: `npm run protocol:generate && npm run protocol:check`, `npm run native:test`, `npm run frontend:test`, `npm run tauri:setup-support:qualify`. Done when a killed engine reaches recovery within 2 s and restarts on its own, a second engine exits `ENGINE_ALREADY_RUNNING`, and a second shell focuses the first — all three met (recovery 219 ms after the kill and the engine back in 1,313 ms with a new pid; `second_engine_on_the_same_app_data_dir_is_refused` end to end, plus the lane's `engine.lock` path for Linux; the second shell handed over and exited with code 0 in 113 ms while the first stayed ready).

## Slice 6 — OSC ingress

Status: landed (operator verification pending) 2026-09-11 (commit `bee067b` `Readiness S6: loopback bind for the TotalMix meter ports, console-only sources, dropped-datagram log`, pushed; CI run 34593996173 recorded under "CI (result)" below by the follow-up commit `Readiness S6 (ledger): CI run on the branch recorded`, as in S0–S5; every S6 lane and `npm run dev:check` green on the workstation; the hardware step is Appendix B item 3, which only the operator can walk). Closes F05. Depends on S0.

Scope (as landed): `E/rme_totalmix_osc.rs` — `ReceivePolicy { console: IpAddr, bind_host: IpAddr }` with `for_console(console, bind_override)`: the bind host is the override when `SSE_OSC_BIND_HOST` names an IP address, else the loopback address of the console's family when the console address is loopback and the unspecified address (`0.0.0.0` / `::`) otherwise; `resolve_console_address(send_host)` turns the commissioned TotalMix address into that console address (`localhost` → `127.0.0.1`, a literal address → itself, anything else through the resolver the send path already uses; an empty or unresolvable host → `None`); `parse_bind_override(raw)` (unset or empty → no override, an IP address → the override, anything else refused with the sentence the thread logs); `accept_source(source: SocketAddr, expected: IpAddr) -> bool` (the console's own address only; an IPv4 console seen through an IPv4-mapped IPv6 address counts as itself); `DroppedSourceLog` (a per-source `Instant` map, `DROPPED_SOURCE_LOG_INTERVAL` 60 s, at most `MAX_TRACKED_DROP_SOURCES` 256 addresses remembered, one WARN line through `append_log` — "RME TotalMix OSC datagram from <source> dropped on receive port <port>: only the TotalMix address <console> is read; further drops from this source are noted once a minute" — the call site S8 moves to `log_event`); `bind_receive_socket(bind_host, port)` (the one non-blocking bind every slot shares) and `local_port_of`; `BoundRmeSlot` and `GlobalOscSlot` carry `console: IpAddr`; `bind_slots(policy, send_port, receive_port)` and `bind_global_slot(policy, …)` bind the policy's host (a classic bind failure still goes to stderr, now naming the bind host; the former separate non-blocking failure message is folded into it); `read_available_packets(…, drops)` and `read_global_packets(…, drops)` apply `accept_source` to every `recv_from` result before decoding — a foreign datagram is recorded and skipped, and never counts as Global slot activity (`last_rx_at`); `spawn_rme_totalmix_audio_metering(sender, db_path, log_file_path)` reads the override once at thread start (a malformed value is logged WARN and the rule applies), keeps one `DroppedSourceLog`, and at every (send host, ports) rebind resolves the console address — an unresolvable TotalMix address binds nothing and logs a WARN once per key instead of listening on every interface for anyone; `bind_test_global_slot` binds loopback with a loopback console, and the hardware lane's `bind_live_global_slot_for_test` follows the engine's rule (console `127.0.0.1`, the override honoured). `E/main.rs` passes `log_file_path` to the metering thread. `E/commissioning.rs::probe_audio_transport` is unchanged: its send socket stays `0.0.0.0:0`, and the persisted TotalMix address (`app.commissioning.audio.send_host`, `127.0.0.1` on the workstation) is the address the metering thread reads. The module is split by remote generation, as the file-health allowlist reason asked of the next OSC feature: `E/rme_totalmix_osc/classic_eq.rs` (the classic page-2 EQ command path — `send_totalmix_eq_update` and the `totalmix_eq_*` scales; 153 lines), `E/rme_totalmix_osc/global_commands.rs` (the Global OSC channel and output-mix commands, `TotalMixSendReport` and the surface ↔ hardware maps; 236 lines), `E/rme_totalmix_osc/tests.rs` (the module's tests; 1,454 lines), the parent at 1,687 lines re-exporting every public name so no caller changed; `send_osc_messages`, the cfg(test) console-port guard, `validated_command_port` and both metering paths stay in the parent because the two generations share the meter state and the send socket. `scripts/file-health.mjs` — the `rme_totalmix_osc.rs` allowlist entry is gone; the module is held at 2,000 lines like any other file. Docs: `docs/OPERATIONS.md` (the metering checklist names the IP on every remote, and a paragraph on the loopback bind, the dropped-datagram log line and the `netstat` check), `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md` (the bind rule, `SSE_OSC_BIND_HOST`, the ephemeral-port discipline of the tests, the module layout; the hardware lane now binds `127.0.0.1:9004`), a CHANGELOG Changed bullet.
**Rescope:** none in what the slice does — the design's bind rule, `accept_source`, the override and the once-a-minute WARN landed as written, and the title stands. Two things the design did not list, recorded here rather than done silently: (1) the split of `rme_totalmix_osc.rs` (2,892 lines, allowlisted with the reason "split by remote generation when the next OSC feature touches it") — the tests moved out first, and with the ingress code the parent still stood at 2,038 lines, over the guard, so the two command paths moved to `classic_eq.rs` and `global_commands.rs` as pure moves (visibility only: `pub(super)` on the two helpers the tests name, `pub use` / `pub(crate) use` re-exports in the parent); the metering paths of both generations stay together, which is what remains of F26 on this module for S14 to judge — the allowlist entry itself is gone, so `file:health` now holds the module at the cap. (2) The metering thread takes the engine log path as a new parameter so the WARN goes through `append_log` and not an eighth `eprintln!` (the module has six now, one of them the cfg(test) guard's; S8 replaces them).
Gates before: the four receive sockets bound `0.0.0.0` and both readers discarded `recv_from`'s source (`_source`), so any host that could reach the workstation's UDP ports 9001–9004 could feed the meters and — through the Global slot — the console link, which applies what "the desk" reports to the stored audio state; no test bound the real slot set or checked a source address; the hardware-lane helper bound `0.0.0.0` too.
Tests added (`E/rme_totalmix_osc/tests.rs`): `accept_source_rejects_foreign_ip` (loopback, LAN and IPv6 cases, the IPv4-mapped form), `receive_policy_binds_loopback_for_a_loopback_console_and_every_interface_otherwise` (the rule for both families, the override, `resolve_console_address` for `localhost` / a literal / blanks, `parse_bind_override` accepting an address and refusing a name with the variable named), `receive_sockets_bind_loopback_for_loopback_console` (the three classic slots and the Global slot bound through `bind_slots` / `bind_global_slot` on a free four-port base ≥ 7011 taken from a throwaway ephemeral socket — every socket on `127.0.0.1` at base..base+3, send ports 7011–7014 stored and never sent to), `foreign_datagrams_are_dropped_and_logged_once_per_minute` (a Global slot commissioned for a LAN console receives three loopback datagrams: nothing reaches the meter state, `last_rx_at` stays unset, exactly one WARN line naming the source, the receive port and the console; the same datagram with the console set to loopback is applied and logs nothing), `classic_slots_read_the_console_address_only` (the same for `read_available_packets` with a `/1/level1Left` message), `dropped_source_log_notes_a_source_once_a_minute` (per address not per port, a second source, the minute boundary, the no-log-path limiter, the 256-source cap). Tests changed (old → new → why): `send_slot_keepalives_pins_each_slot_to_its_bus_and_bank_start`, `service_console_link_reads_back_over_the_global_slot_and_confirms`, `refresh_global_slot_sends_sendall_and_sendstate_to_the_slot_port` → the slot literals carry `console: LOOPBACK` and the reader call passes a `DroppedSourceLog` → the structs gained the field and the reader the parameter; no assertion changed. The 27 existing module tests moved to `rme_totalmix_osc/tests.rs` unchanged (two of them import the classic helpers from `classic_eq`).
Lanes: `npm run native:test`, `npm run rust:clippy`, then `npm run tauri:setup-support:qualify` and `npm run tauri:workspaces:qualify` (the only lane that receives real TotalMix metering on this PC). Done when `netstat -an | findstr 900` shows `127.0.0.1:9001-9004` while an engine with TotalMix metering runs and a foreign datagram is logged and dropped — both met (below).
Validation (workstation, 2026-09-11): before any change, TotalMix FX 2.1 (pid 30764) held `0.0.0.0:7001` and `0.0.0.0:7004` and was sending from connected UDP sockets to `127.0.0.1:9001` and `127.0.0.1:9004`, and the operator database carried `app.commissioning.audio.send_host = 127.0.0.1` with send port 7001 and receive port 9001 — so the remote IP TotalMix sends to is loopback and the loopback bind is the right one for this workstation (design trap 3, settled from the live sockets; TotalMix's preferences file does not record the remote IP). Baseline before editing: `native:test` 348 passed / 1 ignored, `rust:clippy` clean. After: `cargo fmt` applied; `rust:clippy` clean under `-D warnings` (one `manual_range_contains` in the new test helper fixed on the way); `native:test` 354 passed / 1 ignored (tauri-shell 17, engine 328 + 1 ignored hardware-bound — was 322 — `contract.rs` 4, `end_to_end.rs` 5); the module alone 33 tests green (`cargo test -p studio-control-engine --bin studio-control-engine -- rme_totalmix_osc::`); `file:health` passed with the module's allowlist entry removed (`rme_totalmix_osc.rs` 1,687 lines, `classic_eq.rs` 153, `global_commands.rs` 236, `tests.rs` 1,454, `main.rs` 567). The debug test-bridge shell was pre-built (`cargo build --features test-bridge`), the production app was closed (nothing on 38201, 4173 or 9001–9004; port 80 free for the lighting probe server). `tauri:setup-support:qualify` passed all six steps in 53 s (evidence `summary.json`, `githubSha` eaa3cc5): the thirteen checks as in S5 — `shell-stays-responsive-during-stalled-request` `probeMs` 1869 / `heartbeats` 8 / `maxGapMs` 254, `corrupt-db-reaches-recovery-surface`, `engine-crash-reaches-recovery-and-restarts` `detectMs` 333 / `restartMs` 1424 (pid 156984 → 207204), `second-instance-is-refused` `refusedBy` `shell-single-instance` after `refusalMs` 101. `tauri:workspaces:qualify` passed both steps in 22 s against the live TotalMix: `commissioning-probes-and-publish-complete` with `overrideProbes: false` — the audio probe passed on real meter traffic (`audioSnapshot.verified`), which can only have reached the lane's engine through its loopback-bound slots: the lane commissions a throwaway receive port (64053 this run) and send port 64054 on `127.0.0.1`, so nothing the lane sends reaches TotalMix's 7001–7010 and nothing answers on 64053, and the liveness the probe saw came from TotalMix's packets to `127.0.0.1:9001` / `:9004`, the engine's default slots bound by the new rule at start — then `audio-live-mutations-round-trip`, `lighting-live-mutations-round-trip`, `planning-live-mutations-round-trip` and `restart-preserves-migrated-workspace-state`. A `netstat -an` sampler beside the workspace lane recorded the lane engine holding `UDP 127.0.0.1:9001`, `127.0.0.1:9002`, `127.0.0.1:9003` and `127.0.0.1:9004` (about four seconds into the lane, before its commissioning step rebound the slots to the throwaway ports; no `0.0.0.0:900x` in any sample) while TotalMix's sockets toward `127.0.0.1:9001` and `:9004` stayed up in every sample — the done criterion's `netstat` line. The foreign-datagram criterion is met by the two dropped-and-logged tests (a datagram from an address other than the console's is discarded, the meter state untouched, one WARN line in the log); with the loopback bind a LAN-addressed datagram never reaches the engine at all, so on the workstation the source check is the second layer, for the wildcard and override cases. `npm run dev:check` green end to end (82 s on warm caches: format, lint, `scripts:test`, `file:health`, rustfmt, clippy, `protocol:check` with the contract unchanged, `frontend:typecheck`, `frontend:test` — app 64, design-system 175, engine-client 13, tokens 15 — `native:check`, `native:test`). TotalMix FX and Companion untouched — nothing wrote to 7001–7010 beyond the lane engines' own once-a-second pulls with their default settings (the bus and bank pins, `/sendall` + `/sendstate`), which are the pulls the real app makes; the lane runtimes were temp directories, deleted afterwards.
