Scope (as landed): `E/support.rs` (1,469 lines; its tests moved to `E/support/tests.rs`, 1,019, before the new code took the file over 2,000 — the S3/S6 precedent) — `SUPPORT_BACKUP_FORMAT_VERSION` 4: the archive gains `settings`, every key under `RESTORE_KEY_PREFIXES` (`shell.` and `app.control_surface.`) verbatim, so the scene thumbnails, the talent marks, the Setup section, the current lighting section, the window mode and the deck's bank, dial mode, selected scene and last event travel with an export and come back with a restore; `clear_support_settings` deletes those prefixes as a whole and `write_support_settings` writes the structured shell fields formats 2 and 3 exported but never restored (window mode, Setup section, section id, thumbnails, marks) and then the verbatim map, which wins; a `formatVersion` above the reader's is refused before anything is written (`SupportCommandError::UnsupportedVersion` → `SUPPORT_RESTORE_UNSUPPORTED_VERSION`); `SupportBackupKind { Archive, Database }` (serialised `archive` / `database`) on every `SupportFileEntry` and `list_backup_files` (replacing `list_json_files`) lists `.json` and `.sqlite3` files and nothing else; `parse_support_restore_request(params, backups_dir)` canonicalises the path and the backups directory (links followed) and refuses anything outside it, a non-file, or an unknown extension — the one root for restore and verify (F29); `verify_support_backup(request) -> SupportBackupVerification { ok, kind, path, formatVersion | schemaVersion, detail }`, never an error: `inspect_archive` parses a JSON file (a support archive of a readable format, or a legacy `db.json` export), `inspect_database_backup(path) -> DatabaseBackupFacts` opens a database backup read-only, runs `PRAGMA integrity_check`, reads `schema_migrations` and the planning and settings counts, and refuses a schema above `STORAGE_SCHEMA_VERSION`; `restore_support_backup` splits into `restore_archive_backup` (parse and check first, then the rollback archive `native-pre-restore-<ts>.json`, then the one transaction; a legacy `db.json` placed in the folder still goes through `import_legacy_db`; refused in recovery mode with the way out) and `restore_database_backup` (the same check as verify, a `PreRestore` database backup of the live file when `storage_ready`, then `stage_database_restore` copies the backup to `<app-data>/restore-pending.sqlite3` and flushes it; the summary carries `requiresRestart: true`, `sourceFormat: "database-backup"`, the counts read from the backup and `rollbackBackupPath: Option` — `None` only from recovery mode); `prune_pre_restore_archives_in` keeps the newest five rollback archives after every archive restore, `prune_exports(dir, now)` removes diagnostics exports older than `EXPORTS_MAX_AGE` (thirty days); `read_support_snapshot` counts both kinds in its summary and its `restoreSummary` no longer says "engine" or "db.json". `E/storage_backups.rs` — `SnapshotReason::Replaced` (`db-<ts>-replaced.sqlite3`, retention 5, in `ALL` so `prune_snapshots` owns it), `reserve_snapshot_path` `pub(crate)` for the bootstrap, `newest_snapshot` skips `replaced` files (never verified, so the recovery sentence never points at one). `E/bootstrap.rs` (1,189) — `apply_pending_restore(paths)` runs after `hold_instance_lock` and before `initialize_database`: the pending file is checked again with `inspect_database_backup` and the schema bound (a file that fails is removed with a WARN and the live database kept), the live file is renamed into the backups directory as `replaced`, its `-wal` / `-shm` are removed, the pending file takes the live name, one INFO line; `prune_exports` runs after the storage init; `recovery_runtime_context(paths)` builds the `RuntimeContext` a recovery-mode engine runs with (`storage_ready` false, no bridge, no token). `E/app.rs` (1,932) — `RECOVERY_METHODS` (`engine.ping`, `support.snapshot`, `support.backup.verify`, `support.backup.restore`) and the guard at the top of `handle_request` that answers everything else `ENGINE_NOT_READY` while `storage_ready` is false; `EngineApp::recovery(paths)`; `support_error_response` shared by the three support arms; the `support.backup.verify` arm; the restore arm emits `support.changed` only (`backup-restore-staged`) when `requires_restart` and the full set otherwise. `E/main.rs` (592) — the request loop is `serve_requests`; on a `STORAGE_CORRUPT` or `STORAGE_MIGRATION_FAILED` bootstrap failure the engine emits `engine.startupFailed` exactly as before and then serves in recovery mode until stdin closes (every other failure still exits). `E/audio/tests_console_link.rs` — the confidence-writer allowlist names `support/tests.rs` instead of `support.rs`. Protocol: `support.backup.verify` in `v1.contract.json`, the generated schema and `protocol.ts`; `v1.md` documents verify, the restore extensions (`kind`, `requiresRestart`, the nullable rollback path, format 4, the backups-folder rule, `SUPPORT_RESTORE_UNSUPPORTED_VERSION`) and recovery mode. Front-end: `EC/types.ts` (`ShellStore.verifySupportBackup`); `EC/store/createShellStore.ts` (1,397) — `restoreSupportBackup` awaits `restartEngine()` when the answer carries `requiresRestart` (the same path as Retry startup; a failure to come back goes to `backgroundFailures`), `verifySupportBackup` is a plain request, and after a start-up failure whose code is in `RECOVERY_MODE_CODES` the store fetches `support.snapshot` so the recovery surface lists the backups folder; `EC/transports/fixtureTransport.ts` (6,711, allowlisted) — `runtime.paths.exportsDir` (the S5 hook), `kind` on the fixture entries, `findFixtureBackup` with the engine's two refusal sentences, a `support.backup.verify` simulation, `requiresRestart` for a `.sqlite3` path, `formatVersion` 4 and the new restore summary; `FA/app/shellData.ts` — `SupportBackupKind`, `describeBackupKind`, `kind` read from the entry or derived from the extension; `FA/app/setup/components/SupportPlate.tsx` (185) — `onVerifyBackup` and the `Verify latest` key (`support-verify-latest`, the same small key beside Export backup and Restore latest), "N backups" as the section detail; `FA/app/setup/SetupSupportPilot.tsx` (1,686) — `verifyBackup` → `performAction("verify-backup")` with the inline feedback "Backup checked: …" / "Backup failed its check: …", a `Verify path` key (`support-verify-path`) beside Restore path, the kind on every backup row and on the plate's Kind readout, the restart sentence after a database restore, and copy that says "backup archive or database backup from the backups folder" instead of "legacy db.json export"; `FA/app/setup/SetupRecoverySurface.tsx` (473) — `engineRequestsAvailable` is true only with no failure or a storage code (the two failures after which the hardware link is still there to answer), the kind on every row, the restart sentence, the folder rule in the hint, the placeholder and the empty state (the protocol-mismatch sentence kept); `FA/app/tauriShellTestBridge.ts` — `verifySupportBackup` command; `FA/vitest.config.ts` — the `__APP_VERSION__` define the pilot's component test needs. Lane: `scripts/tauri-setup-support-qualification.mjs` (1,115) — step 2 gains `database-backup-verifies-and-restores-after-restart` (seed two projects, the test bridge's `restart` — a graceful stop, which writes the `shutdown` database backup — the backup listed with `kind: database`, a third project, verify ok with `schemaVersion`, junk beside it verified `ok: false`, restore → `requiresRestart`, a `-pre-restore.sqlite3` rollback, the shell ready again at generation + 2 with the two projects, the pending file gone, one `-replaced.sqlite3`), step 4 gains `corrupt-db-restores-from-database-backup` (that backup seeded into the corrupt runtime's backups folder; the recovery surface lists it while `failed`, verify ok, restore stages without a rollback copy, the shell restarts into the two projects, and the junk file is kept byte for byte as the replaced backup). Docs: `docs/OPERATIONS.md` (the manual file swap under "The app fails before the dashboard" replaced by the recovery-surface restore; Data Safety names the `replaced` reason, format 4, verify, the folder rule, the caps), `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md` (the lane's new scenarios), a CHANGELOG Added bullet.
**Rescope:** three deviations, none silent, and the title stands because what landed is what it says. (1) Recovery mode — the design's `.sqlite3` restore assumed a running engine, but after `STORAGE_CORRUPT` / `STORAGE_MIGRATION_FAILED` `main.rs` returned an error and the process ended, so the recovery surface's Restore keys (which S3's sentence points the operator at, and which this slice was to make real instead of the manual file swap) could reach nothing. The engine now stays up in recovery mode after those two codes, answering only the four backup requests, and the store fetches the backup list; the manual swap is gone from the operations guide. (2) The F29 exemption "unless the path came from the test bridge" is dropped: the lane restores the engine's own files inside its temp backups folder, so the exemption had no user, and one root with no special case is the rule the engine, the fixture transport and the copy all state. The staged legacy import under `<app-data>/import/db.json` (S1) stays the first-start path and is not a second restore root — a legacy export is restored by placing it in the backups folder, which the placeholder and hints now say. (3) `SnapshotReason::Replaced`: keeping the moved-aside database next to the pre-restore copy needed a name `prune_snapshots` owns (retention 5) and `newest_snapshot` must skip (it was never verified; after a corrupt start it is the junk); the S3 retention values are untouched. Two things recorded rather than done: the fixture transport stays allowlisted — its three support arms lean on eleven module-private helpers of the state machine (`asArray`/`asRecord`/`countControls`/`countPlanningActivity`/`buildSeededPlanningSnapshot`/`updateFixtureCheck`/`synchronizeFixtureState`/`emit` and the rest), so moving the arms alone would first mean exporting those, which is the S14 (F26) restructuring; and `E/app.rs` stands at 1,932 lines, 68 under the cap, for S8 and S9 to mind.
Gates before: the archive carried `ShellSettingsSnapshot` in full, but the restore wrote back only the workspace, width, height and maximized flag, so the scene thumbnails, talent marks, Setup section, current lighting section and window mode were exported and never restored, and nothing under `app.control_surface.` beyond the selected light travelled at all — the round-trip test asserted planning, commissioning, lighting and audio, never the shell or the deck; `parse_support_restore_request` accepted any non-empty string and `restore_support_backup` read whatever file the engine could reach, writing the rollback archive before parsing it, so junk produced a rollback and an error; a `formatVersion` above the reader's was parsed on faith; `list_json_files` hid every `.sqlite3` backup S3 had been writing; no verify existed; the engine exited on a storage failure, so the recovery surface's restore keys (enabled for every code but `PROTOCOL_MISMATCH`) could reach nothing; no test named a path outside the backups directory, a newer format, a `.sqlite3` source or a pending restore.
Tests added: `support::tests` (`E/support/tests.rs`) — `restore_refuses_newer_format` (format 5 refused with `UnsupportedVersion`, no rollback archive, the data untouched, verify says the same), `restore_round_trips_all_prefixes` (ten keys under both prefixes exported, all changed and two added after the export, every one back verbatim and the added ones gone, the map equal to the archive's), `list_includes_sqlite3` (a `daily` database backup and an export listed newest first with their kinds, a stray `.txt` and a `-wal` sidecar not), `verify_flags_junk_file` (junk `.sqlite3` and `.json`, a stranger JSON, a real database backup with `schemaVersion`, a real export with `formatVersion`, a `.txt` refused at parse), `restore_rejects_path_outside_backups` (an archive copied outside the folder, a missing file, the folder itself, a blank path — all refused before anything is read), `database_restore_stages_pending_file_and_keeps_rollback` (the pending file byte-identical to the backup, the `pre-restore` copy a good database, the live database untouched, the log line; in recovery mode no rollback and an archive refused), `pre_restore_archives_are_capped_at_five`, `exports_older_than_thirty_days_are_pruned`; `bootstrap::tests` — `pending_restore_applied_after_snapshot` (the live file becomes `db-…-replaced.sqlite3` beside the `pre-restore` copy, the fake `-wal` / `-shm` go, the restored data reads back, a full `bootstrap_runtime_from_paths` then opens it), `pending_restore_that_fails_its_check_is_refused` (junk pending removed, live file byte-identical, WARN logged); `storage_backups::tests` — `newest_snapshot_skips_replaced_files` (and `prune_keeps_retention` covers the fifth reason through `ALL`); store tests (`createShellStore.test.ts`) — "restore with requiresRestart restarts the link once and spends no restart budget" (launch 2 through the restart path, the graceful exit ignored, an archive restore restarts nothing, the next real stop is attempt 1 of 3), "fetches the backup list after a storage failure at start" (`support.snapshot` once after `STORAGE_CORRUPT`, never after `ENGINE_ALREADY_RUNNING`); `SetupSupportPilot.test.tsx` (new) — "verify shows inline result" (the plate key → "Backup checked: Backup archive, format 4 …" in the feedback banner, tone ok), "verify shows the refusal for a file outside the backups folder" (tone error, the engine's sentence); lane checks `database-backup-verifies-and-restores-after-restart` and `corrupt-db-restores-from-database-backup`. Tests changed (old → new → why): `restore_support_backup_accepts_legacy_json` and `restore_support_backup_accepts_format_v2_archive_without_storage_version` (source file in the test root → inside the backups folder, through `parse_support_restore_request` → F29); `restore_support_backup_round_trips_native_archive` (`SupportRestoreRequest` gains `kind`; `!rollback_backup_path.is_empty()` → `is_some()` plus `!requires_restart` → the rollback path is optional for a recovery-mode database restore); `export_support_backup_writes_archive_and_lists_it` ("1 backup archives" → "1 backup archive", plus the `kind` → the summary counts both kinds and pluralises); `audio::tests_console_link::console_confidence_has_one_writer` (allowlist `support.rs` → `support/tests.rs` → the support tests moved to their own file); the five existing support tests moved otherwise unchanged; the store test double `supervisedTransport` gained `answer(method, value)` and records requests (no assertion changed); the lane's existing checks are unchanged.
Lanes: `npm run protocol:generate && npm run protocol:check`, `npm run native:test`, `npm run rust:clippy`, `npm run frontend:test`, `node scripts/check-operator-copy.mjs`, `npm run build --workspace frontend/app && npm run frontend:playwright:test`, `npm run tauri:setup-support:qualify`. Done when a restore brings back deck bindings, scene thumbnails and talent marks, verify answers `ok: false` on junk, and the archives are capped — all met (below).

## Slice 8 — Engine health + logging

Status: verified 2026-09-17 (commit `b00ecaf` `Readiness S8: engine health registry, rotating engine and shell logs, request log at DEBUG`, pushed; CI run 35201156819 recorded under "CI (result)" below by the follow-up commit `Readiness S8 (ledger): CI run on the branch recorded`, as in S0–S7; every S8 lane and `npm run dev:check` green on the workstation; no hardware exposure — the slice binds nothing new, writes to no console and changes no operator surface, so it ends as `verified` like S3 and S4). Closes F11 (engine half) F14 F16 F27. Depends on S3 S6.

Scope (as landed): `E/diagnostics.rs` (565 lines, was 80) — `LogLevel { Debug, Info, Warn, Error }` parsed from `SSE_ENGINE_LOG_LEVEL` (`LOG_LEVEL_ENV`; unset or empty → `INFO`, an unknown name → `INFO` plus one WARN line saying so); `LogWriter` keeps one `File` open, counts bytes, and rotates `engine.log` → `engine.log.1` … `engine.log.5` (`LOG_ROTATE_BYTES` 5 MiB, `LOG_ROTATE_KEEP` 5) by renaming with the handle dropped first, best effort — a rename a viewer blocks leaves the file in place and the next line tries again; `init_log(path, level)` installs it in a `OnceLock<Mutex<LogWriter>>` (first call wins); `log_event(level, message)` writes through it, dropping lines below the level, and falls back to stderr when there is no writer yet or the write failed; `append_log(path, level, message)` is routed through the writer when `path` is its file (rotation and the level filter apply) and opens and appends any other path per call as before, so every existing test that reads its own temp log still works without `init_log`; `request_log_line(level, method, id, elapsed, ok)` builds the F27 line (`method=… id=… ms=… ok=…`) only at `DEBUG`; `read_log_tail(path, 64 KiB, max_lines)` seeks to the last `LOG_TAIL_MAX_BYTES` of the current file, drops the partial first line and returns the last `max_lines` (the missing-file and empty-file sentences are unchanged; `read_log_excerpt` is gone). New `E/health.rs` (589 lines) — `SubsystemState { Ok, Attention, Warning, Error }` (serialised lower-case), `SubsystemStatus { state, detail, at }` (`at` Unix seconds), `HealthRegistry` (a `BTreeMap<&'static str, SubsystemStatus>` behind a process-wide `OnceLock<Mutex<…>>`) whose `report` returns whether the state changed (a first `ok` is not a change, a first `attention` is); `report` / `report_at` raise `app.changed { reason: "health" }` through the new `engine_events::emit_app_changed` on a change (a no-op before `main.rs` registers the sender, so the bootstrap's reports raise nothing and the first `health.snapshot` carries them); `effective_entries(entries, now)` turns a `backups` entry that is `ok` but older than `BACKUP_WARNING_AGE_SECS` (48 h) into `warning`; `derive_status(entries, storage_ready)` = `error` when `storage_ready` is false or any entry is `error`, else `attention` if any, else `warning` if any, else `ok`; `read_health_snapshot(runtime)` (moved here from `app.rs` with `format_health_summary`) keeps every field the shell reads, sets `status` from the registry (the never-reachable `"starting"` is gone), adds `checks.engine` = the effective entries keyed by subsystem, and reads the excerpt with `read_log_tail(…, 64 KiB, 12)`. Reporters: `control_surface_http::start_control_surface_bridge` → `bridge` `ok` (the serving sentence) or `attention` ("unavailable because the listener could not bind: …" — no fallback port is scanned); the metering thread in `rme_totalmix_osc.rs` → `osc` after every bind cycle (`bind_slots_reporting` returns the bound slots and the failed ports; `report_meter_port_health` says `ok` "Listening for TotalMix on N meter port(s) from <receive port> on <bind host>" when every classic slot and the Global OSC slot bound, `attention` naming each port that did not; the unresolvable-address branch is `attention`; switching TotalMix metering off reports `ok` "metering is off" once, through `bound_key.take()`); `lighting_sacn_output::run_output_loop` → `sacn` `ok` when the UDP socket is allocated, `attention` when it is not; `main.rs::write_database_backup` → `backups` `ok` with the copy's sentence or `warning` with the failure (daily and shutdown alike); `bootstrap_runtime_from_paths` → `storage` `ok` ("Integrity ok, schema vN") after `initialize_database`, and seeds `backups` `ok` at the modified time of `newest_snapshot(backups_dir)` so the 48 h rule holds across restarts; `apply_pending_restore` → `restore` `ok` with the S7 INFO sentence; `EngineApp::recovery` → `storage` `error`. `E/app.rs` (1,874 lines, was 1,932): `handle_request` times the request, runs the old body as `dispatch`, and appends the DEBUG line through `append_log` when `configured_log_level()` is `DEBUG` — the `INFO` "Handling request: …" line on every request is gone; `shutdown` logs the released talkback holds instead of printing them. `E/main.rs` (611): `mod health`; `init_log(&planned_paths.log_file_path, LogLevel::from_env())` right after `resolve_runtime_paths()` succeeds, before `validate_protocol_version` and `EngineApp::bootstrap`, so the protocol mismatch, the bootstrap failure and the recovery mode all log to `engine.log`; the output-writer failure and a malformed request log at ERROR / WARN. `E/audio/talkback.rs` (the three watchdog and shutdown lines → `log_event`), `E/rme_totalmix_osc.rs` (settings-read, bind, receive and console-link-flush failures → `log_event` WARN; a decode failure → DEBUG, since a malformed-datagram flood must not become a log flood; the `cfg(test)` console-port guard → `println!`), `E/audio/tests_console_link.rs` (two test prints → `println!`). `rg "eprintln!" native/rust-engine/src` = 2, both documented here: `main.rs` "Engine bootstrap failed" when `resolve_runtime_paths` fails (no log path exists yet) and the stderr fallback inside `diagnostics::log_event`. Shell: new `T/shell_log.rs` (177) — `ShellLog::open(logs_dir)` → `<logs>/shell.log`, `write_line(label, message)` with the same 5 MiB × 5 rotation; `T/engine.rs` (1,108) — `EngineBridge.shell_log` opened once per shell in `start()` (`shell_log_for`) and passed to `launch(…, Option<SharedShellLog>)`; `spawn_stderr_thread` writes every engine stderr line as `[ts] ENGINE-STDERR <line>` and echoes it only under `cfg(debug_assertions)`; `T/main.rs` `mod shell_log`. Front-end: no source change (D6) — `EC/machines/recoveryMachine.ts` is unchanged and now reachable; `recoveryMachine.test.ts` and `FA/app/setup/setupState.test.ts` pin the mappings. Lane: `scripts/tauri-setup-support-qualification.mjs` step 7/7 `bridge-port-taken-reads-as-health-attention` — a `net.createServer` holds an ephemeral loopback port, the shell is launched with `SSE_CONTROL_SURFACE_PORT` naming it, and the status file must show `healthSnapshot.status` `attention`, `checks.engine.bridge.state` `attention` with a "could not bind" detail, `appSnapshot.runtime.controlSurface.available` false on that port, `recovery` `degraded`, a string `recentLogExcerpt` ≤ 64 KiB and an `engine.log` in the lane's logs dir. Docs: `native/protocol/v1.md` (the `status` vocabulary, `checks.engine`, the bounded string excerpt, the `health` reason of `app.changed` — no contract method or event changed, `protocol:check` untouched), `docs/OPERATIONS.md` (Health status, Logs), `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md` (the writer, `SSE_ENGINE_LOG_LEVEL`, the two stderr sites, `shell.log`, the reporting rule), `CHANGELOG.md`.

**Rescope:** seven deviations, none silent, and the title stands because what landed is what it says. (1) The bridge reports from `E/control_surface_http.rs`, where S2 moved the listener, not from `E/control_surface.rs` as the design's file list says. (2) The registry, the status derivation and `read_health_snapshot` live in a new `E/health.rs` (the design named the tests `health::tests`, so this is where it pointed); `app.rs` could not take them and stay under the 2,000-line cap. (3) The two documented stderr sites are not "the two pre-log-path failures in `main.rs`": `init_log` runs as soon as the runtime paths resolve, so the protocol mismatch logs to `engine.log` like everything after it, and the second documented site is the fallback inside `log_event` for a line the file could not take — nothing is dropped, and the shell keeps it in `shell.log`. The two test-only stderr prints (the `cfg(test)` console-port guard, `tests_console_link`) became `println!` so the count is honest without an allowlist. (4) `warning` covers a failed backup as well as a late one, and the last-backup time is seeded from the newest backup on disk at bootstrap, so a restart does not forget when the last copy was written. (5) A sixth entry, `restore`, records the S7 pending-restore application (the S7 ledger hook). (6) "Support shows Attention on port fallback" lands as the Support surface already renders it: the health sentence in the DEGRADED state (a probe not green) and the Deck lamp; the Setup word is READY while the probes were green and SETUP REQUIRED on the lane's clean runtime, so the lane asserts the snapshot fields and the store's `degraded` recovery state, and `setupState.test.ts` pins the word / tone / sentence mapping; the front-end source is untouched (D6). (7) The OSC decode-failure line is `DEBUG`, not `WARN`, so a malformed-datagram flood cannot become a log flood. Decisions recorded, not deviations: `health.snapshot` stays out of `RECOVERY_METHODS` (it still reads the database for the lighting, audio and SQLite facts; the recovery surface reads `engine.startupFailed`, and the recovery engine's registry says `storage` `error` for any later reader); no port scan (F14's text asks for `attention` on the failure, and a scan would move the deck's port from under Companion); `recentLogExcerpt` stays the string the protocol has always sent (the front-end `RecoverySurface` reads an array — a pre-existing mismatch left for S9, below); a first `ok` report is silent so the bootstrap's reports do not cost the store a full refresh each.

Gates before: `read_health_snapshot.status` was `"ok"` for every running engine (`"starting"` was unreachable), `append_log` opened the file per line, `read_log_excerpt` read the whole file for twelve lines, fifteen `eprintln!` sites wrote to a stream the release shell discards, and every request wrote an INFO line; no test asserted any of it.

Tests added: `diagnostics::tests` — `read_log_tail_bounded` (a ~500 KiB log: twelve lines, the last one last, the first one whole, an early marker absent, the whole window ≤ 64 KiB), `read_log_tail_scans_only_the_tail_of_a_large_log` (opt-in with `SSE_ENGINE_TEST_LARGE_LOG=1`: a 100 MB log, the tail read must take < 50 ms), `append_log_rotates_at_limit` (the one test that installs the process-wide writer, with a 400-byte limit and three files: `.1` … `.3` appear, `.4` never, a DEBUG line through `append_log` is dropped at INFO, a different path still gets the direct append), `log_writer_filters_below_its_level_and_keeps_the_size_across_reopen`, `log_level_parses_the_environment_value`, `request_line_is_off_at_info_and_on_at_debug`; `health::tests` — `status_error_on_integrity_failure`, `status_attention_on_bridge_port_fallback`, `status_warning_when_the_last_backup_failed_or_is_older_than_two_days`, `report_signals_a_transition_only_on_a_state_change`, `health_snapshot_lists_engine_checks_and_a_bounded_log_excerpt` (a runtime over a 400 KB log: twelve lines, `checks.engine` an object; a `storage_ready: false` runtime answers `error`); `app::tests::handle_request_writes_no_request_line_at_the_default_level`; shell — `engine::tests::stderr_lines_reach_shell_log` (a `cmd /C echo … 1>&2` / `sh -c` process through `launch`; the line lands in `shell.log` as `ENGINE-STDERR …`), `shell_log::tests::shell_log_rotates_at_its_limit`, `shell_log_opens_under_the_logs_dir_and_keeps_existing_content`; front-end — `recoveryMachine.test.ts` (`attention → degraded`, `warning → degraded`, `error → recovery`, `ok → healthy`, missing or unknown → `healthy`), `setupState.test.ts` (attention with a probe not green → DEGRADED / attention / the health sentence; error keeps DEGRADED with the error tone; ok and all green → READY; unpublished → SETUP REQUIRED); lane check `bridge-port-taken-reads-as-health-attention`. Tests changed: `diagnostics::tests::read_log_excerpt_returns_missing_file_message` → `read_log_tail_returns_missing_file_message` and `read_log_excerpt_returns_last_lines` → `read_log_tail_returns_last_lines` (the function was renamed and takes the byte window); `app::tests::health_summary_includes_all_native_domains` → `health::tests::health_summary_includes_all_native_domains` (moved with the function); the shell's three existing `launch` calls pass `None` for the shell log.

Lanes: `npm run rust:clippy` (and `cargo clippy -p sse-exed-tauri-shell --all-targets --features test-bridge -- -D warnings`), `npm run native:test`, `npm run frontend:test`, `npm run protocol:check`, `node scripts/check-operator-copy.mjs`, `npm run tauri:setup-support:qualify`, `npm run tauri:workspaces:qualify`. Done when a 100 MB `engine.log` leaves `health.snapshot` under 50 ms, `rg "eprintln!" native/rust-engine/src` has ≤ 2 documented hits, and the Support surface shows the attention posture on the port fallback — all three met (below).

After: `cargo fmt` applied; `rust:clippy` clean under `-D warnings`, and the test-bridge shell clippy clean; `native:test` 380 passed / 1 ignored (tauri-shell 20, engine 351 + 1 ignored hardware-bound, `contract.rs` 4, `end_to_end.rs` 5) — the twelve new engine cases, the moved summary case and the three shell cases among them; `rg "eprintln!" native/rust-engine/src` = 2. `tauri:setup-support:qualify` passed in 63.4 s with all sixteen checks over seven steps: `shell-stays-responsive-during-stalled-request` `probeMs` 1872 / `heartbeats` 8 / `maxGapMs` 251, `database-backup-verifies-and-restores-after-restart` at schema 6, `corrupt-db-restores-from-database-backup` at generation 2, `engine-crash-reaches-recovery-and-restarts` `detectMs` 211 / `restartMs` 1305, `second-instance-is-refused` by the plugin in 104 ms, and the new `bridge-port-taken-reads-as-health-attention` — port 57252 held, `healthStatus` `attention`, `recovery` `degraded`, `bridgeDetail` "Native control-surface bridge is unavailable because the listener could not bind: Only one usage of each socket address … (os error 10048)"; ## Slice 9 — Front-end resilience

Status: verified 2026-09-17 (commit `96ae4e9` `Readiness S9: error boundaries, domain-scoped snapshot refresh, reply guards, background-failure band` plus the queue follow-up `c87565a` `Readiness S9 (queue): a restart abandons a refresh batch that never settles`, pushed; both CI runs (35208750697 and 35209951483) are recorded under "CI (result)" below by the follow-up commit `Readiness S9 (ledger): CI run on the branch recorded`, as in S5; every S9 lane, the setup-support lane and `npm run dev:check` green on the workstation; no hardware exposure — the slice binds nothing, writes to no console and sends no light output, so it ends as `verified` like S1, S4 and S8). Closes F10 F11 (front-end half) F32. Depends on S5 S8.

Scope (as landed): **Refresh by domain (F11).** New `EC/store/domainRefresh.ts` (145 lines) — `DomainKey` (one per snapshot the store keeps), `DOMAIN_REQUESTS` (`satisfies Record<DomainKey, RequestMethod>`), `ALL_DOMAINS` (ten: the bootstrap's set and what `refresh()` fetches), `CHANGEABLE_DOMAINS` (eight: everything but the fixture catalog and the deck's page model), and the two maps. `EVENT_DOMAIN_REFRESH satisfies Record<EventName, readonly DomainKey[]>` lists all eleven events: `lighting.changed` → lighting + its DMX monitor (2 requests, was 10), `planning.changed` → planning + commissioning, `commissioning.changed` → commissioning, app, health, lighting + DMX monitor, audio, `app.changed` → app + health (S8's `health` reason included), `settings.changed` → app, `support.changed` → support (S7's `backup-restore-staged` included), `audio.changed` → audio for the record only (it keeps its dedicated path with echo suppression and the console-echo rule), and `audio.meters`, `engine.ready`, `engine.startupFailed`, `engine.exited` → nothing. `domainsForEvent` answers the eight changeable domains and `known: false` for an event this build does not know; a development store logs that. `domainsForMethod(method, params)` scopes the refresh that follows a request by method name or prefix (`settings.update` → app; `commissioning.` → the commissioning set, `seedPlanningDemo` adds planning; `lighting.` → lighting + DMX monitor; `planning.` → planning + commissioning; `support.backup.restore` → the eight; `support.` → support; `exports.` → nothing; an unmapped method → the eight), and adds a workspace's own snapshots to the `settings.update` that opens it (Setup: health, commissioning, support, the deck's page model; Lighting: lighting + DMX monitor; Audio: audio; Planning: planning + commissioning). `EC/store/createShellStore.ts` (1,517 lines, was 1,397): `refreshDomain` is gone; `refreshDomains(domains, event)` marks the snapshots wanted and resolves once a batch that went out after the call has fetched them; `runRefresh` keeps one batch in flight and sends whatever was asked for meanwhile as the next, so a burst costs one request per snapshot, not one per event; `fetchDomains` uses `Promise.allSettled`, so a request that fails does not cost the others their answers; `snapshotsToState` writes only what was fetched — `activeWorkspace` only with the app snapshot, `recovery` only with the health snapshot, and never the lifecycle or the start-up failure, which stay the bootstrap's; a batch that comes back after a restart, or after the hardware link stopped, is dropped; `cancelQueuedRefresh` on bootstrap and dispose. The bootstrap keeps the whole fetch (health first, then the rest side by side) through the same `fetchDomains`. The fixture catalog is fetched once per session (`catalogLoaded`), kept across a restart, and fetched again only by `refresh()`. `refreshControlSurfaceSnapshot` goes through the same queue. **Reply guards (F32).** New `EC/store/snapshotGuards.ts` (93 lines) — `snapshotProblem(domain, value)`: the five loosely typed snapshots must be objects; the five typed ones may be absent (`null` — no console, no plan yet) and otherwise must carry their lists (`fixtures` / `groups` / `scenes`, `definitions`, `channels`, `channels` / `mixTargets`, `projects` / `tasks`, the optional ones only when present) with a string `id` on every keyed row; one pass, no allocation, nothing deeper. `SnapshotShapeError` names the request and the field (`lighting.snapshot: fixtures is not a list`). `createShellStore(transport, { development })`: a development store sets `ShellState.snapshotFault` and throws, and `useShellSnapshot` rethrows the fault while rendering so the root boundary shows it by name; a production store records the failure in the ring (`reply refused`), keeps the last good snapshot and never throws. The app passes `import.meta.env.DEV` (`FA/app/createShellEnvironment.ts`). **Boundaries (F10).** New `FA/app/startup/ShellErrorBoundary.tsx` (132) at the root in `FA/main.tsx`: a class component whose page — `THIS SCREEN STOPPED`, the error in the display's code slot, **Reload**, **Export diagnostics** — is the pre-ready state display in a window-filling container (`ShellErrorBoundary.module.css`, existing tokens only), needs no store, provider or layout context, and exports the error, the component stack and, when it can be read, the shell state; a reload hands the screen back the hardware link that is already running (`T/engine.rs::start` returns the running engine's summary). New `FA/app/startup/WorkspaceErrorBoundary.tsx` (65) around the bay's surface in `FA/app/OperatorShell.tsx` (640, was 606), keyed `experience:workspace:reloads`: `LIGHTING STOPPED` on the same state display with **Reload this area**, no element of its own while nothing has failed, and the pre-ready surfaces inside it too, so a recovery surface that fails to draw cannot take the header, the restart dialog and the close dialog with it. New `FA/app/startup/reportUiFailure.ts` (24): both boundaries and the two window listeners in `main.tsx` record through it, and a `WeakSet` keeps each error object once. New `FA/app/shared/BackgroundFailureBand.tsx` (52) + `.module.css`: the design system's `StatusBand` (`warning`) at the foot of the bay while the ring is non-empty and the shell is ready — "Studio Control hit a problem in the background", how many and since when, **Dismiss** until the next failure, no cause named. New `FA/app/startup/WorkspaceCrashProbe.tsx` (18) and the `crash` URL parameter in `createShellEnvironment.ts` (46): fixture transport only, never inside the Tauri runtime; `window.__SSE_TEST_DISARM_CRASH__` exists only when the parameter armed a fault. `FA/app/setup/SetupSupportPilot.tsx` (1,689): the diagnostics export carries `backgroundFailures`, read from `store.getSnapshot()` at the moment of the export. **S8's hook.** `FA/app/startup/RecoverySurface.tsx` `readLogExcerpt` reads the string the hardware link has always sent (split into lines) and still a list; `frontend/packages/test-fixtures/src/fixtures.json` carries strings; `check-fixtures.mjs` requires one. Docs: `CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/OPERATIONS.md` ("When part of the screen stops"), `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md` (the maps, the queue, the development store, the boundaries, the crash hook). No engine, shell or protocol change: `protocol:check` and `native:test` are untouched.

**Rescope:** eight deviations, none silent, and the title stands because what landed is what it says. (1) The refresh after a request stays, scoped by method, beside the event's — the design only names the event map. On the hardware link `settings.update` and `exports.companion.export` answer with no event at all (`E/app.rs` replies with `Self::reply`), so a workspace switch reaches the screen only through that refresh. Dropping it was checked against both transports: the fixture double raises an event for every mutation it implements and would have allowed it, the hardware link does not. The two refreshes share one queue and never run side by side; a lighting request from the UI costs 1 + 2 + 2 requests (was 1 + 10 + 10) — the pair is not merged because the refresh after a request must go out after its reply arrived, and merging it with the event's batch would lean on an ordering between two shell commands that the async command pool (S4) does not promise. (2) Three mappings are wider than the design's examples, each because of what the engine's snapshot builder reads: `commissioning.changed` adds lighting, its DMX monitor and audio (`E/lighting/snapshot.rs` derives `reachable` from the stored probe status and the monitor from the lighting snapshot; `E/audio/helpers.rs` derives the capabilities from the audio probe status; publishing gives the rig its bridge and universe), and `planning.changed` adds commissioning (`read_commissioning_snapshot` carries the planning counts). Both were found by the qualification lanes, not by reading — see Validation. `lighting.changed` is the design's two requests. (3) "Unknown → full refresh" is the eight changeable snapshots, not ten: the fixture catalog is compiled in (`read_lighting_fixture_catalog_snapshot()` takes no argument) and so is the deck's page model (`build_control_surface_snapshot()` takes none; its one live field, `lastEvent`, is polled by the Setup verify step every 500 ms and fetched when Setup opens). (4) Opening a workspace fetches that workspace's snapshots — not in the design. Scoped refreshes no longer sweep up, by accident, the changes that raise no event: the Stream Deck's lighting and planning actions (`E/control_surface*.rs` raises only `audio.changed { control-surface }`), the daily backup appearing in the backups folder, the health sentences that describe lighting and audio state, the commissioning snapshot after `audio.settings.update` reset the audio probe. The moment the operator looks is when they are fetched: two to five requests per switch, was ten. (5) `import.meta.env.DEV` is not read inside `engine-client` — its tsconfig extends the base with no `vite/client` types, so the expression typechecks in the app's program and not in the package's own — but injected as `{ development }`; and "throws, caught by the boundary" is the fault kept in the state and rethrown by `useShellSnapshot` while rendering, because a boundary catches render errors only and a refresh is not one. (6) The band sits at the foot of the bay, against the footer, not inside it: the footer is 40 px of fixed chrome (plan D4) and a 58 px band cannot enter it without moving every board. It is rendered ahead of the surface and placed with `order`, so the surface stays the workspace stack's last child and no existing rule changes. (7) Files beyond the design's list: `domainRefresh.ts`, `reportUiFailure.ts`, `WorkspaceCrashProbe.tsx`, `BackgroundFailureBand.tsx`, the two CSS modules, the recovery surface, the fixtures and their check — the new code went into new files because of the 2,000-line cap (trap 11). (8) The setup-support lane was run although the slice lists only the workspace lane, because the slice changed what that lane exercises (the archive restore's five events, the restart, recovery mode, S8's `degraded`); it found the second coupling. Decisions recorded, not deviations: `shellState.recovery` `degraded` is still rendered nowhere and is left so — the band is for failures, a band for `attention` would put a new state on the three `setup-degraded` boards (D6), and the Support health sentence and the Deck lamp already carry that posture; the pre-ready surfaces sit inside the workspace boundary; the band names no cause (the ring's messages are written for whoever reads the diagnostics file, and the copy gate's words appear in them); `coerceAudioSnapshot` stays for telling a whole audio snapshot from a partial command result.

Gates before: every event and every non-audio request cost ten snapshot requests (twenty for a request whose event also arrived), a refresh that came back after the hardware link stopped wrote `lifecycle: "ready"` and cleared the start-up failure, replies were cast to their types unseen, a render error blanked the window, and the background-failure ring was visible only in a lane's diagnostics export (the Support export did not carry it). No test asserted a request count outside the audio path, and none rendered a component that throws.

Tests added: `EC/store/createShellStore.test.ts` (810 lines, was 372) — "event refreshes only mapped domains" (the measure: one `lighting.changed` → `lighting.snapshot` + `lighting.dmxMonitor.snapshot`; then all eleven events against a table written out in the test and typed `Record<EventName, string[]>`), "a staged restore refreshes support alone and a health transition app and health" (S7's and S8's hooks; `recovery` becomes `degraded`), "unknown event full refresh" (the eight, and the development warning), "catalog once per session" (one request across an event, a request and a restart; `refresh()` asks again and asks for all ten), "a request refreshes only what its method can change" (the four workspace switches, a section change, lighting, selection, planning, a probe, the seed, export, the Stream Deck profile, an applied restore), "events raised together are fetched as one batch" (the restore's five events: each snapshot once, was fifty requests), "events that arrive while a batch is out share the next one" (five events behind a held request → one more `lighting.snapshot`, not five), "a partial refresh writes only what it fetched" (workspace and recovery untouched by an event that names neither; a refresh that returns after `engine.exited` leaves `failed` and `ENGINE_EXITED` alone), "a request that fails does not cost the others their answers", "malformed snapshot rejected in dev" (`refresh()` rejects with the request and field named, the fault is in the state, the last good snapshot stays, the rest of the batch arrives), "a row without an id is refused by name", "a malformed snapshot in production is a failed refresh, never a throw", "every fixture scenario passes the guards" (a development store over every scenario of the fixture double); new `domainRefresh.test.ts` (lighting and its DMX monitor are never fetched apart, over every event, every request method, the four workspace switches and the two fallbacks; the catalog is never refreshed by a mapping and the deck's page model only by opening Setup; a method matches by its own name before its prefix; the workspace-switch rule) and `snapshotGuards.test.ts`; `FA/app/startup/ShellErrorBoundary.test.tsx` (11 cases: the root boundary untouched while nothing fails, the page with no store at all — Reload, Export diagnostics, the fixture file name — the export when the state cannot be read, a malformed reply shown by name in a development build; the workspace boundary adds no element, keeps a render error inside the area and brings the area back on reload; `reportUiFailure` records the same error object once whether the boundary or the window listener sees it first, and contains a sink that throws; the band is absent while nothing failed, says how many and since when, names no cause, returns after Dismiss only for a new failure, and survives an unparseable time; `readLogExcerpt`); Playwright `frontend/app/tests/shell.spec.ts` "workspace crash keeps shell alive" (`audio-populated` with `crash=lighting`: `LIGHTING STOPPED`, no root page, tabs, clock, lamps and the restart dialog alive, the band at one problem, Audio's Solo FX 3/4 toggles, Dismiss, the second failure counted, then the fault disarmed and **Reload this area** brings back the stage and the footer) and "the crash hook is absent unless the fixture URL asks for it". Tests changed: the supervision double in `createShellStore.test.ts` answered `{ protocol: "1" }` for every request → it answers `null` for the five typed snapshots (no console, rig or plan in that double) and the same object for the rest → the guards rightly refuse an object without its lists as a lighting or planning snapshot, and "keeps the last twenty background failures without leaving ready" would otherwise find those refusals in the ring first; the double also gained `hold(method)` and `snapshotRequests()`. Two fixture scenarios' `recentLogExcerpt` list → string.

Lanes: `npm run frontend:typecheck`, `npm run frontend:test`, `npm run build --workspace frontend/app && npm run frontend:storybook:build && npm run frontend:playwright:test`, `npm run tauri:workspaces:qualify`, `npm run tauri:setup-support:qualify`, `node scripts/check-operator-copy.mjs`, `npm run dev:check`. Done when one `lighting.changed` costs 2 requests (was 10) on a recording transport and a Lighting render error leaves Audio usable — both met (below).

Validation (workstation, 2026-09-17): baseline before editing — `frontend:typecheck` green, `frontend:test` 280 (app 70, design-system 175, engine-client 20, tokens 15). After: `frontend:typecheck` green; `frontend:test` 315 (app 81, design-system 175, engine-client 44, tokens 15); `node scripts/check-operator-copy.mjs` 0 hits; `npm run dev:check` green — format, lint, `scripts:test`, `file:health` (`createShellStore.ts` 1,517, `SetupSupportPilot.tsx` 1,689, nothing new over 2,000), rustfmt, clippy, `protocol:check` (no contract change), typecheck, the 315 Vitest cases, `native:check`, `native:test` 380 passed / 1 ignored (tauri-shell 20, engine 351 + 1, `contract.rs` 4, `end_to_end.rs` 5), unchanged. Playwright against the rebuilt `dist` and `storybook-static`: 417 passed in 2.8 min (415 + the two new cases), the 81 UI-contract boards, `visual-review` and `storybook` among them; run twice, the second time on the final maps. Both qualification lanes run the development store (they start `tauri dev` over the Vite dev server), so they are also the proof that every live reply passes the guards. `tauri:workspaces:qualify`: **failed on the first run** — "Expected live lighting DMX monitor snapshot to expose channels": after publish, `setWorkspace("lighting")` no longer dragged a full refresh along, `commissioning.changed` refreshed lighting without its monitor, and the monitor stayed as empty as the clean runtime had left it; `LIGHTING_DOMAINS` now composes every list that names lighting, and `domainRefresh.test.ts` holds the rule. It then passed in 17.7 s with its five checks (audio receive ports 55939 and 52897, throwaways; nothing written to 7001–7010), and again on the final maps. `tauri:setup-support:qualify`: **failed on its first run**, nine checks in, in the database-restore step — "Expected a third planning project after the backup was written": `commissioningSnapshot.planningProjectCount` stayed at 2 because a planning change refreshed planning alone; `PLANNING_DOMAINS` now carries commissioning. It then passed in 49.4 s (63.4 s in S8) with all sixteen checks over seven steps: `shell-stays-responsive-during-stalled-request` `probeMs` 1912 / `heartbeats` 9 / `maxGapMs` 261, `backup-restore-round-trips-native-support-backup`, `database-backup-verifies-and-restores-after-restart` at schema 6, `corrupt-db-restores-from-database-backup` at generation 2, `engine-crash-reaches-recovery-and-restarts` `detectMs` 102 / `restartMs` 1124, `second-instance-is-refused` by the plugin in 100 ms, `bridge-port-taken-reads-as-health-attention` — port 53871 held, `healthStatus` `attention`, `recovery` `degraded` through the scoped `app.changed` map. After the two failures the snapshot builders were read for every input rather than waiting for a third: the app snapshot reads shell, commissioning-stage and planning settings (Planning uses its planning section only to pick a loading skeleton), health reads the two probe-derived checks, the bridge state and the registry, commissioning reads the probe records, the bridge and console addresses and the planning counts, the DMX monitor is a function of the lighting snapshot, support lists the backups folder, the deck's page model and the catalog take no input; the maps above are that table. In the dev build the flow was walked by hand in the Browser pane at 2560 × 1440: the area display and the band beside a live header, the band's text at 5.4:1 or better and 12 px in Studio, Graphite and Bone with a 28 px Dismiss key, the root page rendered from the real component into a scratch root (fills the window, Reload and Export diagnostics answer). **Production app (the standing permission of 2026-09-17):** the packaged app was open on 38201 (shell 65144, engine 63660, both started 09:58:10 from `release\native\windows\…`); the database had not been written since 10:51:06. The shell alone was ended with `taskkill //F`; the engine exited on its own within a second, 38201 was free, TotalMix FX and Companion were not touched. What did not happen: the engine left **no shutdown backup and no log line** for that exit — the newest backup is `db-2026-09-17T08-51-18-418Z-shutdown.sqlite3` (10:51:18, after the last database write, so nothing was lost), and `engine.log` ends on its minute-by-minute "401: a bearer token is required" from Companion (Appendix B item 1 is unsigned, so the deck is refused by that build). The procedure's expectation that a force-ended shell gives the engine its graceful path did not hold for the packaged build; the shell has no job object (`T/engine.rs` sets `CREATE_NO_WINDOW` only), and the cause was not pursued — it is outside this slice. The app stays closed until the operator reopens it.

Queue follow-up (workstation, 2026-09-17; commit `c87565a` `Readiness S9 (queue): a restart abandons a refresh batch that never settles`): found reading the queue again after the slice commit was pushed, not by a lane. `runRefresh` keeps one batch in flight and `refreshRunning` said so until the batch settled — so a request that never settled would have held every later refresh behind it, and `cancelQueuedRefresh` cleared what was queued but not that flag, so **Restart the hardware link**, the operator's remedy for a screen that has stopped updating, would not have freed it. Before this slice a refresh that hung held only itself. It should not happen on the hardware link — `T/engine.rs` gives up on a request after ten seconds and fails what is pending when the engine stops — which is why no lane could find it; the property is cheap to hold anyway. `createShellStore.ts` (1,532 lines): a run counter (`refreshRun`) that every bootstrap and dispose advances; a batch belongs to the run that sent it, an abandoned run drops its answers and leaves the flag to the run that replaced it, and the requests waiting on the abandoned batch are released with the queued ones. Test added: `createShellStore.test.ts` (849 lines) "a restart abandons a batch that never settles" — a `lighting.snapshot` that never answers, a request queued behind it, an event that gets nothing through; after `restart()` the waiting request resolves and the next `planning.changed` fetches its two snapshots. Run against the slice commit's store it fails (`expected [] to deeply equal [ 'commissioning.snapshot', … ]`), so it guards the fix; the double gained `stall(method)`. Validation: `frontend:typecheck` green; `frontend:test` 316 (app 81, design-system 175, engine-client 45, tokens 15); Playwright on the rebuilt `dist` 417 passed in 2.8 min; `tauri:setup-support:qualify` passed in 50.1 s with all sixteen checks (its restarts — the database restore, the corrupt-database restore, the engine crash — are the paths the change touches); `tauri:workspaces:qualify` passed in 18.4 s with its five checks (audio receive port 52006, a throwaway); `npm run dev:check` green.

Baselines: none. No board moved — the band is absent while nothing has failed, the boundary adds no element, and the recovery log excerpt renders only on `RecoverySurface`, which no fixture board reaches (a failed start on a fixture is on the Setup tab and shows `SetupRecoverySurface`); no story was added. The visual lanes ran (above) and no snapshot or ratchet file changed.

Operator hands: none. Notes for later slices: S10 — the Stream Deck's lighting and planning actions raise no event (`E/control_surface*.rs`: only `audio.changed { control-surface }`), so a deck key pressed while Lighting is already open shows only at the next lighting event, request or tab switch; `handle_light_action` is S10's file, and `lighting.changed { reason: "control-surface" }` from there (and `planning.changed` from the planning actions) closes it. S11 — `lighting.output.setArmed` falls under the `lighting.` prefix and its event under `lighting.changed`; a new event fails the typecheck until it is mapped, a new method falls back to the eight until it is; `recentEvents` on the support snapshot is refreshed by `support.changed` and by opening Setup — decide whether recording an action should raise `support.changed`. S13 — the request-count assertions in `createShellStore.test.ts` are the fan-out's regression guard; `audio.settings.update` with a changed console address resets the audio probe engine-side while the audio path refreshes audio alone (older than this slice; opening Setup now fetches the rest). S14 — the fixture double does not implement `lighting.fixture.identify`, `.highlight`, `.identifySequence`, `.identify.clearAll`, `lighting.scene.reorder`, `.pin`, `lighting.group.reorder` and `lighting.fixture.delete` (it answers `{}` and raises nothing); the split is the moment to make it answer as the hardware link does. S15 / operator — the packaged production build predates S5 and S8 and does not shut down as `docs/OPERATIONS.md` describes when its shell is force-ended (above); repackaging from this branch once the Appendix B items are signed is the fix, and the close procedure in the session prompt should say "ends without a shutdown backup on the packaged build" until then.

Correction (2026-09-17, recorded by the Slice 10 session; what Slice 9 wrote above stands as written): the cause that Slice 9's Validation says "was not pursued" is known. The packaged production build under `release\native\windows\SSE ExEd Studio Control Native\` is not merely one that "predates S5 and S8" — it is the S2-era build: `grep -a -c` on its engine finds `control-surface.token` and none of `pre-migration`, `engine.lock`, `ENGINE_ALREADY_RUNNING`, `restore-pending`, `engine.log.1` (engine exe 2026-09-10 16:08, the S2 packaging run; shell exe 2026-09-04). It has no database backup code at all, no integrity check, no single-instance guard and no supervised restart, so ending its shell leaves no shutdown backup and no log line by construction — expected, not a fault of the close, and not a regression of S3 or S5. The `db-*-shutdown.sqlite3` files of that morning can therefore only have been written by a newer build run against the same app-data (this one cannot write them). Nothing was lost on 2026-09-17, as Slice 9 recorded: the database had been idle since 10:51:06, and the 401 lines from Companion in its log are Appendix B item 1, still unsigned. Re-checked on 2026-09-17 by the Slice 10 session with the same `grep` on the same file, with the same result. The note for S15 / the operator above is otherwise right: repackaging from this branch is the fix, and it is the operator's step (Slice 10, Operator hands).

## Slice 10 — Lighting concurrency + sACN render path

Status: landed 2026-09-17 (operator verification pending, Appendix B item 5; commit `957acc9` `Readiness S10: lighting state lock, deck keys through the shared preview, sACN settings cache, kept read connections`, pushed; CI run 35230069147 recorded under "CI (result)" below by the follow-up commit `Readiness S10 (ledger): CI run on the branch recorded`, as in S0–S9; every S10 lane, the setup-support lane, the `dev-fixtures` build and `npm run dev:check` green on the workstation; the slice has hardware exposure — the sACN render path and the Stream Deck's lighting keys — so it ends as `landed` and waits on Appendix B item 5, which is walked together with S11 and does not block it). Closes F12 F18. Depends on S2 S3.

Scope (as landed): **The lock, the preview, the generation (F12).** New `E/lighting/state_lock.rs` (103 lines): `static LIGHTING_STATE_LOCK: Mutex<()>` with the lock-order rule in the comment beside it (the lighting state lock first, then the shared preview; a reader takes the preview alone; never held together with `AUDIO_STATE_LOCK`; not re-entrant); `with_lighting_state(|| …)` takes the lock, runs the whole mutation — its load, its change, its persist — and advances `LIGHTING_RENDER_GENERATION` while still holding it, whether the mutation succeeded or was refused (a guard's `Drop`, so a panic advances it too); `with_lighting_state_and_preview(|preview| …)` is the same with the shared preview taken second and handed in; `shared_lighting_preview()` (a `OnceLock<Mutex<LightingPreviewRuntimeState>>`, as `shared_console_link()` is), `lock_shared_lighting_preview()` for readers, `lighting_render_generation()`, `bump_lighting_render_generation()`; under `cfg(test)` `reset_shared_preview()` and `shared_preview_test_guard()`. `E/app.rs` (1,712 lines, was 1,874): `EngineApp.lighting_preview` and its accessor are gone; `dispatch_lighting_preview_mutate` runs its handler inside `with_lighting_state_and_preview`, `dispatch_lighting_mutate` inside `with_lighting_state`; `read_lighting_snapshot` takes the shared preview and then reads the settings, so the pair is from one side of any preview-aware mutation; the archive restore (`support.backup.restore`) and the dev parity fixture run inside `with_lighting_state`; the commissioning check advances the generation after its write. **Every writer of what the lighting state or the wire depends on, and who holds the lock:** (a) the lighting mutations in `E/lighting/` (about sixty load → persist sites) — reached only through the two dispatchers (IPC thread) and the deck's lighting handler (four bridge workers), all three under the lock; the functions themselves take none, so `*_with_preview` can fall through to the plain function and a recall can record its own failure; (b) the Stream Deck's lighting keys — under the lock, selection keys included (a read-then-write of the selected id); (c) the archive restore, which deletes and rewrites every `app.lighting.*` key in one transaction — under the lock, so a deck key cannot write its older copy back over it (it touches audio settings too and takes no audio lock, so the two locks are never held together); (d) the dev parity fixture (`dev-fixtures` builds only) — under the lock; (e) `commissioning.check.run`, which stores the bridge address and the universe a lighting probe used (`app.commissioning.lighting.*`) and the probe's status — single upserts, never a read-modify-write of the lighting state, so no lock; the generation is advanced after it, because the address and the universe are part of what the wire carries; `commissioning.update` writes the stage keys only, which no lighting read uses — no lock, nothing advanced; (f) the schema migrations, the default seeding and the staged database restore — before any thread exists; (g) `storage.importLegacyDb` — planning rows, planning settings, the workspace and the commissioning stage, no lighting key; (h) `record_lighting_action_failure` — three upserts from inside a lighting function, already under the caller's lock; (i) the deck's `last_event` stamp — one upsert of its own key. `save_lighting_editor_state`, the whole-state writer the deck used, is `cfg(test)` now: outside the tests the state can only be written by a lighting function. **The deck through the screen's functions (F12).** `E/control_surface.rs` (1,202 lines, was 1,451): `handle_light_action` answers `switchToDeckMode` as before (a planning setting) and runs every other key inside `with_lighting_state_and_preview`; `locked_light_action` builds each request with the `parse_lighting_*_request` functions and calls `update_lighting_fixture_with_preview` (toggle, intensity ±5, colour temperature ±200 K, the two resets), `set_lighting_all_power_with_preview`, `recall_lighting_scene_with_preview` — and nothing after it —, `create_lighting_scene_with_preview` and `delete_lighting_scene`. The relative keys start from new `lighting::read_lighting_fixture_levels(settings, preview, fixture_id)` (`E/lighting/preview.rs`, 601 lines): the preview buffer while previewing, otherwise the stored value with a running fade sampled now, never the identify / highlight / solo overlays. Replies of the value, all-power and recall keys carry `preview`; a value key's reply carries what was stored. The `light_intensity` and `light_cct` LCD texts read the same values under the preview guard and end in a third line `PREVIEW` while previewing; the LCD no longer renders a whole lighting snapshot it never used on every poll. **Events from the deck (S9's hook).** `E/engine_events.rs` (69): `emit_lighting_changed`, `emit_planning_changed`; `handle_control_surface_http_action` raises `lighting.changed { reason: "control-surface" }` after a successful lighting key and `planning.changed { reason: "control-surface", projectId: null, taskId: null }` after a planning key or `switchToDeckMode` (`deck_change_event`; `openDetail` and the audio route raise nothing new). **The render path (F18).** `E/lighting_sacn_output.rs` (556, was 332): `RenderSettingsCache::settings_for_tick(generation, now, read)` reads the database when the generation has moved since the last read or `SETTINGS_SAFETY_REREAD` (2 s) has passed, keeps the last good settings through a failed read and retries on the next tick; the loop renders every 40 ms tick from the kept settings — settings, never frames, so fades and identify bursts still move with the clock; `send_frame`, `terminate_universes`, the 800 ms keep-alive and the packet layout are untouched. The S8 health report was already there. **Kept read connections (F18).** `E/storage.rs` (994, was 889): `enable_thread_read_connection()` opts a thread in; `list_settings_by_prefix` then reads through `with_read_connection` — one connection per thread, opened read-write and made `query_only` with the 5 s busy timeout, the journal mode and `synchronous` left as the file has them, replaced when the path differs, dropped after a failed read, no statement held between calls; `checkpoint_database()` (`PRAGMA wal_checkpoint(TRUNCATE)`, an error when a reader blocked it). Opted in: the sACN thread, the metering thread (`E/rme_totalmix_osc.rs`, the reader switch) and the engine's bridge workers (`E/control_surface_http.rs` 1,375: `BridgeContext::keeping_read_connections`, set by `start_control_surface_bridge` only; the worker loop moved into `serve_queued_connections`). **The write-ahead log.** Threads that keep a connection never close it, so SQLite's checkpoint-on-last-close no longer happens: `E/main.rs` (622) checkpoints after the shutdown backup, and `bootstrap::apply_pending_restore` (`E/bootstrap.rs` 1,316) checkpoints the database before it moves it aside and deletes its `-wal` file, best effort (a database that cannot be opened is kept as it is, with a WARN line). Test modules moved out under the 2,000-line guard, paths unchanged: `E/app/tests.rs` (350), `E/control_surface/tests.rs` (621); new `E/lighting/tests_state_lock.rs` (238). Docs: `native/protocol/v1.md` (the lighting keys, the `preview` field, the two `control-surface` reasons — no contract change, both events exist), `docs/OPERATIONS.md` (Lighting Output, Data Safety), `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md` (the lock order, the generation, the kept connections, the checkpoint rule, the test guard), `CHANGELOG.md`. No front-end source changed: the store already maps both events (S9).

**Rescope:** ten deviations, none silent, and the title stands because what landed is what it says. (1) `with_lighting_state` wraps the entry point's whole mutation; it does not take `db_path` and does not load or persist itself. The design's sentence reads both ways; the other reading rewrites about sixty load → persist sites into closures for the same serialisation, because every one of those sites is reached only through the two dispatchers and the deck's handler. The writer list above is the argument, and `save_lighting_editor_state` going `cfg(test)` is what keeps it true. (2) Files: the lock, the generation and the shared preview live in a new `lighting/state_lock.rs` (the design put them in `helpers.rs` and `preview.rs`) so the order rule sits beside everything it governs; `lighting/helpers.rs` and `lighting/palettes.rs` from the design's list did not change; `engine_events.rs`, `control_surface_http.rs`, `bootstrap.rs`, `main.rs`, `lighting/editor_state.rs` and the three test files are beyond it. (3) The deck: `saveScene` and `deleteScene` go through the screen's functions too, which the design does not list. The deck keeps its scene name (`Scene N`); the id comes from `next_custom_scene_id` (highest + 1) — the deck's own rule counted the scenes and, after a delete, gave a new scene the id of a live one. `allOn` / `allOff` with no fixture is refused (409) where it answered ok and did nothing. While previewing, the LCD shows the preview buffer with a `PREVIEW` line rather than the live value: a key whose display does not move reads as a dead key, and a staged number without the word would read as the rig's. (4) The generation advances on every exit from `with_lighting_state`, refusals included, and by hand after the commissioning check (the one writer outside the lighting functions that changes what the wire carries: the bridge address and the universe); a restore and the parity fixture advance it through the lock; anything else is the 2 s safety read's. "≤ 1 read per state change" is therefore: one read per generation change, plus one per two seconds, plus the first — which is what the counting test asserts, with the generation and the clock handed in. (5) The kept connection is a per-thread opt-in inside `list_settings_by_prefix`, not a second function called from every settings read the bridge workers reach across `control_surface.rs` and `control_surface_audio.rs`; a thread that did not opt in — the IPC loop, every test thread, a test bridge's workers — behaves exactly as before. (6) The two checkpoints are not in the design. They follow from trap 7: without them a graceful stop leaves the newest commits in `-wal`, and a restore after an engine that was ended rather than stopped kept a `replaced` copy without them — that second gap exists at `96939dc` too (the new bootstrap test fails without the call), the kept connections would only have made it the normal case. (7) The events from the deck are S9's hook, not the design's. (8) The concurrency test is 2 × 20 operations through the real entry points in `app::tests`, not 2 × 200 in `lighting::tests`. A commit waits for the disk (S3) — about 35 ms here, more when a per-call connection is the last one and its close checkpoints — and the first version (two hundred group creations against two hundred scene saves) passed in 303 s. Twenty group creations against twenty presses of the intensity key (0 % → 20 × 5 = 100 %) pass in 2.6 s and fail every time without the lock, because each side reads while the other commits. (9) Lanes beyond the design's list: `tauri:setup-support:qualify` (the backup, restore and restart paths the kept connections could disturb), the `dev-fixtures` build (the parity dispatcher compiles only there), and the packaging lane run with the production folder moved aside (below). (10) The test modules of `app.rs` and `control_surface.rs` moved to their own files: `app.rs` was 126 lines under the cap with S11 still to come, `control_surface.rs` reached 1,825 with the new tests. Decisions recorded, not deviations: a restore does not clear the preview (the buffer is keyed by fixture id and a missing id is ignored); the deck's `deleteScene` with nothing selected still acts on the first scene, which is the scene its LCD shows as selected; `SACN_PORT` stays a constant — the loop's read and render decisions are tested as values and no test sends a frame, so S11's `held_outputs_send_nothing` is the slice that makes the destination injectable.

Gates before: no test pressed a lighting key on the deck at all (`control_surface.rs` tested LCD text, audio and the verify echo), none ran two lighting mutations at once, none counted the sACN thread's reads, and `pending_restore_applied_after_snapshot` staged empty `-wal` / `-shm` files, so nothing noticed that the log is deleted with whatever it holds. The deck loaded the whole lighting state, changed it and saved the whole of it back with no lock, no preview and no event; its recall saved its pre-recall copy over what the recall had just written; the sACN thread opened the database twenty-five times a second.

Tests added (16; engine 351 → 367): `app::tests` — `deck_and_ipc_mutations_serialize_without_lost_updates` (twenty `lighting.group.create` requests through `handle_request` against twenty `intensityUp` keys through the bridge's action handler, a third thread reading `lighting.snapshot` throughout: twenty more groups, every id once, the fixture at 100 %), `ipc_preview_mode_is_the_preview_the_deck_edits` (`lighting.editor.previewMode` over IPC, a deck `toggleLight`: `previewFixtures` shows it, `fixtures` and the DMX monitor do not move; after `previewDiscard` the same key moves the rig); `control_surface::tests` — `deck_preview_action_updates_preview_runtime` (intensity, toggle, colour temperature, all-on and recall while previewing: the shared buffer holds each change, the stored fixture and every one of the 512 slots the wire would carry are unchanged, no recall is recorded, the LCD reads `INTENSITY\n100%\nPREVIEW`; out of preview the same key moves the stored value and the frame), `deck_relative_keys_store_through_the_fixture_update` (97 % + 5 = 100, the reply and the LCD carry the stored colour temperature, which the fixture's own range caps below the deck's 6500 K), `deck_recall_writes_what_a_recall_writes_and_nothing_after_it` (a 10 s fade from the screen, then the deck's recall: no fade left, every fixture at the scene's values), `deck_save_scene_never_reuses_a_live_scene_id` (save, save, delete the first, save: every id once, `scene_order` kept, the deck selects what it saved), `deck_light_and_planning_actions_raise_their_events` (the one test that installs the process-wide event sender: a refused key raises nothing, a lighting key `lighting.changed`, the deck mode and a planning key `planning.changed`, all `control-surface`; plus the `deck_change_event` table); `lighting::tests_state_lock` — `with_lighting_state_advances_the_render_generation` (success, refusal, the preview variant, the bare bump — asserted as advanced, never as a number), `fixture_levels_are_the_stored_values_with_a_finished_fade_applied` (a fade that ran out twenty seconds ago: the stored value is still the origin, the levels are the end values), `fixture_levels_follow_the_preview_buffer_while_previewing`, `writers_and_readers_of_the_shared_preview_never_wait_on_each_other_forever` (two writers × 200 and a reader × 200; a lock-order mistake hangs it); `lighting_sacn_output::tests` — `rerenders_settings_only_on_generation_change` (a second of ticks, one read; one change, one read; the safety read at two seconds and not a millisecond before; a failed read keeps the last good settings and is retried; nothing read yet → nothing to render), `render_frames_identical_before_and_after_cache` (frames from a fresh read, from the first tick and from ten ticks later are byte-identical; 40 % is DMX 102, an unlit fixture 0 with its colour temperature slot still set; the reader panics if an unchanged generation reads); `storage::tests` — `thread_local_read_connection_reused` (no connection kept without the opt-in; twenty-five reads, one open; another path replaces it; opting out closes it and the directories can be removed), `kept_read_connection_sees_later_writes_and_blocks_neither_backup_nor_checkpoint` (a later commit is visible, a write through it is refused, a failed read opens a fresh one, a backup copy and a checkpoint go through while it is open and the `-wal` file is empty afterwards); `bootstrap::tests::pending_restore_folds_the_write_ahead_log_into_the_replaced_copy` (the file and the log of a killed engine, copied as they are on disk; the premise — the file alone lacks the commit — is asserted; the replaced copy carries it and the restored database is the backup). **Each guard proved against the code without its fix:** the concurrency test with the lock taken out of `with_lighting_state` failed 3 of 3 runs (the fixture at 75 %, then 21 of 22 groups twice); the recall and save-scene assertions, run against an export of `native/` at `96939dc`, both failed (the fade still there; `scene-custom-5` handed out twice); the bootstrap test without the checkpoint read `checkpointed` where `only-in-the-log` was due; the event test without the emit failed on `lighting.changed`. Tests changed: none in substance — the three `app.rs` tests and the eleven `control_surface.rs` tests moved with their modules; `app_for` builds `EngineApp { runtime }`.

Lanes: `npm run rust:clippy`, `npm run native:test`, `cargo clippy` and `cargo test -p studio-control-engine --features dev-fixtures`, `npm run protocol:check`, `npm run tauri:workspaces:qualify`, `npm run tauri:setup-support:qualify`, `npm run native:engine:build && npm run tauri:build`, then `npm run native:package:win:local && npm run native:bridge:win:verify` with the production folder moved aside, `npm run dev:check`. Done when the sACN thread performs ≤ 1 database read per state change (the counting reader; see Rescope 4 for what the sentence has to mean), a deck action while previewing edits the preview buffer and not live output, and the concurrency test is green — all three met (below).

Validation (workstation, 2026-09-17): baseline before editing — `rust:clippy` clean, `native:test` 380 passed / 1 ignored (tauri-shell 20, engine 351 + 1, `contract.rs` 4, `end_to_end.rs` 5); 38201, 4173 and 6007 free, the production app closed since the S9 session. After: `cargo fmt` applied; `rust:clippy` clean under `-D warnings`; `native:test` 396 passed / 1 ignored (tauri-shell 20, engine 367 + 1, `contract.rs` 4, `end_to_end.rs` 5); the engine's unit suite five more times in a row, 367 each time (the preview, the generation and the event sender are process-wide); it takes 7–9 s now, was 3.7 s, because the new deck tests commit to disk; the `dev-fixtures` build: clippy clean, 369 + 1 / 4 / 5; `protocol:check` green with no contract change. The qualification and packaging lanes ran twice: a first time, green with the same checks, before the last two edits (the deck's LCD reading the settings under the preview guard, with the `control_surface.rs` test module moved out; and `commissioning.update` going back to its `96939dc` shape once it was clear that it writes nothing the wire depends on), and again on the final tree after the unit lanes had been repeated on it — the figures here are the second run's. `tauri:workspaces:qualify` passed in 20.6 s with its five checks (audio receive port 54939, a throwaway; `overrideProbes: false`). `tauri:setup-support:qualify` passed in 47.5 s with all sixteen checks over seven steps: `shell-stays-responsive-during-stalled-request` `probeMs` 1860 / `heartbeats` 9 / `maxGapMs` 258, `backup-restore-round-trips-native-support-backup` (the archive restore, now under the lighting lock), `database-backup-verifies-and-restores-after-restart` at schema 6 with its `replaced` and `pre-restore` files (the staged restore, with the checkpoint in front of it), `corrupt-db-restores-from-database-backup` at generation 2 (the restore whose replaced file is junk, which by the code takes the checkpoint's best-effort branch — the lane keeps no engine log to show the WARN line), `engine-crash-reaches-recovery-and-restarts` `detectMs` 203 / `restartMs` 1221, `second-instance-is-refused` by the plugin in 101 ms, `bridge-port-taken-reads-as-health-attention` on port 54055 with `attention` / `degraded`. Production is what it was, to the byte and the second: shell `sse-exed-tauri-shell.exe` 9,823,232 bytes 2026-09-04 16:44:46, engine `studio-control-engine.exe` 8,801,792 bytes 2026-09-10 16:08:06; no `windows.production-keep` left; 38201 free and no engine process afterwards. `npm run dev:check` green before the commit.

Operator hands: Appendix B item 5, walked together with S11 (it needs `SSE_SAFE_START`): with the rig in a state that may be lit, switch Preview on in Lighting and press the deck's intensity, toggle and recall keys — the rig must not move, the deck's displays must read `PREVIEW` on a third line, and the page's preview must show each press at once; switch Preview off and press again — the rig follows, and so does the page. Also the operator's, and not done here: **promoting a build from this branch to production.** The packaged app under `release\native\windows` is still the S2-era build (the correction under Slice 9); `npm run native:engine:build && npm run tauri:build && npm run native:package:win:local` replaces it, after which Appendix B items 1–5 are walked on that build; until then the running app has none of S3–S10. Notes for later slices: S11 — take the lighting lock around `lighting.output.setArmed` like any lighting mutation (its dispatcher arm does it) and let the armed flag ride the generation, so the hold reaches the wire within a tick; `held_outputs_send_nothing` needs the sACN destination injectable (`SACN_PORT` is a constant and `send_frame` builds the address itself) — inject the port, keep the packet path byte-identical; `record_event` from a deck key belongs inside `locked_light_action`, where the preview and the outcome are known (a staged change is not a change of the rig — decide whether it is recorded, and with which source); `app.rs` is 1,712 lines and `control_surface.rs` 1,202. S13 — the new deck tests cost the unit suite four to five seconds of disk commits on the workstation; if the suite's time starts to matter, a `cfg(test)` relaxation of `synchronous` is the lever, not fewer assertions. S15 — `docs/OPERATIONS.md` now says what a copy of the database by hand needs after a close that was not graceful.
