## Slice 11 — Audit trail + safe start

Status: landed 2026-09-17 (operator verification pending, Appendix B item 5; commit `9c45d36` `Readiness S11: action log with sources, held light outputs and safe start, schema 7`, pushed with the baseline refresh `4b89191` `Readiness S11 (baselines): Support plate with the Light outputs switch and Recent actions`; CI run 35261830154, on the baselines commit, recorded under "CI (result)" below by the follow-up commit `Readiness S11 (ledger): CI run on the branch recorded`, as in S0–S10; every S11 lane, the `dev-fixtures` build, the packaging lane with the production folder moved aside and `npm run dev:check` green on the workstation; the slice has hardware exposure — the hold on the light output, the Stream Deck's handlers — so it ends as `landed` and waits on Appendix B item 5, which covers S10 and S11 together and does not block S12). Closes F30 F31. Depends on S3 S10.

Scope (as landed): **Schema 7 (F30).** `E/storage.rs` (1,036 lines, was 994): `STORAGE_SCHEMA_VERSION = 7`; a new `if schema_version < 7` step creates `event_log(id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY, at, source, domain, action, target, detail)` and `event_log_at_idx` on `at DESC`; the v5 → v6 step is pinned to its own literal (`if schema_version < 6`, inserts 6, sets 6) — it was keyed to the constant, so with the constant at 7 a v5 database would have recorded 7 and never created the table, and a v6 database would have run the v6 palette seed a second time, which reads an empty palette list as unseeded and would have given an operator who deleted every palette the defaults back; `set_settings_owned_and(db, settings, |transaction| …)` lets a writer that already commits add rows to the same transaction. S3's pre-migration copy is taken as before (whenever 0 < version < the constant). **The action log (F30).** New `E/action_log.rs` (882; tests in `E/action_log/tests.rs`, 596): `ActionSource { Ui, Deck, Console, Watchdog, Launch }`, `ActionRecord`, `RecordedAction`, `insert_actions(transaction, rows)` (the insert and the prune in one transaction; the prune is by id — `DELETE … WHERE id <= MAX(id) - 5000` — because ids only ever leave from the bottom), `record_actions` / `record_actions_or_log` (a transaction of their own; a row that cannot be written is a `WARN` line, never a failed action), `list_recent_actions(db, limit)` (by id, newest first: the order the rows were written, which a clock step cannot disturb; the index on `at` is the design's and serves time-window reads), `ui_actions(method, params, result, staged)` and `deck_actions(path, action, reply)` — the two tables, pure functions — and `ui_method_class`, which puts every method of the contract in `RECORDED_UI_METHODS` (24) or `NOT_AN_ACTION_UI_METHODS` (57). **What a row is:** a discrete action that changed what a device receives — lights, groups and all lights on / off, a scene recall, a palette, identify / highlight / light solo, a fixture added, removed or repatched, lighting switched on / off, the bridge address (from the settings request or a probe that was given one), arming / holding the light outputs; every channel and output switch (mute, solo, 48 V, phase, pad, instrument, AutoSet, dim, mono, talkback), the EQ, low-cut, EQ-band and dynamics switches, the send switches, a Console mix recall, clear-all-solo, TotalMix control on / off and its address; an applied backup archive. **What is not:** a ride (a fader, a gain, an intensity, a colour temperature, the grand master, a Stream Deck dial detent), a change staged in the lighting preview (it never touched the rig — the engine has no "apply preview", a staged look reaches the rig only through a later recall, which is a row), a refused action (nothing reached a device; the last-action status and `engine.log` carry it), a selection, editor bookkeeping, planning. **Who knows the source — the entry points, not the functions they share:** `EngineApp::handle_request` (`E/app.rs` 1,752, was 1,712 — one hook after the dispatch, source `ui`; it keeps the params only for recorded methods, skips a refused reply and a recovery-mode engine, and reads the shared preview for the five preview-aware methods: only the IPC thread switches the preview, so what it reads is what the mutation saw), `handle_control_surface_http_action` (`E/control_surface.rs` 1,222, was 1,202 — source `deck`, from the route, the key and the reply, which already says `preview`; two replies grew for it: the lighting value keys' `light` carries `name`, and `talkOn` / `talkOff` carry `changed` (`E/control_surface_audio.rs` 1,331), because Companion repeats `talkOn` while the key is held), `audio::apply_console_activity` (`E/audio/console_link.rs` 491 — source `console`; `console_update_action` names the switch as the screen names it, `… at TotalMix: <name>`), the talkback watchdog (`E/audio/talkback.rs` 567 — `release_expired_hold` is what the loop calls per expired hold; source `watchdog`) and the bootstrap (source `launch`). **What a row costs:** the deck's row rides the transaction that stamps its last event and the console's rows ride the flush's own write (`persist_audio_state_with_actions`), so neither waits for the disk a second time — the flush runs on the metering thread; a screen action pays a second commit on the IPC thread; a row is on disk before the reply says ok, so nothing is lost at a hard kill beyond the moment between the action's commit and the row's. No writer thread: it would have put a process-wide thread into a 387-test binary, made the lanes wait, and traded the on-disk-before-ok property for a saving the measurement (below) does not need. **`recentEvents`.** `E/support.rs` (1,506, was 1,469): `SupportSnapshot.recent_events` — the newest fifty, newest first; empty with no query when `storage_ready` is false (`support.snapshot` is a recovery request and there is no database); a list that cannot be read is a `WARN` line and an empty list, never a Support surface that cannot list its backups. Recording raises no event. **Held light outputs (F31, D1).** New `E/lighting/output_arming.rs` (82): `app.lighting.output_armed` — `"false"` holds, anything else (above all a database that never had the key) is armed, so a default launch is what it was; `lighting_output_armed(settings)`, `set_lighting_output_armed` and its request; `LightingSnapshot.outputArmed` (ts-rs; `status` does not change — a hold is not a fault). New method `lighting.output.setArmed { armed }` through `dispatch_lighting_mutate`, so the lock and the render generation come with it; it raises `lighting.changed { reason: "output-armed-changed" }` (no new event). `E/lighting_sacn_output.rs` (852, was 556): the loop body is `SacnOutput { socket, port, active_bridge, universes, held }` with `tick(settings, log)`; the hold is applied there and not in the renderer — `read_lighting_sacn_output_state` and the DMX monitor keep answering what would be sent; held from the start of a launch, nothing is sent at all; armed → held while streaming takes the existing ineligible path (three stream-terminated packets per active universe, then silence — what a receiver expects); the destination port is a field (`SACN_PORT` for the engine) and nothing else about the packet path moved — `build_e131_data_packet`, the sequence numbers, the 800 ms keep-alive and the terminate path are byte for byte what they were. **Health.** `SubsystemState` has no "held" and gets none: the `sacn` entry stays `ok` with the detail `Light outputs held: …` — `attention` would turn the store's recovery state to `degraded` and put the Setup word and the lamps into their fault posture for something that works as asked. The registry announces changes of state only, so the output thread raises `app.changed { reason: "health" }` itself when its detail moves, and the bootstrap writes the held entry before the ready event goes out (found by the lane, below). **Safe start.** `E/bootstrap.rs` (1,641, was 1,316): `RuntimePaths.safe_start`, read with the other variables through the injected reader in `resolve_runtime_paths_from` (`SSE_SAFE_START`: empty, `0`, `false`, `off`, `no` mean no, anything else holds — an operator who wrote `yes` wanted a hold, and a stream is the one answer a safe start must not give); `hold_or_carry_light_outputs` runs right after `initialize_database`, before the bridge and the sACN thread exist, writes the flag and its rows in one transaction, and fails the start if it cannot (a safe start that could not hold must not go on to stream). The flag is persisted: the hold outlives the launch that made it, and only the switch arms. **A restore never arms a held rig.** The key sits under `app.lighting.`, which the archive restore deletes and rewrites as a whole: the export leaves it out, the delete spares it, the write skips one an archive names. A database restore replaces the whole file: `apply_pending_restore_carrying` reads the flag of the database being replaced before it moves it aside and the bootstrap writes it into the restored one (both directions — a restore never changes the switch); when the old database cannot be read (the recovery surface after a failed check) the restored database's value stands, and `SSE_SAFE_START=1` is the answer there — `docs/OPERATIONS.md` says so. The start that applied a restore leaves a `launch` row, because the restored file brought its own, older log. **Front end.** `EC/store/createShellStore.ts` (1,535) + `EC/types.ts`: `setLightingOutputArmed`; `EC/store/domainRefresh.ts` (150): `lighting.output.setArmed` → lighting + monitor + support, ahead of the `lighting.` prefix — the switch is a row in the list beside it; no other lighting request fetches support; `EC/transports/fixtureTransport.ts` (6,746, allowlisted): the double implements the method as the hardware link does — the flag, the row, `lighting.changed` — and its default lighting snapshot says `outputArmed`; `fixtures.json`: `outputArmed` beside every `reachable` (six), `recentEvents` on the three Setup fixtures (none, nine, three — all five sources across them); `check-fixtures.mjs` checks both. `FA/app/setup/components/SupportPlate.tsx` (237, was 185): **Light outputs: Armed / Held** in Workstation, a `Segmented` like Theme, with a readout that says what is sent — `the rig follows the app` / `nothing is sent to the rig` (amber) — and never what the room looks like. New `FA/app/setup/components/RecentActions.tsx` (102) + `.module.css`: a `Section` on the plate after About — the sentence, under it the local time, at the row's end who, as a `StatusBadge` (Screen / Stream Deck / Console neutral, Watchdog attention, Start-up info); the newest eight of the fifty (the plate has about 520 px between About and the red key at 2560×1440; below that the plate is in the drawer), the rest travel in the diagnostics export, which already carries the support snapshot; rows it cannot read are skipped. `FA/app/setup/SetupSupportPilot.tsx` (1,713, was 1,689): the wiring and the feedback sentence (`Light outputs held: nothing is sent to the rig until they are armed. The rig keeps its last look, or does what the bridge does without a source.`). `FA/app/shellData.ts` (765): `deriveLightingWorkspaceTone` — no bridge, then held, then unsaved — and `winsTies` on the workspace tone: the lamp took a workspace's word only when it was strictly worse than the health check's tone, so a bridge that has not been probed (attention) hid `held` (attention); held means nothing reaches the rig at all and is a state the operator has to remember to undo, so it wins the tie, and `unsaved` still does not. `FA/app/OperatorShell.tsx` uses it and hands the pilot `lightOutputsArmed`; `FA/app/tauriShellTestBridge.ts`: `setLightingOutputArmed`. Protocol: `native/protocol/v1.contract.json` (methods += `lighting.output.setArmed`; 81 methods), generated schema, `EC/generated/protocol.ts`, `LightingSnapshot.ts`; `native/protocol/v1.md`. Docs: `docs/OPERATIONS.md` (Holding the light outputs, Recent actions, Lights stop responding, the one-way upgrade under Data Safety, the held health entry), `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md` (the action log's rules, held outputs, the migration rule and the database hazard), `CHANGELOG.md`.

**Rescope:** twelve deviations, none silent, and the title stands because what landed is what it says. (1) The module is `action_log.rs`, not `events.rs`: `engine_events.rs` beside it is the event sender, and a second "events" file would be misread; the table keeps the design's `event_log` and the snapshot field its `recentEvents`. The design's `events::tests::record_list_prune` is `action_log::tests::record_list_prune`. (2) No `record_event` call threaded through the lighting and audio functions, and most of the design's `E/lighting/*` and `E/audio/*` did not change: those functions are shared by the screen and the deck, so the source is known only at the entry points — four hooks and two tables instead of an `ActionSource` parameter through some thirty signatures. (3) A fifth source, `launch` (the operator's word: Start-up). A safe start and an applied database restore change what the rig gets and nobody at a surface did them at that moment; a row with a wrong source is worse than none, and no row leaves the list with an "armed" that has no "held" before it. (4) What is recorded is wider than the design's list (above: identify and highlight, patch changes, addresses, the processing switches, an applied archive) and bounded by a rule rather than a list — rides, staged changes and refusals are out — with `every_contract_method_is_classified` as the guard that "every hardware-affecting action" stays true when a method is added. (5) `safe_start_env_holds` lives in `bootstrap::tests`, not `app::tests`: the variable is read and written there, through the injected reader; nothing calls `std::env::set_var`. (6) Held is a detail of an `ok` entry, not a registry state, and it is applied in the output loop rather than by returning `None` from the renderer — so the DMX monitor and the wire differ on purpose while held, and the snapshot, the lamp and OPERATIONS say so. (7) The restore rules (the archive leaves the key out; the database restore carries it) are not in the design; they follow from the key's prefix and from "a hold outlives the launch". (8) The v5 → v6 migration step pinned to its literal is a fix to older code the design did not list; without it this slice would have damaged every existing database's palette list on upgrade. (9) UI: the plate shows eight of the fifty, not fifty — fifty rows do not fit a surface where nothing scrolls, and the export carries them; the held state has no board of its own (81 boards, ratchets and baselines per platform unchanged in number) — it is measured by a UI-contract case that presses Held on the `setup-ready` board and runs the same census against that board's ratchet in all three themes; the lamp needed `winsTies`. (10) The engine's sentence for an audio recall is `Console mix recalled: <name>`: the source copy gate allows "snapshot" in a sentence that names the Console, but the UI contract's in-page scan allows the word inside the Console's own DOM only, and the Setup boards caught it (`copyHits: 1`); the Console's own word for what it saves is the mix (`Save current mix into …`), and `sentences_avoid_the_words_the_operator_never_reads` now forbids the word outright on the engine's side. (11) The output thread's own `app.changed`, and the bootstrap's synchronous held entry, are not in the design; the second was found by the setup-support lane (Validation). (12) Lanes beyond the design's list: `rust:clippy`, the `dev-fixtures` build, `frontend:typecheck`, the copy gate, the Storybook build, `tauri:visual:review`, the packaging lane with the production folder moved aside, `dev:check`; the safe-start scenario is two launches, the second without the variable, because the persistence is the part an operator would trip over. Decisions recorded, not deviations: a row's `at` is stamped by SQLite inside the insert; recording raises no `support.changed` (it would cost every action one more request) — the list moves when Setup is opened and with the switch, and OPERATIONS says it does not tick; `resetIntensity` / `resetCct` on the deck are rows (a press, a jump), the relative keys are not; `soloClearAll` with nothing soloed is not a row; a staged database restore is not a row (it is written into a file about to be replaced — the start that applies it writes one); at first contact the console reports every switch that differs from the saved state, and those are `console` rows (the lanes show them on the workstation, where their engines hear the real TotalMix).

Gates before: nothing recorded who did anything — the deck's last event was one overwritten key, the planning activity log is planning's; nothing could stop the light output short of disabling lighting or clearing the bridge; no test sent an sACN packet anywhere but the e131 round-trip, none ran the output loop's body at all; no test upgraded a v6 database, and `migration_snapshots_before_upgrading_v5` compared against the constant, so the step keyed to it could not be seen.

the v5 road — 6 and 7 both recorded, seeded once; the fresh road; table and index there on all three); `action_log::tests` — `record_list_prune` (sources, newest first, the caller's limit, 5,007 rows in one transaction leave exactly the newest 5,000), `ui_requests_leave_rows_for_discrete_changes_only`, `deck_keys_leave_rows_for_discrete_changes_only`, `every_contract_method_is_classified`, `sentences_avoid_the_words_the_operator_never_reads`; staged keys and a refused key leave nothing) and `deck_audio_keys_record_source_deck` (the mute; the press and the release of TALK, not the repeats); `app::tests` — `ui_requests_record_source_ui` (through `handle_request`, read back through `support.snapshot`; The first start of a build from this branch against the real app-data directory upgrades the operator's database to schema 7 (S3's `db-*-pre-migration.sqlite3` copy is written first). The S2-era build refuses a schema-7 database ("newer than this engine binary") and has no way to restore a database backup, so from that moment the way back is by hand — the older build, plus that pre-migration copy put in place of `studio-control.sqlite3` with the app closed (`-wal` and `-shm` beside it deleted) — and it loses everything recorded after the promotion. Promote when ready to stay; until then no engine from this branch may be started against the default app-data directory (`npm run tauri:dev`, a release exe or `studio-control-engine.exe` by hand need `SSE_APP_DATA_DIR` pointing at a scratch directory — `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md` says so). Notes for later slices: S12 — nothing from this slice. S15 — OPERATIONS now documents safe start, Recent actions and the one-way upgrade; `README.md` / `SECURITY.md` need nothing from this slice.

## Slice 12 — Supply chain + release CI

Status: verified 2026-09-18 (commit `d507356` `Readiness S12: supply-chain job on every push, release evidence on a tag, SBOMs, dependency fixes` plus the evidence follow-up `8b8d113` `Readiness S12 (evidence): the release build never caches npm downloads`, both pushed; every workstation lane and `npm run dev:check` green; the slice's last lane — the `v0.0.0-evidence` tag run of `release-evidence.yml` — green on Windows and macOS on the first attempt and again on the follow-up, and the branch's CI runs with the new `supply-chain` job green, recorded under "Release evidence (result)" and "CI (result)" below by the follow-up commit `Readiness S12 (ledger): CI run on the branch recorded`, as in S0–S11; no hardware exposure, so the slice goes straight to `verified`; Appendix B item 7, the certificates, blocks nothing). Closes F17, F03 (release). Depends on S0.

Scope (as landed): **The npm gate (F17).** New `scripts/check-npm-audit.mjs` (295 lines) + `scripts/npm-audit-allowlist.json` + the shared date rule `scripts/supply-chain-expiry.mjs` (54): `npm audit --json` for every lockfile in the repository, each scope with its own bar — `prod` (`--omit=dev`, what ships inside the app) fails from `moderate`, `all` (what builds, lints and tests it) from `high`, `tools` (the SBOM generator's own tree, `tools/sbom/`) from `high`. An advisory at or above its bar fails unless the allowlist names it — GitHub advisory id **and** package — with a reason of at least twenty characters and an `expires` day that is a real calendar day, has not passed, and lies at most 90 days out (`MAX_EXCEPTION_DAYS`); an entry with a problem fails by itself and covers nothing; a severity word the script does not know ranks above `critical`; a report that is not an `auditReportVersion: 2` report (offline, registry error) is exit 2, "could not run", never a pass; `--report=FILE --scope=…` reads a recorded report, which is how the tests stay off the network. **The allowlist starts empty, because the findings were fixed and not listed:** `npm update brace-expansion browserslist` moved seven packages, all of them only under `eslint` → `minimatch` and `eslint-plugin-react-hooks` → `@babel/core` (`npm ls`): `brace-expansion` 5.0.7 → 5.0.12, `browserslist` 4.28.2 → 4.29.0 and its data packages (`baseline-browser-mapping`, `caniuse-lite`, `electron-to-chromium`, `node-releases`, `update-browserslist-db`) — four high advisories and one moderate gone; what is left in the full tree is below the bar (4 moderate: `vitest` and `@vitest/mocker` GHSA-82fw-gwwq-j7x9, `qs` × 2; 2 low: `@babel/core`, `esbuild`) and the shipped tree is clean (0 of 29). **The Rust gate (F17).** New `native/deny.toml` (75): `[graph]` limited to the three triples that are built anywhere (`x86_64-pc-windows-msvc`, `aarch64-apple-darwin`, `x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu`) with `all-features`; advisories — a vulnerability or an `unsound` crate anywhere fails, `yanked` fails, an `unmaintained` notice fails for a direct dependency only (`unmaintained = "workspace"`: the six today are `proc-macro-error` under GTK and five `unic-*` crates under Tauri's `urlpattern`, none with an upgrade); licences — `MIT`, `Apache-2.0` and five more, each with the crates that need it named in the file (`Apache-2.0 WITH LLVM-exception`: target-lexicon; `BSD-3-Clause`: brotli, alloc-no-stdlib, alloc-stdlib; `MPL-2.0`: cssparser, cssparser-macros, selectors, dtoa-short, option-ext; `Unicode-3.0`: the eighteen ICU4X crates and unicode-ident; `Zlib`: foldhash) — written from `cargo deny list` (twelve identifiers) and cut to what the check needs, so the LGPL arm of `r-efi` and the 0BSD / CC0 / MIT-0 / Unlicense arms of dual-licensed crates are deliberately absent, and cargo-deny reports no unused allowance; bans — `wildcards = "deny"`, duplicates reported (Tauri's tree carries 21); sources — crates.io only, no git dependency. One ignored advisory: RUSTSEC-2024-0429 (glib 0.18 `VariantStrIter`, unsound; the fix is glib 0.20, which Tauri 2's GTK3 stack cannot take; Linux runners only, never in a bundle), written with `review by 2026-12-15`. New `scripts/check-deny-ignores.mjs` (122): cargo-deny keeps no time, so every `[advisories] ignore` entry must be one line `{ id = "…", reason = "… review by YYYY-MM-DD" }` under the same date rule; a line of the list it cannot read is a failure, never a skipped entry. **Fixes on the Rust side:** `cargo update -p plist` (plist 1.9.0 → 1.10.1, `quick-xml` 0.39.2 → 0.42.0 — RUSTSEC-2026-0194 and -0195 — and `base64` 0.23.1 added; `native/Cargo.lock` now has 500 packages) and `cargo update -p anyhow` (1.0.102 → 1.0.104, RUSTSEC-2026-0190, unsound). `native/Cargo.toml` `[workspace.package]` gains `license = "MIT"` (the repository's `LICENSE`) and `publish = false`, inherited by the three crates: without them the licence check reports all three as unlicensed and the wildcard ban refuses the version-less path dependency on `studio-control-protocol`. **CI.** `.github/workflows/dev-checks.yml` (298): ninth job `supply-chain` — `npm run supply-chain:npm`, `node scripts/check-deny-ignores.mjs`, then `cargo deny check` through `EmbarkStudios/cargo-deny-action` pinned to the commit behind v2.1.1 (`3c6349835b2b7b196a839186cb8b78e02f7b5f25`; Dependabot's github-actions updates move the pin) with `rust-version: 1.95.0`; each step reports even when another failed; nothing is installed or built, the three read lockfiles. **Release evidence (F03 release, F17, D2).** New `.github/workflows/release-evidence.yml` (210 with the evidence follow-up's `package-manager-cache: false`): `push.tags: ["v*"]` + `workflow_dispatch`, `permissions: contents: read`, matrix `windows-latest` / `macos-14`, no build cache and no third-party action (the toolchain comes from `native/rust-toolchain.toml` through rustup): `npm ci`, `npm run release:check` for a real tag only, `native:engine:build`, `tauri:build`, `native:package:<win|mac>:local`, the signing posture and the signing step, `native:checksums:<win|mac>:staged-write`, the manifest checked against the archive (`sha256sum -c` / `shasum -a 256 -c`), the SBOMs, a run summary (commit, toolchain, digest, signed or not), one artifact per runner — the bundle archive, `release/checksums/<target>/`, `release/sbom/<target>/` — kept 30 days. It creates no GitHub Release and uploads nowhere else. **SBOMs.** New `scripts/write-release-sboms.mjs` (200): three CycloneDX 1.5 files under `release/sbom/<target>/` — `…-frontend.cdx.json` (`cyclonedx-npm --omit dev`: 22 components), `…-engine.cdx.json` (38) and `…-shell.cdx.json` (239; `cargo cyclonedx --describe binaries --target <triple>`, so no GTK in a Windows SBOM); cargo-cyclonedx writes beside each crate's manifest and has no output directory, so its files are moved and never left in the tree (`.gitignore` names them as well); each file is read back and refused unless it is CycloneDX 1.5, describes the right component at `package.json`'s version and lists the one dependency that component cannot exist without (`react`, `rusqlite`, `tauri`). New `tools/sbom/` (`package.json` + its own `package-lock.json`, 176 packages, `@cyclonedx/cyclonedx-npm` 6.0.1 exact): installed with `npm ci --prefix tools/sbom --ignore-scripts` by the release workflow only. **Signing, wired and dormant (D2).** `scripts/native-sign-windows.mjs` (204) and `scripts/native-sign-macos.mjs` (145) gain `--bundle-only` — the shell, the engine and the bundle archive, and no installer; without the flag nothing changes; the Windows skip line now names its two variables. The workflow's signing step runs on every run with the secrets in that one step's environment: with none set the script prints why it skipped and exits 0, and the posture step writes `Signing: SKIPPED — … this bundle is UNSIGNED` into the log and the summary; with them set it signs, after a keychain import on macOS (`SSE_MACOS_SIGN_CERT_BASE64` / `_PASSWORD` — a runner has no keychain to find `SSE_MACOS_CODESIGN_IDENTITY` in) and a `signtool` lookup on Windows. `package.json`: `supply-chain:npm`, `supply-chain:cargo`, `supply-chain:check`, `native:sign:<mac|win>:bundle`, `native:sbom:tools:install`, `native:sbom:<mac|win>:write`. `.github/dependabot.yml`: an npm entry for `/tools/sbom`. Docs: `docs/RELEASE.md` (Release Evidence (CI), Supply Chain Checks, the runner's secrets under Optional Signing), `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md` (§4 Supply chain: what the gates are, take the fix first, never `npm audit fix`; the nine-job paragraph), `AGENTS.md` (command map, CI table), `docs/HANDOFF.md` (nine jobs), `scripts/README.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

**Rescope:** twelve deviations, none silent, and the title stands because what landed is what it says. (1) The npm gate reads every lockfile with its own bar instead of `npm audit --omit=dev` failing on high / critical alone. On inspection the shipped tree is clean and **all six of GitHub's open Dependabot alerts are development-scope** (`gh api …/dependabot/alerts`): the designed gate would have said "0" beside a high alert; the build tools run on the studio workstation, which is the production machine, and on the runner that builds the bundles; and with the full tree gated at high, a second look at the shipped tree is only worth having at a stricter bar, hence `moderate` there. (2) The allowlist starts empty, where the session prompt expected it non-empty: both highs had an upgrade inside their ranges, and an upgrade beats a listed exception. `npm audit fix` was **not** used: its dry run proposed 25 changes, among them `esbuild` 0.27.7 → 0.27.2 — a downgrade of the bundler that produces the shipped `dist`, to step out of an advisory range, against the `allowScripts` pin. The targeted update is proved harmless rather than argued: `frontend/app/dist` rebuilt after it is identical to the build from before it, 22 files by name and SHA-256. (3) The 90-day cap, the minimum reason and the id-and-package match are not in the design, which says only that entries carry an expiry date: without a cap, `2099-01-01` is a permanent mute; without the package, an entry for one package would cover the same advisory on another. (4) `@cyclonedx/cyclonedx-npm` is a locked dependency of `tools/sbom/`, not a root devDependency: as one it added 163 packages — one with a native install script, `libxmljs2` — to every `npm ci` on every job and on the workstation, to serve one step of a workflow that runs on tags. It keeps an integrity-pinned lockfile (an `npx` fetch would not), is audited and has its Dependabot entry. (5) `cargo deny check` runs all four checks (the design names three; `sources` is the one that refuses a git dependency), on three named triples, with `unmaintained` scoped to direct dependencies and `unsound` widened to the whole tree. (6) `scripts/check-deny-ignores.mjs` and the `review by` convention are not in the design; they give the Rust side's exceptions the date the design gives the npm side's. (7) `native/Cargo.lock` and the three crates' manifests are not in the design's file list: the two advisory fixes and the licence / publish metadata the checks need. (8) The release workflow restores no cache and uses no third-party action — a from-scratch build is the cleaner evidence and tags are rare — and it runs `release:check` for a real tag only: `resolveReleaseTag` reads `GITHUB_REF_NAME`, and `v0.0.0-evidence` is a valid tag by its pattern, so the rehearsal would have failed against `package.json` 2.2.1; a tag ending in `-evidence` is a rehearsal. The manifest self-check and the run summary are additions. (9) `--bundle-only`, the keychain import with its two further secrets, and the `signtool` lookup are not in the design. They exist because `native:sign:*:release` as written rebuilds and signs the QtIFW installer, which a runner cannot have: wired as designed, the step would have failed on the day the secrets arrived. **The signing path has never run — no certificate exists**; what is verified is the dormant path, on the workstation and on both runners, and that half a configuration fails instead of skipping. (10) `workflow_dispatch` is in the workflow, but GitHub starts a manual run only from a workflow file on the default branch, so until this branch is merged the tag is the only trigger; the session prompt asked for the dispatch so the evidence run would not depend on the tag, and on this branch it still does. (11) The SBOM script validates what it wrote and refuses an empty or foreign SBOM; three SBOMs, one per thing inside the bundle; CycloneDX 1.5 on both sides because cargo-cyclonedx 0.5.9 writes nothing newer. (12) Lanes beyond the design's list: `tauri:foundation`, the packaging lane with the production folder moved aside (package, dormant signing, checksums, manifest check, SBOMs, bridge), both qualification lanes (the shell's dependency tree moved), the `dist` hash comparison, fifteen neutralisations and the policy run against an export of HEAD, `dev:check`. Decisions recorded, not deviations: the supply-chain lanes are **not** part of `dev:check` — they need the network and they judge today's advisory databases, so they would change `dev:check`'s answer overnight; for the same reason no test judges an exception's date against today's, so a date can turn the `supply-chain` job red and never `scripts:test`; the below-the-bar findings were left alone (their fixes were part of the refused `npm audit fix`); `bans` stays light — no deny-list was invented; QtIFW installers stay workstation-built, as the design says.

Gates before: nothing looked at a dependency — no `npm audit`, no advisory, licence or source check for the 499 packages of `native/Cargo.lock`, Dependabot's alerts visible on GitHub only; a `v*` tag ran no workflow at all (`dev-checks.yml` triggers on branches); the two signing scripts had no test (`scripts/README.md`: accepted gap); nothing described what is inside a bundle.

Tests added (`scripts:test` 85 → 114; 110 pass, the 4 skipped are the ones skipped before): `scripts/check-npm-audit.test.mjs` (13; on the two `npm audit --json` reports recorded on the workstation on 2026-09-17 before any fix, `scripts/fixtures/npm-audit/recorded-2026-09-17-{all,prod}.json` — it never calls the registry): the eleven distinct advisories of the nine vulnerable packages, worst first; an unlisted high fails and the same report passes with all four listed, one left out is named; the two bars; an expired entry fails by itself, covers nothing, and fails even with nothing left to cover, the expiry day itself still counts; the date rule (day 90 yes, day 91 no, 2099 no, `2026-02-31` and six other non-days); what an entry needs; an entry covers its own package only; a report that is not one is `AuditReportError`; an unknown severity fails at every bar; the command line on recorded reports — exit 0, exit 1 naming the advisories, exit 1 on a 2020 expiry, exit 2 for a non-report, a missing allowlist and a bad flag; the committed allowlist's shape. `scripts/check-deny-ignores.test.mjs` (7): entries read with reason and date, both spellings of empty, a passed date and one too far out, an unreadable line is a failure (four forms), only `[advisories]` is read, the committed `deny.toml`, the command's exit codes. `scripts/write-release-sboms.test.mjs` (3): the plan (names, folder, the dependency each must list), the refusal rule (empty, nested, wrong version, wrong component, wrong spec, not CycloneDX), unknown targets. `scripts/native-sign.test.mjs` (6): both scripts, with and without `--bundle-only`, skip with exit 0 and name their variable; a blank value is no configuration; half a configuration fails and never prints "Skipping". **Each guard proved against the code without it** — twelve neutralisations from a script that keeps a copy, replaces exactly one needle, runs the test files, restores and asserts the needle is back (an expired date accepted; the cap gone; a non-report read as no advisories; an unknown severity below the bar; an entry covering any package; a broken entry still covering; an unreadable ignore line skipped; an ignore without a date; an SBOM without its must-list component; an SBOM of another component; Windows half-configuration skipping; the dormant macOS path exiting 1) — every one fails, and the restored tree passes. The policy itself the same way, through `cargo deny --config` on neutralised copies: without the glib ignore `advisories FAILED` (exit 1), without `MPL-2.0` `licenses FAILED` (5 rejected, exit 4), without the registry `sources FAILED` (380 crates, exit 8); and the finished policy against the tree as it was (`git archive HEAD native` into a scratch directory, the new `deny.toml` beside it): exit 7, `advisories FAILED, bans FAILED, licenses FAILED, sources ok` — the two `quick-xml` vulnerabilities, `anyhow`, the two wildcard bans, the three unlicensed crates. The npm gate against the tree as it was: exit 1, four `FAIL high` lines. Tests changed: none.

Lanes: `npm run supply-chain:check` (`cargo install cargo-deny --locked` once: 0.20.2, 78 s), `npm audit --omit=dev`, `npm run scripts:test`, `npm run lint`, `npm run rust:clippy`, `npm run native:test`, `npm run frontend:test`, `npm run tauri:foundation`, the packaging lane with the production folder moved aside, `npm run tauri:setup-support:qualify`, `npm run tauri:workspaces:qualify`, `npm run dev:check`; then the push, tag `v0.0.0-evidence` on the branch head, both runs watched, the tag deleted locally and on origin. Done when both jobs are green — the `supply-chain` job and the two `release-evidence` jobs — and the tag produced bundles + SHA256 manifests + SBOMs as artifacts.

Operator hands: Appendix B item 7 (certificates, D2) — it blocks nothing. When certificates exist, add as repository secrets: Windows — `SSE_WINDOWS_SIGN_CERT_BASE64` (the `.pfx`, base64), `SSE_WINDOWS_SIGN_CERT_PASSWORD`, optionally `SSE_WINDOWS_SIGN_TIMESTAMP_URL`; macOS — `SSE_MACOS_CODESIGN_IDENTITY`, `SSE_MACOS_SIGN_CERT_BASE64` (the Developer ID `.p12`, base64), `SSE_MACOS_SIGN_CERT_PASSWORD`, optionally `SSE_MACOS_NOTARY_APPLE_ID` / `_PASSWORD` / `_TEAM_ID`. Then push a rehearsal tag (`v0.0.0-evidence`) before any real one: that run is the signing path's first test, on both runners, and should be expected to need an iteration. Also the operator's: merging, after which `gh workflow run release-evidence.yml` works without a tag and GitHub closes the Dependabot alerts the lockfile updates answer (the `browserslist` high among them). Notes for later slices: S13 — `supply-chain` is already in the required-checks list the design gives S13; say beside it that the job reads today's advisory databases and can turn red on a push that changed nothing (the answer is an upgrade or a dated exception, the same day), and that the "green on 5 consecutive runs" rule is about the e2e project. The first dates to come due are `review by 2026-12-15` in `native/deny.toml` and whatever entries the allowlist holds by then. S14 — nothing from this slice. S15 / operator — (a) the packaged bundle carries the **debug-profile engine**: `scripts/native-package.mjs` copies `native/target/debug/studio-control-engine.exe`, as it always has, so the evidence bundle does too; whether the engine should ship from the release profile is a release decision this slice did not take. (b) `npm run clean` and `clean:local` delete `release/` whole (`scripts/clean.mjs` lists it as a target), and on this workstation `release\native\windows` **is** the production app; `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md` §4a recommends the command without saying so. Not run in this session, not S12's to change; a task chip was raised 2026-09-18 and the session prompt should carry the warning until it is fixed. **Fixed the same day, outside the slice scheme** (commit `chore(scripts): clean keeps a packaged app under release/native unless --include-release` — not a slice, no finding, no `Readiness` title): both commands keep `release/native` whenever a packaged shell or engine executable is inside it and say what they kept, `--include-release` removes it and refuses while a process runs from the folder, `--dry-run` removes nothing; `scripts/clean.test.mjs`, and `scripts:test` is 127 from that commit on (123 pass, the same 4 skipped). `scripts/native-package.mjs` is unchanged: the packaging procedure with the production folder moved aside stays as written. (c) `README.md` and `SECURITY.md` say nothing yet about the supply-chain job or the evidence workflow; `docs/RELEASE.md`, `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md`, `AGENTS.md` and `docs/HANDOFF.md` do.

Release evidence (result, 2026-09-18 local time; the runners' clocks read 2026-09-17 22:18–22:48 UTC): run 35281355605 (https://github.com/Fikarn/sse-exed-studio-control/actions/runs/35281355605), event `push`, ref `refs/tags/v0.0.0-evidence` at `d507356` — **both jobs green on the first attempt**, the macOS one never having run anywhere before (there is no Mac here). `bundle (macos)`, macos-14 (image 20260831.0302), 3.6 minutes: rustc 1.95.0 from `native/rust-toolchain.toml` through rustup, host `aarch64-apple-darwin`; a cold build — 398 crates compiled, the debug engine in 18.7 s and the release shell in 1 min 43 s, `plist` 1.10.1 and `quick-xml` 0.42.0 among them; `native:package:mac:local` assembled the `.app`, signed it ad hoc and archived it; the posture step wrote `Signing: SKIPPED — the secret SSE_MACOS_CODESIGN_IDENTITY is not set, so this bundle is UNSIGNED. Signing stays dormant until certificates exist (decision D2; docs/RELEASE.md, Release evidence).` into the log and the summary, the keychain and `signtool` steps were skipped, and the signing step itself ran and printed `Skipping native macOS signing because SSE_MACOS_CODESIGN_IDENTITY is not configured.` with exit 0 — the dormant path, on the real runner; manifest `6a22489b72d72158e215d9781cd5c3fe67775c6524722ff9db09b6b5be838e78  SSE-ExEd-Studio-Control-Native-macOS.zip`, `shasum -a 256 -c`: `OK`; `cargo-cyclonedx` 0.5.9 installed in 53 s; three CycloneDX 1.5 SBOMs with 22 / 39 / 237 components (web assets / engine / shell); artifact `release-evidence-macos`: 5 files, 7,745,972 bytes, kept until 2026-10-17. `bundle (windows)`, windows-latest, 9.1 minutes: host `x86_64-pc-windows-msvc`; cold — 411 crates, the engine in 34.4 s, the release shell in 4 min 25 s; `Signing: SKIPPED — the secret SSE_WINDOWS_SIGN_CERT_BASE64 is not set, …` and `Skipping native Windows signing because no signing certificate is configured (SSE_WINDOWS_SIGN_CERT_PATH or SSE_WINDOWS_SIGN_CERT_BASE64).`; manifest `61cc0582a7058ba0ad3b01890174ed3bcfa9cf4f6e5ef3c04fbbdbeae56b7408  SSE-ExEd-Studio-Control-Native-windows.zip`, `sha256sum -c`: `OK`; `cargo-cyclonedx` in 2 min 32 s; SBOMs 22 / 38 / 239 — the workstation's counts; artifact `release-evidence-windows`: 5 files, 6,588,065 bytes. On both: `GITHUB_TOKEN Permissions` `Contents: read`, `Metadata: read` and nothing else; `release:check` skipped, as a rehearsal tag should; no cache restored or saved; no Release exists for the tag (nothing could have made one). **Reading the log found one thing:** setup-node runs with its default `package-manager-cache: true`. It cached nothing — the action caches by itself only when `package.json` names a `packageManager`, and ours does not; the log has no restore or save line — but "no cache" should hold by construction, so `8b8d113` `Readiness S12 (evidence): the release build never caches npm downloads` sets it to `false`. The tag was moved to that commit and the lane run again on the final tree: run 35282765635 (https://github.com/Fikarn/sse-exed-studio-control/actions/runs/35282765635), ref `refs/tags/v0.0.0-evidence` at `8b8d113` — both jobs green again, with `package-manager-cache: false` among the step's inputs and no cache line in either log: `bundle (macos)` 5.4 minutes (the release shell in 2 min 40 s), manifest `9393ec3ba9d7182855e2dd42d7c00a9a0d4e0ae8233df448baf12ac4f336002f`, `OK`, SBOMs 22 / 39 / 237, artifact 7,745,965 bytes; `bundle (windows)` 10.2 minutes (the release shell in 5 min 11 s), manifest `6e54723ffaa8e8510c8cf7947fc10f2b7ef15389b5b656749b06ed3d0fb616e9`, `OK`, SBOMs 22 / 38 / 239, artifact 6,588,120 bytes; the same two skip lines on each. The archive digests differ between the two runs although only a workflow file changed between the two commits: the bundle is not bit-for-bit reproducible (the archives carry file times, at least), so a manifest identifies one run's archive and is not a reproducibility claim. Afterwards the tag `v0.0.0-evidence` was deleted on origin and locally (`git ls-remote --tags origin "v0.0.0*"` and `git tag -l "v0.0.0*"` both empty). Not exercised: `workflow_dispatch` (Rescope 10 — GitHub starts a manual run only from a workflow on the default branch) and the signing path itself (Rescope 9 — no certificate exists).
