The failed titles were not counted but diffed against the S11 run's list (35261830154; that list regenerated from the S11 job's own log, 93 = 52 + 39 + 2, as recorded there): the two sets are identical, nothing only in one of them — the 91 linux snapshot cases (52 `visual-review.spec.ts` + 39 `storybook.spec.ts`), the constant `audio-arm-countdown.spec.ts:45` dwell case and the S13-list case `audio.spec.ts:1054`, whose error block was extracted from both logs and is identical (the same locator, line 1093, `data-armed` expected `true` and received `false` after five seconds). No new title. Run 35282760471 (https://github.com/Fikarn/sse-exed-studio-control/actions/runs/35282760471) on `8b8d113`, 13.4 minutes: every job green on the first attempt again — `supply-chain` (0.9) with the same three verdicts, `rust` (3.8), `qualification` (11.1: the setup-support step 385 s, the workspace step 91 s) — but `frontend-e2e`, 92 failed / 330 passed of 422: diffed against the same S11 list, the one difference is that `audio.spec.ts:1054` passed this time, which leaves the 91 snapshot cases and the dwell case, the tightest shape this stack has had; nothing is only in the new list. Nothing is pending on S12.

## Slice 13 — Test posture

Status: verified 2026-09-18 (no hardware exposure; commit `cf681ad` `Readiness S13: blocking e2e with a dated quarantine, coverage floors, property tests, five CI flakes fixed at the cause` with the baseline refresh `ab581e4` `Readiness S13 (baselines): 91 linux captures from the branch's runs, two Storybook audio shells that were one solid mask colour`, made from one validated tree and pushed together; CI run 35337703363 recorded under "CI (result)" below by the follow-up `Readiness S13 (ledger): CI run on the branch recorded`). Every S13 lane and `npm run dev:check` are green on the workstation, and the identical tree was green on all ten CI jobs on a rehearsal branch before it landed — `frontend-e2e` included, for the first time on this stack. The slice's own Done criterion — the default e2e project green on five consecutive runs of the final tree — could only be met after the push, and was: five executions of the `frontend-e2e` job on `ab581e4`, 419 passed each time, after the rehearsal run's. Closes F25 and the e2e half of F03.

Scope (as landed): **The premise, checked first.** The design's Done — "default e2e blocking and green on 5 consecutive runs" — could not be met by a quarantine project alone: `frontend-e2e` had been red on every one of the branch's thirty runs (34459772849 … 35327014360; each job log read, the failed titles tabulated by `ci_history.py`) for 91 snapshot cases that are stale or missing linux baselines (52 visual-review + 39 Storybook; the same 91 titles on all thirty, compared by title), plus five behaviour titles: `audio-arm-countdown.spec.ts:45` on 30 of 30, `audio.spec.ts:1054` on 19, `audio-talkback.spec.ts:64` on 6, `lighting.spec.ts:721` on 2, `audio-arm-countdown.spec.ts:68` on 1 — and `audio.spec.ts:702` once, on the first attempt of run 34514042594. The two cases the design names first, `audio-render-budget.spec.ts` and the `shell.spec.ts` case, failed on **none**. So the slice did three things the design did not list (Rescope 1–3): it refreshed the linux baselines itself, it found the cause of each of the five and fixed the tests, and it kept the quarantine for what is left — assertions that are wall-clock measurements. **The five causes (each proved from the run's `trace.zip`, then reproduced on the workstation by throttling the page's CPU over CDP, `Emulation.setCPUThrottlingRate`).** (a) `:45` and `:1054` are one defect: "a second click inside the 350 ms arm dwell" was clicked in real time, and on the runner the first click takes 1,479 ms, the `expect` between them 240 ms and the second click 754 ms (trace of 35327014360) — the press lands outside the dwell and applies, which is the product doing what it should. Unthrottled the two clicks are 115 ms apart and the case passes; at rate 6 they are 1,451 ms apart and it fails with CI's error verbatim. New `tests/helpers/pageClock.ts` (`pausePageClock`): the page's clock is stopped for the two presses and moved past the dwell with `page.clock.fastForward` for the confirm, so inside and outside are true by construction — the rewritten body passes at rate 8 (566 ms of real time between the clicks) and rate 20 (1,661 ms). (b) `:64` and `:68` are one defect: `data-testid="audio-workspace"` is also on the "Loading the console…" surface, where the cluster is empty and nothing listens for `T` or the snapshot keys; the focusing click landed there, the key went to nobody, and a held key is not sent twice (trace of 35263526586: `T` down 6 ms after the click returned, the Talkback key absent from every DOM snapshot until after it; at rate 8 and 20 the original fails the same way with "talkback key present at key-down: 0"). They now wait for the control the key belongs to. (c) `:721` read the rotation field once, straight after the pointer came up; the field shows the fixture's stored rotation, which moves when the reply to the drag's one commit has been fetched. It now waits for the field to leave 0. No product source changed for any of the five; `frontend/app/dist` is byte-identical before and after the slice (22 files by SHA-256). **The quarantine (F25, F03).** `frontend/app/playwright.config.ts` has two projects built from `frontend/app/tests/quarantine.json`: `default` (`grepInvert`, 419 cases) and `quarantine` (`grep`, 3 cases, `workers: 1`, `retries: 2`, its own `test-results-quarantine`); 419 + 3 = 422, checked with `--list`. The list is the membership — no tag in a spec — and holds `audio-render-budget.spec.ts` (both cases: render counts in fixed 500 ms / 1.5 s windows) and `audio.spec.ts` "marks simulated audio metering as test-stage movement" (5 s of real time for four seeded meters to read as more than one level), each with its reason and where it was seen, one rule, and one exit date, **2026-10-31**. New `scripts/check-playwright-quarantine.mjs` (185 lines; `npm run frontend:playwright:quarantine:check`): every entry names exactly one existing test, says why and where; `visual-review.spec.ts`, `storybook.spec.ts` and `ui-contract.spec.ts` can never be listed; at most six cases; the exit date is a calendar day at most 90 days out (`scripts/supply-chain-expiry.mjs`, S12's rule) and the check fails from the day after it. `scripts:test` runs all of it except the date's distance from today; the CI job judges the date too. **CI.** `dev-checks.yml` (369 lines, ten jobs): `frontend-e2e` checks the list, runs `frontend:playwright:test:blocking` (both builds, `--project=default`) and fails on it, then `frontend:playwright:test:quarantine` with `continue-on-error`, a job-summary line and a warning annotation when it is not green, and uploads a third artifact, `playwright-quarantine`; `frontend-test` adds `frontend:test:coverage`; new job `rust-coverage`. `npm run frontend:playwright:test` still runs both projects — the workstation lane is unchanged. **Coverage floors (F25).** v8 coverage in the Vitest configs of the app, the design system and the engine client (every source file counts, loaded by a test or not; `text-summary` + `json-summary` into an ignored `coverage/`), floors = measured − 2 points, `npm run frontend:test:coverage`, added to `dev:check`. Measured on the workstation and, identical to the last statement, on the runner — statements / branches / functions / lines: app 8.75 / 7.79 / 8.01 / 8.89 % (789 of 9,016 statements) → floors 6.75 / 5.79 / 6.01 / 6.89; design system 52.18 / 56.85 / 53.38 / 54.32 → 50.18 / 54.85 / 51.38 / 52.32; engine client 41.78 / 39.24 / 47.06 / 42.63 → 39.78 / 37.24 / 45.06 / 40.63. The app's figure is what it is: its behaviour is covered by the 422 Playwright cases, and the floor is a ratchet, not a claim. Rust: `npm run rust:coverage` = `cargo llvm-cov --workspace --summary-only --fail-under-lines 78.1`; the runner measured 80.10 % of 34,810 lines (regions 79.17 %, functions 69.53 %) and 80.07 % on the next run — the figure moves by a few hundredths with thread timing — and the workstation 80.11 %; the floor is the runner's 80.10 − 2. **Property tests (F25).** `proptest` 1.11.0 as an engine dev-dependency (`default-features = false, features = ["std"]`; nine crates, `native/Cargo.lock` 500 → 509; `supply-chain:cargo` green with no new licence allowance). `E/control_surface_http/fuzz.rs` (289 lines, seven properties): `read_http_request_never_panics` (arbitrary and request-shaped bytes, handed over in arbitrary pieces: no panic, a bounded read, a sound request when one is returned), `_stops_reading_at_its_limits` (an endless client), `_returns_what_was_sent`, `_refuses_a_body_over_the_cap` (every length up to `usize::MAX`, refused from the headers alone), `_takes_a_body_at_the_cap`, `query_parameter_never_panics`, `query_parameter_decodes_what_was_encoded`. `E/rme_totalmix_osc/fuzz.rs` (282 lines, four): `osc_decode_never_panics` (arbitrary datagrams and real packets with bytes damaged, through `rosc`, the meter state for each bus and a console link — values of their own, never the process-wide ones), `osc_packets_never_panic`, `accepted_classic_levels_are_levels`, `accepted_global_levels_are_levels`. No case opens a socket. **What the property tests found.** A NaN from the wire was taken as a meter level in both namespaces (`/1/level1LeftVal [NaN]`, `/level/in/0 [NaN]`; `NaN.clamp(0, 1)` is NaN and `"nan".parse::<f64>()` succeeds) and would have reached the meters as `null`. `E/rme_totalmix_osc.rs`: `is_level_dbfs` — a level is a finite number or −∞ dBFS — in `parse_global_level` and the display path, and a finite-only fader-scale value. TotalMix does not send NaN, so nothing changes on the desk; the seeds are committed under `native/rust-engine/proptest-regressions/`. **What inspecting the baselines found.** The two Storybook audio shell stories were captured as one solid mask colour and compared equal to it on every platform: `storybook.spec.ts` still masked the full-workspace `audio-meter-canvas`, which `visual-review.spec.ts` had stopped doing. One definition now, `tests/helpers/liveAudioMasks.ts` (moved from `visual-review.spec.ts` unchanged); the two win32 captures were refreshed on the workstation (16 of 16 on `--repeat-each 8`) and the two linux ones came from the rehearsal run. No other capture moved.

**Rescope:** thirteen deviations, none silent, and the title stands because what landed is what it says. (1) The slice refreshed the 91 linux baselines itself. The design put that under the operator's pull request ("linux from the CI artifact — needs a PR run", Appendix B item 6); since S0 a branch push produces the artifact, this ledger's own procedure already names it as the source, "linux baselines stale" is part of F03's text, and without it the job could not be green and so could not be made blocking. Item 6 keeps the pull request and the required checks. (2) The quarantine holds other cases than the design's three. `audio.spec.ts:1054` is fixed, not listed; the `shell.spec.ts` case (`:95`, the command palette's focus) stays in `default` untouched — it has never failed on CI, and the one mechanism that looked likely (the tabs are `disabled` during start-up, so `focus()` would be a no-op) did **not** reproduce at rate 8 or 20, so there was nothing to fix and no evidence to list it on; `audio-render-budget.spec.ts` is listed as designed, though it has never failed on CI either, because its assertion is a wall-clock render budget (`docs/DEVELOPMENT.md` §2c had it flaking under three workers on the workstation); `audio.spec.ts:702` is listed on the one CI failure it has. (3) Five cases were fixed at the cause instead of quarantined, because each had one; the rule in `quarantine.json` says so for the next one. (4) The exit date is enforced, not only recorded: a listed case blocks nothing, so the list is a file with a guard — reasons, no gates, a cap of six, a date that turns the job red — on S12's expiring-exception pattern. That is more than the design's "an exit date recorded in the ledger", and it means `frontend-e2e` can turn red on 2026-11-01 on a push that changed nothing; the answer is to fix or delete the case, or to move the date with every reason restated. (5) The tokens package (its one test reads CSS: 0 of 24 statements) and shared-graphics (no tests) have no floor; the floors are in `dev:check` as well as in CI, which the design did not say. (6) `llvm-tools-preview` is installed by the `rust-coverage` job and once by hand on a workstation, not added to `native/rust-toolchain.toml`: there every other Rust job and both release runners would download it for nothing. `cargo-llvm-cov` is `cargo install --locked --version 0.9.1`, no third-party action. The floor is the runner's figure, not the workstation's, because the runner is where it is enforced. (7) The HTTP properties are in `E/control_surface_http/fuzz.rs` (S2 moved the reader out of `control_surface.rs`), there are eleven properties rather than the three named, and `proptest` is built without its default features (no process-forking runner, none of its crates). (8) An engine change the design did not list: the NaN level. (9) A test defect the design did not list: the Storybook mask, with two win32 baselines refreshed. (10) A rehearsal branch, `rehearsal/s13-test-posture`, was pushed twice and deleted (runs 35335389634 — cancelled by the second push with every job finished but `qualification` — and 35336198990): only the runner can say what its Rust coverage is, whether refreshed linux baselines hold on a fresh machine and what the two Storybook captures look like under the corrected mask, and a slice commit that guessed would have needed follow-ups for all three. S12's rehearsal tag is the precedent. (11) "Green on 5 consecutive runs" is counted as five consecutive executions of the `frontend-e2e` job on the final tree — the rehearsal run, the push, and re-runs of the job — not as five pushes, which would have taken four empty commits; they are listed under "CI (result)". (12) The optional vitest bump was not taken: `@vitest/coverage-v8` must move with it, and the two moderates stay below the bar as S12 left them. (13) `read_log_tail_scans_only_the_tail_of_a_large_log` stays opt-in and was run by hand (below), not wired into a lane — there is no workstation lane it belongs to, and AGENTS.md says not to invent one.

Gates before: `frontend-e2e` had never been green on this stack and was required by nothing, so a red run carried no information — a real regression in any of the 419 behaviour and contract cases would have looked like every other run; two Storybook snapshots asserted nothing on any platform; no coverage was measured anywhere (the `@vitest/coverage-v8` devDependency was unused); the bridge's request reader and the OSC ingest were tested by hand-written examples only, and the NaN path by none; nothing distinguished a case that depends on the runner's speed from one that does not.

Tests added: the eleven properties above (`native:test` 416 → 428 passed / 1 ignored: tauri-shell 20, engine 387 → 399 + 1, `contract.rs` 4, `end_to_end.rs` 5; the engine suite still takes ~8 s on the workstation, 17 s on the runner under the instrumented build) and `rme_totalmix_osc::tests::a_level_that_is_not_a_number_is_not_a_level`; `scripts/check-playwright-quarantine.test.mjs` (15; `scripts:test` 127 → 142, 138 pass, the same 4 skipped); the coverage floors. **Each guard was proved against a sabotaged copy** (keep a copy, replace exactly one needle, run, expect red, restore, assert byte-identical): six in the engine — header cap removed, body cap removed, body not cut at `Content-Length`, percent decoding broken, NaN taken as a fader-scale level, any dBFS taken as a level. The first pass caught four of six, and the two misses were real gaps: the request-shaped generator rarely builds a _valid_ request with a hostile length, so the body cap had no property of its own (added: `_refuses_a_body_over_the_cap`), and the level properties asked the engine's own `is_level_dbfs` what a level is, which cannot fail (they now state the rule themselves). Second pass: six of six. Seven in the quarantine check (gates listable, no cap, an entry matching no test, matching two, the date never judged, a reason optional, `test.skip(` counted): seven of seven. The floors: a floor raised above the measurement (`--coverage.thresholds.lines=60` on the design system: exit 1, "54.32% does not meet global threshold (60%)") and coverage lowered on purpose (`createShellStore.test.ts` moved aside: engine client 2.96 % lines, exit 1 on all four floors; restored, `git hash-object` equal to HEAD's blob).

Tests changed (old assertion → new → why): `audio-arm-countdown.spec.ts` "a second click inside the dwell…" and `audio.spec.ts` "aligns audio input hardware controls…" — the second click followed the first in real time → the page's clock is stopped for the two presses and moved past the dwell for the confirm → a CI runner's clicks are over a second apart; what is checked is unchanged. `audio-talkback.spec.ts` "holding T talks…" and `audio-arm-countdown.spec.ts` "a held Shift+digit…" — the focusing click went straight after `page.goto` → it waits for the Talkback key to be enabled / the snapshot tile to be visible → the workspace's test id is also on the loading surface. `lighting.spec.ts` "rotates the selected fixture…" — one read of the rotation field after `mouse.up()` → waits for the field to leave 0 first → the field follows the commit's reply. `storybook.spec.ts` — its own mask list with `audio-meter-canvas` → the shared `helpers/liveAudioMasks.ts` → the old list blanked the whole page. `visual-review.spec.ts` — its mask list moved to the helper, unchanged.

Lanes: `npm run frontend:test:coverage`, `npm run rust:coverage` (`cargo install cargo-llvm-cov --locked`: 0.9.1, 43 s; `rustup component add llvm-tools-preview` from `native/`), `npm run frontend:playwright:test`, `npm run supply-chain:cargo`, `npm run scripts:test`, `npm run native:test`, `npm run dev:check`, CI.

Validation (workstation, 2026-09-18; the production app closed throughout, ports 38201 / 4173 / 6007 free before every Playwright run, no qualification lane run): baseline before editing — `rust:clippy` clean, `native:test` 416 / 1 ignored, `frontend:test` 325. On the final tree: `npm run dev:check` green (format, lint, `scripts:test` 142 = 138 + 4 skipped, file health, rustfmt, clippy, protocol, typecheck, `frontend:test` 325 = app 90 + design system 175 + engine client 45 + tokens 15, `frontend:test:coverage` at the figures above, `native:check`, `native:test` 428 / 1 ignored); `npm run frontend:playwright:test` **422 passed** in 2.9 min with both projects and no retry used — both lanes run a second time, with the same results, after the last edit to anything a lane reads (the evidence text in `quarantine.json`); the rewritten cases 36 of 36 on `--repeat-each 6` (six titles matched the filter); `npm run supply-chain:cargo` "advisories ok, bans ok, licenses ok, sources ok"; `package-lock.json` untouched, so `supply-chain:npm` had nothing new to read; `npm run rust:coverage` 80.11 % on the second try — the first failed on `audio::tests_console_link::recall_pushes_the_snapshot_and_the_console_confirms_it` (`main_after.dim`, the recall itself fully confirmed), a threaded test with two fixed 500 ms sleeps, while a commit hook and a push ran beside the instrumented build; it passed in every other run of the day, this slice does not touch it, and it joins `console_pull_that_never_goes_quiet_is_incomplete` as a Rust-side timing case seen once (Notes). `SSE_ENGINE_TEST_LARGE_LOG=1 … read_log_tail_scans_only_the_tail_of_a_large_log`: the tail of a 100 MB log read in 4.08 ms (S8's criterion: under 50 ms). Rehearsal, the same tree on the runner: run 35335389634 — `frontend-e2e` 417 passed / 2 failed, the two being the Storybook audio shells whose linux captures did not exist yet, quarantine 3 passed; all five rewritten cases green on Linux; run 35336198990 with those two captures and the floor — **all ten jobs green**, `frontend-e2e` 419 passed (6.5 min) + quarantine 3 passed (8.8 s), `rust-coverage` 1.9 min warm (4.8 min cold), `rust` 2.4 min, `qualification` green.

Baselines: `Readiness S13 (baselines)` — 91 linux + 2 win32. The 89 came from run 35327014360's `playwright-test-results` (227 MB; `235241c`, whose front-end is the final tree's: no front-end source changed), the two Storybook audio shells from the rehearsal run 35335389634 (9 MB). Mapped from the job log's `Expected:` / `Received:` pairs — long snapshot names are cut and hashed in the results folder, so the file name alone does not say which baseline it is; 73 were stale and 18 had never existed, and the repository now carries 93 linux beside 93 win32 (darwin 75, pending the macOS host). **Every capture was inspected:** compared with its win32 sibling — the board this workstation has validated — after an 8× box reduction that averages font anti-aliasing out and leaves anything moved, missing or different (mean difference ≤ 2.6 of 255 for all but four: `setup-ready-1280x800` 9.7 and `audio-populated` at 1600 / 1728 / 1920 about 5, each a line that wraps one word earlier under Linux's font metrics, at fallback viewports), and looked at on sixteen contact sheets plus side-by-sides of the outliers. That inspection is what found the two solid-colour captures.

Operator hands: Appendix B item 6 — the pull request and the required checks (D4). The list: `format-protocol`, `lint`, `frontend-typecheck`, `frontend-test`, `frontend-e2e`, `rust`, `tauri-foundation`, `qualification`, `supply-chain`, and the tenth this slice added, `rust-coverage`. Three of them can turn red on a push that changed nothing, by design: `supply-chain` reads today's advisory databases (the answer is an upgrade or a dated exception the same day — `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md` §4 Supply chain; the first date to come due is `review by 2026-12-15` in `native/deny.toml`), and `frontend-e2e` fails from **2026-11-01** until the three quarantined cases are back in the default project or gone. `frontend-e2e` is blocking on its `default` project only; its quarantine step never fails the job. The linux baseline refresh that item 6 used to ask for is done. (Correction, 2026-09-23: Slice 15 emptied the quarantine, so only `supply-chain` can turn red on a date now. `frontend-e2e` has no date. The operator decided on 2026-09-23 to require all ten checks — After the program.)

Notes for later slices: S14 — the split moves source, so every capture should hold; if one moves on linux, the refresh recipe is under "Baseline refresh procedure" (take the pairs from the job log). Lazy-loaded workspaces lengthen the "Loading…" window that `:64` and `:68` fell into: a spec that sends a key or a click after `openFixture` must first wait for the control it is aimed at, and `data-testid="audio-workspace"` is on the loading surface too — worth a different id there. The quarantine's exit date falls inside S14 / S15: `audio-render-budget.spec.ts` needs a settle predicate instead of its 500 ms window, `audio.spec.ts:702` a page clock driving the fixture's metering interval. S15 / operator — two Rust tests wait on the wall clock in threads and have each failed once under load (`console_pull_that_never_goes_quiet_is_incomplete` on a runner, `recall_pushes_the_snapshot_and_the_console_confirms_it` on the workstation under the instrumented build); `rust` and `rust-coverage` both run them, so a required-check failure there is a `gh run rerun --failed` until their sleeps become waits on the fake console's state. `README.md` and `SECURITY.md` say nothing about coverage or the quarantine; `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md` §4, `AGENTS.md` and `docs/HANDOFF.md` do.

CI (result, 2026-09-18): run 35337703363 (https://github.com/Fikarn/sse-exed-studio-control/actions/runs/35337703363), event `push`, sha `ab581e4` (the slice commit and the baselines commit pushed together, one run), created within seconds of the push; 13.5 minutes end to end; ten jobs. **Every job green on the first attempt** — the first run on this branch of which that is true: `lint` (23 s), `frontend-typecheck` (33 s), `supply-chain` (52 s; "advisories ok, bans ok, licenses ok, sources ok" with `proptest`'s nine crates in the lock, the npm gate with nothing new to read), `frontend-test` (57 s: 325 tests, then `frontend:test:coverage` at the workstation's figures to the last statement — app 8.75 / 7.79 / 8.01 / 8.89 %, design system 52.18 / 56.85 / 53.38 / 54.32, engine client 41.78 / 39.24 / 47.06 / 42.63 — so v8 coverage of these suites does not depend on the platform), `format-protocol` (1.2 min; `scripts:test` with the quarantine check's fifteen), `tauri-foundation` (2.5 min), `rust` (2.8 min with the lockfile moved: 20 + 399 / 1 ignored + 4 + 5, the engine suite 15.85 s, the eleven properties among them), `rust-coverage` (4.4 min; 80.08 % of 34,810 lines against the floor of 78.1 — 80.10 and 80.07 on the two rehearsal runs, 80.11 on the workstation: the figure moves by hundredths with thread timing, the floor is two points away), `frontend-e2e` (7.7 min: the quarantine list's check "3 of at most 6; exit 2026-10-31", then `default` **419 passed** in 6.4 min, then `quarantine` 3 passed in 8.8 s with no retry used), `qualification` (10.9 min: setup-support 381.5 s / 17 checks, workspaces 93.4 s / 6 checks). Compared with the S12 run's failed titles (35281353996: 93 = 91 snapshots + `audio-arm-countdown.spec.ts:45` + `audio.spec.ts:1054`): all 93 left, none arrived — the 91 with the baselines commit, the two with the page clock. **Five consecutive runs (Done).** The `frontend-e2e` job was then run four more times on the same commit (`gh run rerun 35337703363 --job …`; the other nine jobs kept their first result), each on a fresh runner: attempt 2 — 419 passed (5.5 min) + 3 passed; attempt 3 — 419 (6.5) + 3; attempt 4 — 419 (6.5) + 3; attempt 5 — 419 (6.6) + 3; no retry used and no quarantined case failed in any of them. With the rehearsal run 35336198990, whose tree differs from `ab581e4` only in this ledger and in the wording of two `seen` sentences in `quarantine.json`, that is six green executions in a row, and 2,514 default-project cases without one failure on a platform where the job had been red on thirty runs out of thirty. The rehearsal runs, for the record: 35335389634 (`654657c`) — nine jobs finished, `frontend-e2e` red on exactly the two Storybook audio shells whose linux captures did not exist yet (417 passed / 2 failed, quarantine 3 passed), `qualification` cancelled by the next push; 35336198990 (`f5c0af7`) — all ten green, 13.8 min. The branch `rehearsal/s13-test-posture` is deleted on origin and locally; its runs and artifacts stay readable by id. The follow-up's own run is not recorded, by convention.

## Slice 14 — Front-end structure

Status: verified 2026-09-18 (no hardware exposure; every lane green on the workstation on the final tree — see Validation; commit `354306c` `Readiness S14: the three orchestrators split by line range, a chunk per workspace, the file-size allowlist gone`; CI run 35354775196, all ten jobs green — see CI below). Closes F26. Depends on S9 S11 (D3: last implementation slice).

**Rescope:** fourteen deviations, none silent, and the title stands because what landed is what it says. (1) **The premise.** The design said "remove the three allowlist entries" (Lighting, Planning, the pilot). On inspection `scripts/file-health.mjs` excused four sources and only one was on that list: `LightingWorkspace.tsx` (3,170 lines), `fixtureTransport.ts` (6,746), `native/rust-engine/src/lighting/tests.rs` (3,480) and `AudioWorkspace.module.css` — whose entry was stale: the file is 387 lines since visual overhaul A split it. `PlanningWorkspace.tsx` (1,771) and `SetupSupportPilot.tsx` (1,713) were under the cap and never listed. F26's own text is "the 2,000-line guard is bypassed by an allowlist that keeps growing", so "no source file > 2,000 lines" is read literally: all four entries are gone, and with nothing left to excuse the allowlist for source files is removed from the script rather than left empty (the 1.5 MB artifact allowlist, one design image, stays). (2) Planning and the pilot are split although neither was over the cap: F26's fix names the three orchestrators and D3 says the same; the line cap was never the reason. (3) **Folder names.** The design's `lighting/clusters/*` + one `useLightingEditor.ts` could not land as written — the hook alone would have been 2,330 lines, over the cap it was meant to clear — so `useLightingEditor.ts` composes six hooks under `lighting/editor/` (rig, session, scene editor, fixture editor, rig controls, commands) and the render is six components under `lighting/regions/` ("cluster" names one region in system A; a folder of bay, plate and dialogs called `clusters` would mislead). Planning is `planning/editor/` (view, actions, shortcuts) + `planning/bays/` as designed. (4) The design's `setup/support/{WorkstationSection,BackupsSection,DiagnosticsSection}` describes a layout visual overhaul A Slice 7 replaced: the bay shows the runner's step or the support screen, and the workstation's controls are the `SupportPlate`. The pilot is split along what exists: `setup/pilot/` (state, actions, shortcuts, chrome), `setup/steps/` (import, probe, map + verify, publish) and `setup/support/` (the support screen, the dialogs, and `SetupWorkstationPlate.tsx`, which takes the plate's wiring — Recent actions and the light-outputs switch among it, as Slice 11 asked). (5) **`manualChunks` per workspace is not used.** The shell reads three modules from workspace folders statically (`audio/audioFormatting`, `lighting/lightingDrift`, `lighting/useUnsavedScenePrompt`); a folder rule would put them in the workspace's chunk and the shell would fetch the whole workspace at startup to reach them. The chunks are cut by the four `import()` calls in `FA/app/workspaceChunks.ts` and named by them (`LightingWorkspace-*.js`, `AudioWorkspace-*.js`, `PlanningWorkspace-*.js`, `SetupSupportPilot-*.js`); the vendor rule is unchanged. (6) **`build.cssCodeSplit: false`**, not in the design: split styles are appended when their chunk arrives, so which of two equally specific rules wins would depend on which workspace the operator opened first — a fault no capture could find, since every capture opens one fixture fresh. One stylesheet, one order. (7) The design loads "after `ready`". The active workspace's chunk is asked for in an effect after the shell's first draw instead (the startup surface is on screen before any workspace code is fetched, which is F26's point), so it is in hand when the hardware link reports ready; the other three follow at idle once ready. A chunk in hand is given to `lazy()` as a thenable that answers at once, which React supports and which never suspends: a workspace switch mounts in the commit that asks for it, as it did before the split. The fallback is a new pre-ready surface, `startup/WorkspaceLoadingSurface.tsx` (`data-testid="workspace-loading"`, never a workspace's id); a failed chunk fetch gives the workspace boundary a fresh `lazy()` so "Reload this area" can fetch again. (8) **What is not a move.** Every moved body is a line range of the original, assembled by script and checked line for line (Validation). The edits: six dependency arrays that gained state setters or refs which now cross a hook boundary and which `react-hooks/exhaustive-deps` therefore asks for — setters and refs are stable, so no effect re-runs differently (Lighting: the command-palette registration, the two quick-palette effects, the shortcuts; Planning: the shortcuts; the pilot: the shortcuts); `boardColumns` and `planningFilters` hoisted from the Planning component to module constants because two files read each; Planning's inline props type written out as an interface; two JSX text lines prettier re-joined at the new indentation (`{" "}` + newline became a space — the same text node); and (9) one sentence: the delete-scene confirmation read "you can undo it from the mhe deletion" since visual overhaul A Slice 8's copy pass (`4638f6a`, where "the next toast can undo the deletion" was garbled); it reads "from the message that confirms the deletion". No board shows the dialog and no test pinned the sentence. (10) The pilot's feedback band — Slice 11's note — is on system A's material now (`var(--color-surface-raised)`, `--color-surface-border`, `--radius-key`: what the design system's `StatusBand` is made of) instead of its pre-A gradient pair, and the allowance in `ui-contract.spec.ts`'s held-state case is gone: the band is measured like everything else, in three themes. The element, its test id and `data-tone` are unchanged (the pilot's Vitest reads all three), which is why it is restyled rather than replaced by `StatusBand`. `.utilityRow` and `.degradedBanner`, which share the rule, are drawn by nothing (`grep` over `src`), so no board could show them. (11) **The fixture double** is split although the design does not name it: 6,389 lines moved into twelve modules under `EC/transports/fixture/` — `json`, `audioMetering`, `audioConsole`, `planning`, `lightingCatalog`, `lighting`, `state`, `requestContext`, and one request handler per domain (`lightingRequests` 1,402 lines, `audioRequests`, `planningRequests`, `setupRequests`) — leaving `fixtureTransport.ts` at 176: the startup gate, the meter ticks, the snapshot reads and a loop that offers a request to each domain. The arms needed nothing of the closure but `state` and `emit`. `scripts/check-operator-copy.mjs` skipped the double by file name and now skips its folder too (`SKIP_DIRECTORIES`), for the reason already written there. (12) **What the double still does not do** (Slices 9 and 11 left both for this slice to decide): it answers `{}` and raises nothing for `lighting.fixture.identify`, `.highlight`, `.identifySequence`, `.identify.clearAll`, `lighting.scene.reorder`, `.pin`, `lighting.group.reorder` and `lighting.fixture.delete`, and records a Recent-actions row for `lighting.output.setArmed` only. Decided: not in this slice. S14 is provable because nothing but position changed; new behaviour inside the files whose line-for-line identity is the proof would undo that. Each has an obvious home now (`fixture/lightingRequests.ts`), and doing it properly needs the reply shapes read from `E/lighting/*` and a Playwright case per method — recorded under Notes for later slices. (13) The Rust lighting tests are not front-end, and are split anyway so the allowlist could go: `lighting/tests.rs` → `lighting/tests/{mod,catalog_and_preview,fixtures_and_overlays,ordering}.rs` (101 / 1,229 / 1,159 / 1,007 lines), helpers in `mod.rs`, all 3,150 non-blank lines below the helpers verbatim, the same 64 tests. (14) **Operator ruling, 2026-09-18, at the start of this session: only the Windows build and only 2560×1440 matter; Linux, macOS and every other resolution are to be disregarded.** No work in this slice was done for another platform or viewport: the rehearsal-branch technique was not used, no linux capture was looked at, and the existing fallback-viewport and linux guards were left as they are. Of the fifteen spec sites hardened below, one (`lighting.spec.ts` "MacBook-sized viewport") is a fallback-viewport case — it was touched only because it failed for the general reason, not for its viewport.

Scope (as landed): `FA/app/lighting/LightingWorkspace.tsx` 3,170 → 60 lines over `useLightingEditor.ts` (24), `lightingWorkspaceModel.ts` (152: the module-level helpers, exported), `editor/useLightingRig.ts` (90), `useLightingSession.ts` (226), `useLightingSceneEditor.ts` (685), `useLightingFixtureEditor.ts` (901), `useLightingRigControls.ts` (360), `useLightingCommands.ts` (448), `regions/LightingClusterRegion.tsx`, `LightingPlatePanel.tsx`, `LightingBayRegion.tsx`, `LightingPaletteQuickPanel.tsx`, `LightingBottomStrips.tsx`, `LightingDialogs.tsx`; the DOM order inside the shell element is unchanged (cluster, bay and drawer, quick palette, bottom strips, footer, dialogs), and the quick-palette key listener is still registered before the shortcut listener, because its Escape must win. `FA/app/planning/PlanningWorkspace.tsx` 1,771 → 145 over `usePlanningEditor.ts`, `planningWorkspaceModel.ts`, `editor/usePlanningView.ts` (400), `usePlanningActions.ts` (517), `usePlanningShortcuts.ts` (195), `bays/PlanningBoardBay.tsx`, `PlanningTimelineBay.tsx`, `PlanningUnscheduledTray.tsx`, `PlanningScreenHead.tsx`, `PlanningClusterRegion.tsx`, `PlanningLoadingSurface.tsx`. `FA/app/setup/SetupSupportPilot.tsx` 1,713 → 109 over `useSetupPilot.ts`, `setupPilotModel.ts` (206), `pilot/*` (4), `steps/*` (4), `support/*` (3). `FA/app/workspaceChunks.ts`, `FA/app/startup/WorkspaceLoadingSurface.tsx`, `FA/app/OperatorShell.tsx` (the four static imports gone, two effects, one `Suspense`), `frontend/app/vite.config.ts`. `scripts/file-health.mjs` (no source allowlist), `scripts/check-operator-copy.mjs`. Result in `dist`: the entry script 324,941 bytes (was 754,346), `LightingWorkspace` 207,518, `AudioWorkspace` 131,105, `PlanningWorkspace` 65,456, `SetupSupportPilot` 51,506, vendor 279,801 (unchanged), one stylesheet 307,616 (was 300,364 + 7,484 in two). The largest source file in the repository is now a spec, `audio.spec.ts` (1,977); the largest product source is `E/rme_totalmix_osc.rs` (1,787).

Gates before: `file:health` passed with four excused files, one of them 3.4 times the limit and one entry naming a file that no longer needed it — nothing checked that an entry was still true, and nothing stopped a fifth. Nothing asserted anything about the bundle: no test knew whether a workspace was part of the entry script. The UI contract's held-state case carried an allowance by test id for a gradient it could not otherwise pass.

Tests added: `scripts/file-health.test.mjs` (4 cases, each running the real script in a throwaway git repository: 2,000 lines passes; 2,001 fails and is named; all five source extensions are held and `.md` / `.json` are not; a path the old allowlist excused, at its old size, is refused and the script no longer contains the allowlist) — proved a guard: against `HEAD`'s script the last case fails (3 pass / 1 fail), against the new one 4 pass, the script restored byte-identical in between. `shell.spec.ts` `lazy workspace loads`: all four chunks are fetched on a ready fixture; each workspace's marker test id is in its own chunk's source and NOT in the entry script's (a workspace imported statically again moves its marker into the entry and fails this); and a `MutationObserver` proves the switch to the Console never draws `workspace-loading`. `tests/helpers/openFixture.ts` `expectWorkspaceMounted(page, workspace)` — one mark per workspace that only the mounted workspace draws.

Tests changed (old → new → why): fifteen spec sites sent a key, or read the DOM once, as their first step after `openFixture` with nothing awaited in between → each waits with `expectWorkspaceMounted` first → a workspace is a chunk fetched after the shell draws, so `openFixture` returning says nothing about the workspace being mounted (S13's two flakes were this shape). Twelve keys: `audio.spec.ts` ×3 (Ctrl+K), `lighting.spec.ts` ×3, `planning.spec.ts` ×4 (Shift+B), `shell.spec.ts` ×2; three one-shot reads: `lighting.spec.ts` "MacBook-sized viewport" (the only case that actually failed — it read `[data-toolbar-primary]` once and found the pre-ready surface's `title`) and two request-count baselines in `audio.spec.ts`. Found by script (`racy_keys.py`, `first_await.py`: the first awaited statement after every `openFixture`), not by waiting for them to fail. `data-testid="audio-workspace"` on the Console's own loading surface is unchanged: renaming it is a DOM change in a pure-move slice, and the two specs that click it already wait for their control. `ui-contract.spec.ts` held-state case: measures minus the band's one known gradient → the measures as they are → the band has no gradient any more.

Validation (workstation, 2026-09-18; the production app closed throughout, ports 38201 / 4173 / 6007 free before each lane, `release\native` untouched — shell 9,823,232 and engine 8,801,792 bytes as before): purity reports — Lighting 2,825 non-blank lines moved, 3 not verbatim (two of the patched dependency arrays, the sentence), 11 in no unit (the old signature, `const inspectorPanel = (`, `return (`, closers); Planning 1,579 moved, 1 re-joined by prettier, 12 in no unit (the old signature and the loading guard's wrapper); the pilot 1,389 moved, 0 not verbatim, 13 in no unit (the old signature); the fixture double 6,389 moved, 1 re-wrapped by prettier, 1 in no unit (`default:`); Rust 3,150 of 3,150. `npm run dev:check` green on the final tree (`scripts:test` 146 = 142 pass + the same 4 skipped; `frontend:test` 325, unchanged; Vitest coverage — app 9.05 / 7.78 / 8.25 / 9.24 %, design-system 52.18 / 56.85 / 53.38 / 54.32, engine-client 42.06 / 39.38 / 47.38 / 42.91, every floor held; `native:test` 428 passed / 1 ignored, unchanged, the lighting module's 64 among them). `npm run frontend:playwright:test` 423 passed in 3.5 min (420 `default` + 3 `quarantine`): every win32 visual-review and Storybook capture and all 81 UI-contract boards unchanged — zero visual diffs with the three splits, the chunks and the single stylesheet in place. `npm run tauri:workspaces:qualify` passed (6 checks, 22.9 s) and `npm run tauri:setup-support:qualify` passed (17 checks, 66.2 s) — both run the shell over the Vite dev server, where the chunks are separate module requests; `npm run tauri:visual:review` 54 passed. The first full Playwright run of the session had 1 failed / 422 passed (the one-shot read above); everything after the three extra waits was green. The dev-server lanes log a React warning that predates this slice (`<div>` inside the plate head's `<p>` in the Console's inspector); it is not S14's and is recorded below.

Baselines: none. No capture moved.

Operator hands: none.

Notes for later slices: S15 — the Playwright quarantine's exit date (2026-10-31) was left to "S14 or S15" and is S15's now: `audio-render-budget.spec.ts` needs a settle predicate instead of its 500 ms window, `audio.spec.ts` "marks simulated audio metering…" a page clock driving the fixture's metering interval; `audio.spec.ts` is 1,977 lines, so either fix must split the spec first or `file:health` — which excuses nothing now — fails. The fixture double's eight unanswered lighting methods and its single Recent-actions row (Rescope 12) are open, with `EC/transports/fixture/lightingRequests.ts` as their home. `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md` §5 ("Keep files modular") and §2c should say that the file-size guard has no allowlist and name `expectWorkspaceMounted`; `AGENTS.md`'s `file:health` line is still true. The Console's inspector nests a `<div>` inside `PlateHead`'s `<p>` (`AudioInspectorChannelHeader`): invalid markup that React warns about in every development build; a task chip was raised for it on 2026-09-18. Operator / after the program — per the ruling of 2026-09-18 the linux and darwin captures, the macOS release-evidence runner and the fallback-viewport guards are nobody's work; if one turns red for a reason that is not also a Windows 2560×1440 reason, it is recorded and left.

CI (result, 2026-09-18): run 35354775196 on `354306c` — all ten jobs green on the first attempt, 13.8 min from push to the last job. `frontend-e2e` 5.7 min: the quarantine list's check, `default` 420 passed (4.7 min), `quarantine` 3 passed (7.8 s) — so the linux captures held as well, with no work done for them (Rescope 14): the split, the chunks and the single stylesheet moved nothing on either platform. `rust` 2.4 min (428 passed / 1 ignored on the runner: shell 20, engine 399 + 1, contract 4, e2e 5), `rust-coverage` 2.3 min, `tauri-foundation` 2.4 min, `qualification` 11.3 min (setup-support and workspaces both passed over the Vite dev server, where the workspace chunks are separate module requests), `format-protocol` 1.5 min (`scripts:test` with the four new file-health cases, `file:health` with no allowlist), `frontend-test` 1.0 min (the coverage floors held on the runner), `supply-chain` 0.8 min, `frontend-typecheck` 0.6 min, `lint` 0.4 min. The follow-up commit's own run is not recorded, by convention.

## Slice 15 — Docs + closeout

Status: verified 2026-09-21 (no hardware exposure; every lane green on the workstation on the final tree — see Validation; commit `93603e1` `Readiness S15: the documents at the program's close, the Playwright quarantine emptied at its causes, two Rust tests wait on state`, and its quarantine-test follow-up `b098233`; CI below). Depends on all; the last slice. Every finding has its commit, its status and its guard (Traceability, Appendix A); the **program** stays open until Appendix B is signed, which is the operator's (item 8).

**Rescope:** ten deviations, none silent, and the title stands because what landed is docs and closeout. (1) **The metering case's premise.** Slice 14's note said "a page clock driving the fixture's metering interval". On inspection the assertion that failed (`audio.spec.ts` "marks simulated audio metering as test-stage movement": the four seeded front-preamp strips, rounded to whole percent, polled for 5 s until they read as more than one level) compares values the interval never moves: a strip's `--meter-level` is the Console's audio snapshot, fetched once at start (`audio.snapshot` ×1 in 6 s; the four values identical across five reads 500 ms apart while the canvas checksum changed on every read); only the painter's canvas follows the ticks. The levels are a function of `Date.now()` at that fetch, and a probe of the fixture double's own functions over 3,611 instants an hour apart found all four rounding to one whole percent at 73 of them (2.02 %) — the case failed on any runner at those instants, and polling could not rescue it. Reproduced: with the page clock pinned so the fetch happens at 2026-09-21 08:00:00.000 UTC the levels read 85.37 / 85.02 / 85.11 / 85.31 and the old case fails with `Expected: > 1, Received: 1, Timeout 5000ms exceeded while waiting on the predicate` — the shape recorded on run 34514042594 — and passes pinned two seconds later. The fix compares the four levels unrounded (no two were equal at any of the 3,611 instants; the smallest gap 0.00014 percentage points), which still fails when seeding gives two strips one envelope; and the page clock does drive what the interval does drive — the painter's first frame and its movement, formerly real-time polls of 5 s and 1.8 s, now `page.clock.runFor` on a paused clock (`page.clock.install()` before `openFixture`, `pausePageClock` once the Console is mounted, `resume()` before the second fixture). (2) **The render-budget cases' settle predicate** is what the note asked for, and what it waits for was found first: the Console renders three times as it starts — its mount, then the recall pulse on the snapshot slot the fixture double reports as just recalled (`AudioWorkspace.tsx`, `setRecentlyRecalledSnapshotId`), then that pulse's end on a 1.5 s timer (`AUDIO_RECALL_PULSE_MS`) — and the old baseline was read 500 ms after `audio-workspace`, which is also the id of the Console's loading surface. Measured with the old and the new cases under CDP CPU throttling (the old ones three runs each to ×16 and two at ×30 and ×50; the new ones as committed two at every rate, after a first round of three at ×1–×16 before their settle waits were lengthened): the old idle window counted 1, 1, 0, 0 and 1 renders at ×1, ×8, ×16, ×30 and ×50 and the sections case 4, 5, 4, 5 and 6–7 — whichever start-up renders the window happened to take in, the last one at its budget of 7; the new cases count idle 0 and sections 4 at every rate to ×16 and 0 / 4–5 at ×30 (one more start-up render separates from the burst there, inside both budgets); at ×50 their waits run out before anything is measured, as the whole suite's do. The wait is `openSettledConsole`: an init script records the pulse's start and end in the page with a `MutationObserver` on `data-flash`, so no read from the test can miss it, and the two waits before the baseline may take 20 s (the old `waitFor()` had the test's whole time). The idle window itself stays a real-time 1.5 s: run on a paused clock, forty-five ticks would reach React in one task and a per-tick re-render would be counted once. (3) **The empty list.** Emptying `quarantine.json` "per its own rules" (`"exit": null`, `"cases": []`, the rule text kept) exposed a trap nobody had hit: `playwright.config.ts` built `new RegExp("")` from an empty list, which matches every title — the `default` project, the one CI fails on, would have run nothing, and the whole suite would have run as advisory. The config now builds `(?!)` for an empty list, reads `SSE_PLAYWRIGHT_QUARANTINE_LIST` when set (so a test can hand it a list of its own), and `frontend:playwright:test:quarantine` passes with no tests (`--pass-with-no-tests`; without it Playwright exits 1 on "No tests found" and the CI step would warn every run). (4) **The spec split** the note asked for moved fifteen cases, not one: every metering case of `audio.spec.ts` — the strips' and the plate's meters, the painter's canvas, the fixture double's simulated metering and the meter formatters — into `audio-metering.spec.ts`, by line range, every body verbatim (568 lines, 516 non-blank; a purity check found each in order), leaving `audio.spec.ts` at 1,390 lines with its now unused imports pruned; the request-count case and the not-verified case between them stayed. (5) **The Rust pair.** `recall_pushes_the_snapshot_and_the_console_confirms_it` waits on the console link's state as the note asked: `settle_console_link(&pump)` returns once no send is pending, no read-back is outstanding and the test pump has flushed twice more (a new `#[cfg(test)] ConsoleLinkState::outstanding_count` and a cycle counter on the test's `SlotPump`), in place of each 500 ms sleep and once more before the stored state is read. `console_pull_that_never_goes_quiet_is_incomplete` does not wait on the fake console, because its verdict is time-based by design — the engine calls a pull complete after `quiet_ms` without a control message — and a 40 ms stream from one thread against a 150 ms window read by another is a race no wait removes: its quiet window is now 60 s against a 1 s timeout, so the fake still never goes quiet and the pull cannot see it go quiet; the rule under test (not quiet by the timeout, with something received, is incomplete) is unchanged, and the quiet rule itself is `is_complete`'s unit tests. (6) **The open items the session was asked to decide.** Taken: the `<div>` inside `PlateHead`'s `<p>` in the Console's plate head (the task chip of 2026-09-18 was never taken — no branch, worktree or commit since): `AudioInspectorChannelHeader` renders two `<span>`s, and `.inspectorSubtitle` gains `display: block` (`.inspectorEyebrowRow` is already a flex container), so the boxes are the ones the `<div>`s made. Handed on, with the reason: the fixture double's eight unanswered lighting methods and its single Recent-actions row (Slice 14, Rescope 12) — test-double fidelity, no finding rests on it, and doing it properly means the reply shapes read from `E/lighting/*` and a Playwright case per method; its home is `EC/transports/fixture/lightingRequests.ts`. Handed on, with the reason: the fixture double's health words (`ok` / `attention`, where the engine says `ready` / `not-verified` / `unconfigured` / `disabled` / `unavailable` — `E/audio/snapshot.rs`, `E/lighting/snapshot.rs`, `E/commissioning.rs`, `E/control_surface_http.rs`), the gap that hid `db2df4d`'s bug: `healthCheckTone` already maps both vocabularies to the same tones, so only the lamp's word would change — "not verified" for "attention" on the boards that show an unverified subsystem — a deliberate visual change with a win32 baselines commit of its own, not a closing docs slice's; `db2df4d`'s six tests hold the header in the engine's words; the rest of the fix belongs in `EC/transports/fixture/state.ts`'s health builder with `check-fixtures.mjs` holding the vocabulary. (7) **The footer's `Metering TotalMix · live`, checked before anything was written: it is a measurement.** The session prompt (and the memory note of the promotion) said the engine sets it whenever the probe has passed. `read_audio_snapshot` does — `metering_state` is `live` when the probe has passed — but the TotalMix metering thread publishes `audio.changed { reason: "metering-tick" }` every 33 ms while meter data flows and every 250 ms when it does not, with `meteringState` from the age of the last meter message (`status_at`: live ≤ 500 ms, stale ≤ 2,000 ms, else offline), and the store writes that into the snapshot the footer reads (`createShellStore.ts`). Measured with an engine from this branch on a scratch app-data and unused ports (Validation): frames `live` while a fake sender fed the Global OSC slot, `stale` 529 ms and `offline` 2,133 ms after it stopped, while `audio.snapshot` kept answering `live`. So it is measured, except for the moment between a fresh `audio.snapshot` and the next frame (≤ 250 ms); OPERATIONS says exactly that, and no product change was needed. What the workstation showed on 2026-09-21 — `live` beside still meters — was true: TotalMix was sending, and the meters were held by the Console's confidence gate (OPERATIONS, "When the Console's meters stay still"). (8) **OPERATIONS said four things the code does not.** The Console's healthy state display reads `VERIFIED`, not `READY` (`describeAudioStatus`; two places corrected); `OFFLINE` has two causes the guide merged — a stored probe failure (never re-run on its own: only `commissioning.check.run` runs the probe) and two seconds without meter data — told apart by their sentences, because the titles `describeAudioStatus` also returns (`CONSOLE UNREACHABLE`, `RME METERING OFFLINE`, `TALKBACK REFUSED`) are rendered nowhere since visual overhaul A; so a refused talkback reads `ACTION FAILED` with the code `AUDIO_TALKBACK_REFUSED`, not `TALKBACK REFUSED` (three places corrected); and the gate does not lock the Console on `DISCONNECTED` (`ensure_audio_action_allowed` checks OSC and the probe, `canEditMixerState` the same). Its live-session checklist sent the operator to "the `1920x1080` studio monitor"; it names display 3 at 2560×1440 now. The session prompt said TotalMix listens only on remotes 1 and 4 (7001 / 7004); at 14:50 on 2026-09-21 `netstat` showed TotalMix (PID 31180) on UDP 7001–7004 with four sockets sending to 127.0.0.1:9001–9004, so the guide's commissioned layout (remotes 1–3 classic, 4 Global OSC) stands as written. (9) **Beyond the design's list:** `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` gains the program's modules under "Current Module Ownership" (lines added, none changed); `CHANGELOG.md`'s consolidation also rewrote four earlier entries' forward references that later slices kept (Slices 0, 3 ×2 and 13). (10) **Operator ruling of 2026-09-18:** nothing in this slice was done for Linux, macOS or another resolution; the docs now state the ruling where they spoke of 1920 (README, AGENTS, DEVELOPMENT §2b, OPERATIONS).

Quarantine-test follow-up (2026-09-21, found by CI after the push; commit `b098233` `Readiness S15 (quarantine test): list one spec, so the check needs no Storybook build`): run 35605667300 on `93603e1` failed `format-protocol` in `scripts:test`. The new projects case ran `playwright test --list` over every spec, and `storybook.spec.ts` reads `storybook-static/index.json` when it loads — present on the workstation, where every Playwright run builds it, and absent on that job, which builds nothing. The case now lists `audio-render-budget.spec.ts` alone, which is all it needs: an empty list must leave both of its cases in `default`, and one listed case must move exactly that one. Re-proved: 16 pass normally, 16 pass with `frontend/app/storybook-static` moved away (the job's condition), 15 pass / 1 fail with `db2df4d`'s `playwright.config.ts` in place; the config restored and `cmp` byte-identical, `storybook-static` put back. The lesson for the next test that spawns Playwright: a spec may read a build at load time, so list or run only the specs the test is about.

## After the program

**2026-09-22 — the bridge's queue and the deck's LCD poll** (commit `7b85f04` `fix(bridge): room for the deck's once-a-second LCD burst; refusal lines count what they stand for`; not a slice). The operator exported the Stream Deck profile from Setup step 1 (three exports, 10:55:57–10:56:14) and imported it with Full Reset & Import. The live app's log has no `401` after 10:56:09. From 10:56:58 it has a `503` line (`The bridge is busy with other requests; retry shortly.`) every minute without a gap.

The bridge writes at most one line per status per minute (`REJECTION_LOG_INTERVAL`), so a line a minute is a floor, not a rate. **Correction:** the same holds for the `401` lines this ledger read as "Companion refused with `401` about once a minute" (the correction under Slice 11 and Slice 15's Operator hands). Every request was refused; the log wrote one line a minute.

The cause, read from the exported profile: its `SSE audio LCD poll` trigger (1 s) sends one `GET` per audio LCD key, 25 in all, at the same instant and one connection each. About 2,900 connections to the bridge's port were in `TIME_WAIT` at 10:59. The pool held 20 (`WORKER_COUNT` 4 + `QUEUE_CAPACITY` 16, Slice 2, F06), and a single key press can add up to 17 more (the audio page's key 4: its action and 16 LCD refreshes).

Reproduced on a scratch engine from this branch (its own app-data, bridge on 38297, simulated audio so no TotalMix port is bound, `SSE_SAFE_START=1`), driven the way Companion drives the live app for 20 seconds per run:

- 101 of 500 LCD requests were refused, the same five keys every second: `audio_state_dim`, `_talk`, `_solo`, `_gated` and `workspace`. The `SSE follow app` triggers read `workspace` to move the deck's page.
- A request arriving 0 or 2 ms after a burst began was refused 39 times out of 40; from 10 ms, none were.
- A refused connection closes before the client reads the `503`, which Node mostly sees as `ECONNRESET`.

Companion's generic-http module (2.7.0) keeps got's default retry, which repeats a refused `GET` about a second later but never a `POST`. So the five LCD values lagged by a second or two, and a key press that met a full bridge was lost.

The fix:

- `QUEUE_CAPACITY` 64. The workers stay at 4, so a flood still cannot grow the thread count.
- Every refusal line ends with how many refusals with its status went unwritten since the previous line: `… (1512 more with this status since the last such line)`.

Guards:

- `the_pool_holds_the_decks_worst_instant` opens the deck's worst instant as connections, before any request is sent, against a pool with the engine's constants, and requires every one served. The count comes from `exports::deck_worst_instant_requests()`: the poll's requests plus the most any one control step sends, counted from the generated profile (42 today). Proved: with `QUEUE_CAPACITY` 16 it fails, 22 of 42 unserved.
- `a_refusal_line_counts_the_refusals_it_stood_for`. Proved: it fails without the count.

The file was restored byte-identical after each proof. The reproduction re-run on the fixed engine served 500 of 500 requests at 25 a second and 840 of 840 at 42, refused no press at any offset, and wrote no refusal line.

Docs: `SECURITY.md` (the queue and the counted lines), `docs/OPERATIONS.md` ("Control-surface bridge stops responding", step 3: read refusals by their count), `CHANGELOG.md`. Appendix B item 1: the import is done. The operator signs the item once the fixed build is live and one key press and one LCD refresh are seen.

**2026-09-22 — Appendix B item 1 signed.** With `7b85f04` live, the operator pressed a deck key and watched an LCD refresh ("Key press and LCD refresh both work, sign item 1") and signed item 1. Slice 2 and its findings F01, F06 and F28 move from `landed` to `verified`.

**The ledger follow-up's run was red on `qualification`** (run 35714853611 on `5746f2b`, which changed docs only; the other nine jobs green). Step 4 of the setup-support lane failed `corrupt-db-restores-from-database-backup` with `Expected launch 1 after the restore's restart, got 2.` The app did what it always does; the lane misread it. The lane takes the launch number before the restore from the status it writes once the recovery screen lists the backups (`listedInRecovery.testBridge.engineGeneration`), and the test bridge fills that number in asynchronously: `engine_summary` is fetched after each change of lifecycle or last event and starts out `null` (`frontend/app/src/app/tauriShellTestBridge.ts`). This time the backups were listed before the fetch returned, so the number before was `null`, and `null + 1` is `1` in JavaScript. The engine in recovery was launch 1 and the restore's one restart made launch 2, the value every passing run records for this check (generation 2, eight times in this ledger). The shell had reached `ready` with no start-up failure; the checks after this one (the restored projects, the replaced file) were not reached.

The same code passed on 35712843386 thirteen minutes earlier. Not re-run. **Fixed the same day** by `e991cc8` `fix(qualification): the setup-support lane counts launches from numbers it waited for`. The new `scripts/tauri-launch-number.mjs` reads a launch number only when a status carries one (`launchNumberOf`) and gives `waitForStatus` a predicate for it (`carriesLaunchNumber(after)`). The lane now waits for its baseline, then for the next number, before it compares, at every place that was exposed: the recovery restore that failed, and step 2's requested restart and restore, which read both numbers from a command's status. Correction to this paragraph as first committed (`222c682`), which also named the engine-crash check: step 5 was not exposed, because it waits for the engine's process id, which comes from the same summary as the launch number, both before and after. `tauri-launch-number.test.mjs` holds the rule in three cases; all three fail when the module is given the old lane's behavior (the file restored byte-identical). The lane itself was verified by CI, not on the workstation, where it would have needed the live app closed again: run 35751453676 on `e991cc8`, all ten jobs green on the first attempt (13.3 min; `qualification` 10.8 min with all eight setup-support steps and both workspace steps; `format-protocol` with the three new cases, `scripts:test` 150 = 146 + the same 4 skipped). The run before it, 35749767806 on `222c682` with the old lane, was green as well: the race is intermittent.

**2026-09-22 — the fixture double answers the rig's eight lighting actions** (commit `7413b53` `fix(fixture-double): answer the rig's eight lighting actions as the hardware link does`; not a slice; the first half of what Slice 14 handed on in its Rescope (12) and Slice 15 in its Notes (a)). The browser test double, which every Playwright case and the fixture mode run against, answered `lighting.fixture.identify`, `.highlight`, `.identifySequence`, `.identify.clearAll`, `lighting.fixture.delete`, `lighting.scene.pin`, `lighting.scene.reorder` and `lighting.group.reorder` with `{}` and changed nothing. Against it the Highlight and Solo keys never lit, Identify and Find lit nothing, Delete fixture left the light on the plot beside a toast saying it was deleted, and a pinned or dragged scene or group went back where it was. No test pressed any of them.

The fix, in `frontend/packages/engine-client/src/transports/fixture/`:

- `lightingRequests.ts` answers each method with the fields of its result struct (`E/lighting/types.rs`), the refusals of `E/lighting/parse.rs` and the handlers word for word, and one `lighting.changed` with the reason `app.rs` gives it.
- `lightingOverlay.ts` (new): `lighting.snapshot` and the DMX monitor read the stored rig the way `E/lighting/snapshot.rs` does. An identify flash (100 %, on, the fixture's highest colour temperature) stands over a highlight (100 %, on, 4,500 K within the fixture's range), which stands over the solo mask (every other light off); none of them is written into the stored rig. The scenes are shown pinned first, each group in the stored order, so an unpinned scene goes back to its own place. `highlightFixtureIds` and `soloFixtureIds` are always present.
- `state.ts` keeps the identify flashes (`lightingIdentifyBursts`) by fixture, each with its start and length. A Find schedules them a step apart and replaces whatever was flashing or waiting; the clear-all behind Esc counts the finished ones not yet pruned, as the hardware link does.
- Mirrored, not changed: a deleted light's id stays in the Highlight and Solo sets and among the flashes, and the two reorder sentences name the id, not the name. The hardware link does both.

Guards:

- `frontend/packages/engine-client/src/transports/fixture/lightingRequests.test.ts`: 8 Vitest cases, one per method — the reply's fields and values, the event's reason, what `lighting.snapshot` shows after the action and after a flash has run out, and the refusals. All 8 fail against the double at `HEAD` (its two files swapped in; the fix restored afterwards).
- `frontend/app/tests/lighting-rig-actions.spec.ts`: 8 Playwright cases, one per method, each through the screen — the stage plot's marker levels, the Highlight and Solo keys' pressed state, the scene tiles' "N on", the rails' order. All 8 fail against the double at `HEAD`. With only the clear-all answer disabled, the Esc case fails at its own step (Back still at 100 percent, not at the Find before it). Identify and Find stop the page's clock and move it on by hand. The two drag cases use dnd-kit's keyboard drag and wait for two things a quick keyboard skips: the sensor starts listening for the arrow keys on a timer after the pick-up (the case waits until a timer it queues behind that one has fired), and which item the dragged one is over settles a render later (the drop waits for dnd-kit to announce the neighbour to a screen reader).

Found on the way, not changed (open):

- On a scene tile and a group chip, dnd-kit's key handler replaces the item's own, because it is spread after it (`SceneTile.tsx`, `GroupChip.tsx`: `onKeyDown={handleKeyDown}`, then `{...listeners}`). So Enter on a focused scene tile picks it up for a drag instead of recalling the scene, F2 does not open the rename, and Enter on a focused group chip picks it up instead of switching the group on or off, as its label says it will. Checked in the built app against the double: after Enter the tile carried `data-dragging` and no request was sent. Clicking, and recalling by number key, are unaffected.
- The double leaves nine more methods unanswered — `planning.context`, `planning.project.delete`, `planning.project.update`, `planning.select`, `planning.task.checklist.delete`, `planning.task.update`, `settings.get`, `storage.importLegacyDb`, `dev.parityFixture.load` — but nothing in the front end sends any of them (no store method, no call site), so no test can be misled by them.

**2026-09-22 — the fixture double's Recent actions** (commit `a598b11` `fix(fixture-double): Recent actions list every action the screen asks for, as the hardware link records it`; not a slice; the second half of the item Slice 14 handed on in its Rescope (12)). The double wrote a Recent-actions row for `lighting.output.setArmed` only, from inside that one handler. Against it nothing done on the Lighting page, the Console or in Setup showed in Setup / Support's Recent actions, where the hardware link writes a row for every discrete action that reaches a device (Slice 11, F30).

The fix, in `frontend/packages/engine-client/src/transports/`:

- `fixture/actionLog.ts` (new) is `native/rust-engine/src/action_log.rs`'s screen half: the 24 methods of `RECORDED_UI_METHODS`, the five of `PREVIEW_AWARE_UI_METHODS`, and `uiActions`, which gives each request the rows `ui_actions` gives it, sentence for sentence — nothing for a lighting change staged in the preview.
- `fixtureTransport.ts` writes them where the hardware link does (`EngineApp::handle_request`): after a request has been answered, newest first, ids that only grow, fifty kept. A refused request never gets there. Nothing is written while the start failed (the hardware link's recovery mode has no storage), and writing a row raises no event, as on the hardware link.
- `fixture/lightingRequests.ts`: the light-outputs switch no longer writes its own row; the hook writes the same one.
- The double's replies already carried every field the rows read (a channel's name, the scene, the fade, the palette, the TotalMix address), so no reply changed.

Guards:

- `frontend/packages/engine-client/src/transports/fixture/actionLog.test.ts`: the two method lists are read from `action_log.rs` and compared, so a method the hardware link starts recording fails here first; 34 cases hold the sentences of all 24 methods (each filled in from `ui_actions`' format strings, the cases that leave no row included); and four cases drive the transport — rows newest first with growing ids and the source `ui`, none for a refusal or a change staged in the preview, fifty kept, the rows a scenario starts with carried on, and none while storage was refused at start. The four transport cases were run against the double as committed in the fix before (its `fixtureTransport.ts` and `lightingRequests.ts` swapped in, the new module left in place so the file loads): three fail — the one that passes is the scenario case, because the old switch wrote that row itself.
- `frontend/app/tests/setup.spec.ts` "Recent actions lists what was done on the Lighting page, newest first, from the screen": Highlight on and off, Identify, then a recall, and Setup / Support lists all four with Screen as the source. It fails against the double without the hook (no row at all).

Found on the way, not changed (open): **the Lighting page goes on showing an identify flash after it has ended.** The flash is shown over the rig while it lasts (`E/lighting/snapshot.rs`, and now the double), the page reads the lighting state right after the press, and nothing reads it again when the flash ends: no event is raised when a flash ends, and nothing on the page re-reads the lighting state on a timer. So after Identify the light reads 100 percent at its highest colour temperature, the header says "Scene drift: unsaved", and both stay until something else refreshes Lighting — leaving the page then asks about unsaved changes nobody made. Seen with the double three seconds after a press; not checked on the live app, where the hardware link does the same by its code. A Find leaves its first light's flash on screen the same way; the later flashes are never read. The browser case above runs the flash out on a stopped clock before the recall for that reason.

**2026-09-22 — Setup reads the Stream Deck bridge and the health checks in the hardware link's words** (commit `d3adaca` `fix(setup): the bridge row and the recovery diagnostics read the hardware link's own words`; not a slice; found while preparing the fixture double's health words, Slice 15 Notes (a)). The hardware link names the Stream Deck bridge `ready` or `unavailable` (`app.snapshot` → `runtime.controlSurface.status`, `E/app_state.rs`) and reports each health check in its subsystem's own words: `ready`, `not-verified` and `attention`, lighting also `unconfigured` and `disabled`, the bridge `ready` and `unavailable` (`E/health.rs`). The browser double says `ok` and `attention`. `db2df4d` taught the header's lamps the hardware link's words; three places still knew only the double's:

- Setup's Import step ("Companion link") and Publish step ("Companion profile") lit the bridge row `ok` only for the word `ok` (`asStatusTone(status, "info") === "ok"`), so a serving bridge, `ready`, lit it yellow.
- The recovery screen's diagnostics (the deck, the bridge, the desk) mapped each check through `asStatusTone`, so after a failure a `ready` check read "Needs attention".

All three now read the status through `healthCheckTone`, the header's mapping; a check that reported nothing keeps its fallback. The double's `ok` and `attention` map the same through it, so no board moved. Found by reading the code, not on the workstation: the runner's steps show only while commissioning, and the recovery screen only after a failure. The live build (`7b85f04`) still has the old reading; the fix reaches the workstation with the next build of this branch.

Guard: `frontend/app/src/app/setup/SetupHardwareLinkWords.test.tsx`, three cases fed the hardware link's words — Import (a serving bridge lit `ok`, a bridge that could not bind `attention`), Publish (a serving bridge lit `ok`), and the recovery diagnostics (Ready, Ready and Needs attention for `ready`, `ready` and `not-verified`). All three fail on the code before the fix: `attention` for a serving bridge, "Needs attention" for a `ready` check.

Seen on the way, not changed: the Setup pilot maps the overall health status through `asStatusTone(status, "info")`, so the hardware link's `warning` (a late or failed backup, Slice 8) is `info` there, while the store's recovery machine calls it degraded. The pilot shows the health sentence either way; which tone a late backup deserves is a design question, not a misread word.
