**2026-09-22 — the fixture double says the hardware link's health words** (commit `f29bbad` `fix(fixture-double): the health checks and the Stream Deck bridge say the hardware link's words, worked out on every sync`; not a slice; the health-words part of Slice 15 Notes (a)). The browser test double answered each health check (`health.snapshot` → `checks.lighting`, `.audio`, `.controlSurface`) with `ok` or `attention`, from its probe alone — the deck's from the deck's probe — and the Stream Deck bridge's own state (`app.snapshot` → `runtime.controlSurface.status`) the same way. The hardware link says the rig in five words (`unconfigured`, `disabled`, `ready`, `attention`, `not-verified`, from the stored bridge address, whether lighting is switched on and the probe: `E/lighting/snapshot.rs`), the console in three (`ready`, `attention`, `not-verified`, from its probe alone: `E/audio/snapshot.rs`) and the deck by whether its bridge is serving (`ready` / `unavailable`: `E/control_surface_http.rs`), never by whether the deck was verified; `E/health.rs` passes those words on, with `ok` true for `ready` alone. So every Playwright case and the fixture mode saw words the workstation never shows, and the screen's reading of the hardware link's words (`db2df4d`, `d3adaca`) had no browser case. The header showed it: on the populated Lighting board the Lighting, Audio and Deck lamps all read "attention", where the workstation would say ready, not verified and ready.

The fix, in `frontend/packages/engine-client/src/transports/fixture/state.ts`:

- The rig's word is worked out on every sync by the hardware link's rule: the address a lighting probe stored (or the rig's own), lighting on unless switched off and on by default once there is an address, then the probe's outcome. A lighting probe stores the address it is given before it runs, passed or failed (`E/commissioning.rs`), so a probe that has run means an address even where a scenario leaves it out. Until a probe runs, a scenario's own rig status stands in for it. The lighting state's `status` and `enabled` and the health check now agree; only the fixtures' own `loading` (the board where the rig has not answered yet) stays in the lighting state, and it never reaches the health check.
- The console's word comes from its probe alone, OSC on or off, as on the hardware link — also on the start where the console has not loaded, which returned before the health checks were written.
- The deck's check and `runtime.controlSurface.status` say whether the bridge is serving: `ready`, or `unavailable` when the scenario's bridge could not bind. Each check carries `ok`.
- The scenarios still say `ok` for a probe that passed, `error` for one that failed and `attention` for one not run or not confirmed; the double reads them so, as its audio state always did.

`frontend/packages/test-fixtures/`: the health and bridge words the three setup scenarios carry are the ones the double serves (twelve words), and `check-fixtures.mjs` refuses any other word for the three checks, the bridge, the console and the rig (the rig's state may also say `loading`; a health check may not). `frontend/app/src/app/shellData.ts` and its test no longer say that the double speaks `ok` / `attention`; those two still map as they did.

Review. An adversarial review of the first version (finders and two skeptics each) found that it wrote the rig's word once, from the scenario, so a lighting probe run in the session never reached the lighting state or the header — the frozen word this fix exists to remove. The version committed works the words out on every sync. A second review of that version (four lenses: the hardware link's rules, state that goes stale, the moved captures and the tests; two skeptics per finding; 46 agents) found no defect in the change and five gaps, all closed before this commit: four rules no case held (the older `ok` read as passed, a scenario's own `attention` standing in for a failed probe, a failed probe and a stored address each meaning an address, the switch after a probe run in the session), and two scenario words that disagreed with what the double serves (`setup-required` and `setup-degraded` said lighting `not-verified`; the double serves `unconfigured`, because neither has a bridge address). Sixteen other findings were refuted, most as older than the change and not made worse by it; the ones worth keeping are listed below.

Guards: `frontend/packages/engine-client/src/transports/fixture/healthWords.test.ts`, 10 Vitest cases:

- every scenario answers in the hardware link's vocabulary, with `ok` true for `ready` alone and the bridge's state in `app.snapshot`;
- the rig's words by the rule, the older probe words included, and the published scenarios' full answers;
- each scenario's own words are the ones served;
- the lighting state and the health check agree, and the switch with them;
- `loading` stays out of the health check;
- a lighting probe run after start moves both: `setup-required` to `ready` on 10.0.0.5 and to `attention` on 0.0.0.0, the switch on in both, and `lighting-populated` to `attention` on 0.0.0.0;
- the console from its probe, before and after a probe run and with OSC off;
- the start with no console loaded;
- the deck serving and refused.

All 10 fail against the double at `HEAD` (its `state.ts` swapped in, the fix restored byte-identical), and each of the eight mutations the second review named fails at least one case.

Boards: 27 captures moved, every one in the header's lamp strip only. A pixel diff of each against its baseline shows one region, the top 48 px; below it no channel moved more than 7 of 255 (render noise).

- Audio "attention" → "not verified" where its probe has not run.
- Deck "attention" → "ready": its bridge is serving.
- Lighting "attention" → "ready" on the Lighting boards, whose rig is ready. Elsewhere the page's own "no bridge" outranks the health word, before and after.

They are the nine `lighting-populated` and nine `planning-populated` captures (every viewport and theme), `lighting-empty`, `lighting-dmx-unreachable`, `planning-empty`, `setup-degraded` and `audio-not-verified` at 2560×1440, and the Storybook shells of `setup-required`, `setup-degraded`, `lighting-populated` and `planning-populated`. **By the operator's decision (2026-09-22), only the 15 win32 captures at 2560×1440 are refreshed**, in the `(baselines)` commit after this one (`293678b`). The 12 win32 captures at other sizes (1280, 1440, 1600, 1728 and 1920 wide, and the 1512×982 studio preview, two boards each) and all 27 linux captures keep their old lamp words. So the local Playwright lane fails those 12, and CI's `frontend-e2e` those 27, until someone refreshes them (Baseline refresh procedure).

Found on the way, not changed (open):

- The double's rig state keeps `reachable` and `connected` from its first sync, from the scenario or the default rig (`false`). The hardware link works both out on every read as `enabled && probe passed` (`E/lighting/snapshot.rs:14`). The header's Lighting lamp reads `reachable`, so on the Console boards (`audio-*`) and `setup-ready`, whose lighting probe passed, it says "no bridge" where the workstation would say "ready". Older than this fix and not made worse by it.
- The double refuses the Grand Master. The Lighting page sends `lighting.settings.update { grandMaster }` (`useLightingRigControls.ts`), and the double answers only the scene and fixture selection, so moving the Grand Master against it shows "Grand master update failed." The hardware link takes it (`E/lighting/settings.rs`).
- The double's audio state still says `not-verified` while OSC is switched off; the hardware link's says the probe's word. The header reads the health check, which is right.
- The scenarios' probe words are still the older `ok` / `attention`, and the double's commissioning steps count `attention` as failed while its health checks read it as not run.

**2026-09-22 — every engine test that sends to the console runs one at a time with the console-link tests** (commit `a922b51` `test(engine): every test that sends to the console runs one at a time with the console-link tests`; not a slice; test code only). In test builds a guard drops every datagram aimed at a TotalMix port, but the send is still registered on the process-wide console link. Twelve tests did that without `SHARED_LINK_TEST_LOCK`: the six send tests in `E/rme_totalmix_osc/tests.rs`, five audio edit tests and the snapshot CRUD test in `E/audio/tests.rs`, and the native archive restore test in `E/support/tests.rs`. Run in parallel, one of them could replace a serialised test's pending send for the same key, so the recall test saw "adjusted by the console" (a review of D3 below measured it at 1 full run in 30 on `HEAD`); and the CRUD test's recall started and finished a push while another test's push was running ("122 values pushed, 0 confirmed", seen once in 3 runs with D3's new recall test). **2026-09-23 — a change made at TotalMix just before the app's own write no longer outlives it** (commit `919047b` `fix(console-link): the desk's confirmation of the app's own value is written too, and a flush writes what it drains before anything else can`; not a slice; Slice 15 Notes (b), now reproduced). The metering thread drains the console link every 100 ms and writes what the desk reported under `AUDIO_STATE_LOCK`. A report of a change made at TotalMix that was still waiting in the link when the app wrote its own value for the same parameter — a recall's or an edit's — was written after the app's value, and the desk's read-back of the app's value wrote nothing: outside a pull a confirmation queued nothing. So the app kept the older value while the desk had the app's, and a recall marked the console aligned. Separately, a flush took the reports out of the link before it held the state lock, so a write of the app's, or another flush that had taken the confirmations that followed, could land in between.

Reproduced first, on the code before the fix, deterministically (`E/audio/tests_console_ordering.rs`, new: a console model on loopback remembers what it is sent and answers read-backs; a pump reads its replies and sends the read-backs but never flushes, so only the code under test writes what the desk said):

- `a_change_at_totalmix_reported_before_a_recall_does_not_outlive_it`: Host is muted at TotalMix and the desk reports it; a recall that unmutes Host follows before any flush. The desk confirmed all 122 values, the recall said aligned, and the app still showed Host muted. 5 runs of 5.
- `a_change_at_totalmix_reported_before_an_edit_does_not_outlive_it`: Host's gain turned to 40 dB at TotalMix and reported, then set to 45 dB in the app and confirmed by the desk; the next flush left the app at 40.
- `a_flush_takes_the_console_reports_only_when_it_can_write_them`: with the state lock held, as by a write of the app's, a flush took the waiting report out of the link at once.

The fix:

- `E/rme_console_link.rs`: a confirming reply queues the value the app sent (`ConsoleUpdate::confirms_send`), and so does a send confirmed by absence (an off send or solo the desk's reply leaves out); during a pull the reported value is queued, as before, now marked the same way. Written in queue order, a confirmation lands after anything the desk reported before it.
- `E/audio/console_link.rs`, `flush_console_link`: after a cheap check that the link holds anything, it takes the state lock before it drains the link and holds it until written — the lock order the code already documented (state, then link). A drained update whose parameter the app has sent again since (a pending send in the link) is not written: the link queues nothing for a parameter while a send of it is pending, and every edit registers its send under the state lock before it writes, so that send is newer and its own read-back will confirm, adjust or expire it. Such a replaced change at TotalMix is still a row in Recent actions, and a talkback refusal among them is still acted on (`ConsoleFlushReport::talkback_refused` tells the screen). A confirmation writes no "… at TotalMix" row, and a confirmed level that rounds to the one sent (levels travel as 32-bit floats) changes nothing.
- `E/rme_totalmix_osc.rs`: `pump_global_slot_without_flush_for_test`, the test pump above.

Review. The first adversarial review of the fix (four lenses, two skeptics per finding, 40 agents) found three defects in its first version, all corrected before this commit: the desk's confirmation of an older send was written over a newer write of the same parameter — the defect in reverse; a Stream Deck dial turned a detent every 200 ms lost detents, and a quick mute or talkback press flashed back — now the replaced update is not written; every fader and volume confirmation counted as a change (0.62 came back as 0.6200000047683716), one extra write and screen refresh per move — now compared at 32-bit precision; and it measured the test isolation fixed in `a922b51`. It also proposed letting the metering thread skip a tick while the app writes; the second review (of the corrections, 22 agents, 8 of them cut short by a usage limit) showed that this starved the flush for the whole of a deck dial ride, so it was taken out again — the metering thread waits for the state lock as it did before the fix — and that a change at TotalMix the app overrode before the next flush lost its Recent-actions row, which it now keeps (`a_change_at_totalmix_the_app_overrides_before_the_flush_is_still_a_row`).

Tests changed (old → new → why):

- `readback_reply_within_tolerance_confirms_the_send`: "confirmations queue nothing" → three queued confirmations, each marked, the fader's as the position the app sent → the new contract.
- `pull_applies_every_dump_value_even_when_it_confirms_a_pending_send`: "no pull: confirmation queues nothing" → the confirmation queued as the app's value, and during the pull marked and carrying the dB the desk reported → the new contract; the comment's premise ("the app already holds the value") is what the reproduction disproved.
- `off_send_is_confirmed_by_absence_once_the_submix_reply_finishes`: also checks that the off node's confirmation is queued after the desk's report, as the value sent.
- `E/audio/tests_console_link.rs` shares its console model, `pull_test_db`, `serialize_shared_link` and the request helpers with the new module (`pub(super)`); the new tests live apart because this file would have reached 1,979 of its 2,000 lines.

Guards: the three reproductions, and `a_confirmation_of_an_older_send_does_not_overwrite_a_newer_one`, `the_desk_confirming_the_apps_own_level_writes_nothing`, `a_talkback_refusal_is_acted_on_though_the_hold_has_sent_talkback_again`, `a_flush_writes_an_expired_send_or_a_lost_connection_on_its_own` and the Recent-actions case above. Each part of the fix undone on its own fails at least one test (twelve mutations, each file restored byte-identical after). In one of those runs `recall_pushes_the_snapshot_and_the_console_confirms_it` failed as well; it is the test `a922b51` steadied, and it has not failed in the 37 full runs since.

Whether this path produced the one recorded symptom of the recall test (2026-09-18: the recall fully confirmed, `main_after.dim` false) is not shown; it is the path Slice 15 named, and it is closed.

Found on the way, not changed (open; each older than this fix, which neither causes nor worsens it):

- **Talkback on the studio desk as it is** (no talkback channel assigned): while the key is held, each heartbeat after a refusal sends talkback again and records "succeeded", so the Talkback key and its "refused" hint flicker every 750 ms and TotalMix gets `/controlroom/talkback 1` each time; a release that lands before the desk has answered the latest send leaves no refusal on screen. Each refusal is also logged as "Talkback off at TotalMix", beside a "Talkback on" row per heartbeat. Appendix B item 5's TALK step will show it until a talkback channel is assigned. **Closed 2026-09-23, not pursued:** the operator's talkback ruling (below).
- **A Sync during an edit:** during a pull a dump line TotalMix sent before the app's send reached it, but read after the send was registered, is taken as the desk adjusting that send and written; the send is then never read back and Sync writes aligned. With a talkback channel assigned, a dump's `talkback 0` read that way would clear the hold of a TALK press made during the Sync. That talkback variant is **closed 2026-09-23, not pursued** (the operator's talkback ruling, below); the finding itself stays open.
- A phones fader edit also writes the channel's `fader` (the main level) (`E/audio/channels.rs`), so which of the two the channel's fader shows depends on timing.
- Dynamics and send-mode edits rewrite the channel state without `AUDIO_STATE_LOCK`, so a flush that commits in between is undone.
- A flush whose database write fails has already taken the reports out of the link; they could be put back now that the drain happens under the lock.
- The metering thread waits for the state lock while the app writes, as before; it now also flushes after every confirmed send (reading the state, writing nothing unless something changed).

**2026-09-23 — starting the Stream Deck bridge is one line in the engine log** (commit `122cef2` `fix(bridge): starting the bridge writes one log line, from the bootstrap`; not a slice). Every start of the hardware link that served the bridge wrote `INFO Native control-surface bridge is serving deck actions and LCD payloads at …` twice, in the same second — a copy of the live app's log read 2026-09-22 holds 84 such lines, two at each of 42 starts. Why: both writers date from the first bridge (`15b3a12`, 2026-04-16). `start_control_surface_bridge` wrote the line when it served, and nothing when it did not; the bootstrap then wrote the bridge's summary for either outcome (`INFO` or `WARN`). Slice 2 moved the starter into `E/control_surface_http.rs` with its line (`c2b7361`).

The fix: the bootstrap is the one writer. `start_logged_control_surface_bridge` (`E/bootstrap.rs`) starts the bridge and writes its line, `INFO` while it serves and `WARN` when its port was refused; the starter writes none. Nothing reads or counts the line (lanes, scripts, OPERATIONS).

Guard: `starting_the_bridge_is_one_log_line` (`E/bootstrap.rs` tests) starts the bridge on a port the system picks and then on a port another listener holds — never 38201 — and requires one `INFO` line and one `WARN` line. On the code before the fix it failed with the serving line twice.

**2026-09-23 — Enter and F2 on a scene tile, and Enter on a group chip, do what they say** (branch `after-the-program-2026-09`, cut from `main` at `1911559`; the finding recorded under `7413b53`; decision 7 of the operator's decisions). A scene tile (`SceneTile.tsx`) and a group chip (`GroupChip.tsx`) are dnd-kit sortable items. dnd-kit's `listeners` carry an `onKeyDown` of their own, the keyboard sensor's activator, and it was spread after the item's `onKeyDown={handleKeyDown}`, so it replaced it:

- Enter on a focused scene tile picked the tile up for a drag instead of recalling the scene;
- F2 never opened the rename;
- Enter on a focused group chip picked the chip up instead of switching the group on or off, as its label says ("… Toggle on.").

The fix: each item takes dnd-kit's `onKeyDown` out of `listeners`, runs its own handler first, and passes on to dnd-kit only a key its own handler left alone (`defaultPrevented` unset). Space still picks an item up, which is the key both rails document for a keyboard drag, and the one `lighting-rig-actions.spec.ts` drags with.

Guards: `frontend/app/tests/lighting-keyboard.spec.ts`, three Playwright cases on `lighting-populated`:

- Enter recalls the Interview scene (`aria-current`), and the tile is not picked up;
- F2 opens "Rename scene Interview", focused and holding the name, and Esc leaves it;
- Enter switches the Back group (`aria-pressed` flips), and the chip is not picked up.

All three fail with the two components as they were at `1911559` (the app rebuilt; each fails at the step that shows the defect), and pass with the fix. The two keyboard drags in `lighting-rig-actions.spec.ts` still pass.

**2026-09-23 — the fixture double takes the Grand Master** (branch `after-the-program-2026-09`; the finding recorded under `f29bbad`; decision 7). The Lighting page's Grand master sends `lighting.settings.update { grandMaster }` 200 ms after the last move (`useLightingRigControls.ts`). The double answered only `selectedSceneId` and `selectedFixtureId` and refused anything else, so against it the page showed "Grand master update failed." and a re-read put the master back at 100 %.

The double now reads `grandMaster` as the hardware link does (`E/lighting/parse.rs`, `parse_i64_value`): a number, rounded and clamped to 0–100, or refused with the same sentence. It stores it, names it in the summary before any selection (`E/lighting/settings.rs`: "grand master -> 63%"), and returns it in the reply. As the hardware link does, it checks every selection before it stores anything, so a request naming an unknown scene or fixture changes nothing. The DMX monitor already scaled by the stored master.

Guards:

- `lightingRequests.test.ts` "lighting.settings.update takes the Grand master, rounded and clamped, as the hardware link does": 62.6 → 63, 140 → 100, −5 → 0, a master together with a fixture selection and its summary, a text value refused, and a refusal with an unknown scene storing nothing.
- `lighting-rig-actions.spec.ts` "The Grand master is taken and kept": Home on the Grand master slider, the stopped page clock moved past the 200 ms commit, then Setup and back to Lighting. The readout still reads 0 % and no failure toast shows.

Both fail against the double as it was at `1911559` (the Vitest case with "lighting.settings.update requires one or more supported fields", the Playwright case with the readout back at 100 %), and both pass with the fix.

The double's "no bridge" on the Console boards stays as it is, by the operator's decision: fixing it moves captures.

**2026-09-23 — the Lighting page shows an Identify or Find flash end** (branch `after-the-program-2026-09`; the finding recorded under `a598b11`; decision 7). The hardware link stores each flash with its start and length and works out at every read whether it is still lit (`E/lighting/identify.rs`, `active_identify_burst_ids`). It announces nothing when a flash starts or ends. So the page read the lighting state once, during the flash, and went on showing it: the light at 100 % and its highest colour temperature, and "Scene drift: unsaved", until something else refreshed Lighting. Leaving the page then asked about unsaved changes nobody had made, and a Find showed its first flash only.

The fix is in the shell's store, which already decides what to read and when:

- `identifyFlashes.ts` (new): `identifyFlashMoments(reply)` gives the moments at which an Identify or a Find changes the lighting state, read from the hardware link's reply. For an Identify (`durationMs`) that is the flash's end. For a Find (`fixtureCount`, `stepMs`, `durationMs`) it is every flash's start but the first, and every flash's end, capped at the hardware link's 64 fixtures.
- `createShellStore.ts`: after the reply of `identifyLightingFixture` or `startLightingIdentifySequence`, the store reads `lighting.snapshot` and `lighting.dmxMonitor.snapshot` again 60 ms after each of those moments. The hardware link timed the flashes from before its reply left, so the store is never early.
- A clear-all, a restart and a dispose drop the reads still waiting.

No protocol, hardware-link or test-double change. The same store runs on the workstation and against the double, so the browser case below exercises the code the operator runs.

Guards:

- `identifyFlashes.test.ts`, 4 cases: an Identify, a Find (including a step as long as a flash, and one light), the 64-light cap, and replies that give no moment.
- `createShellStore.test.ts` "reads the lighting state again as each Identify or Find flash starts and ends", on the double with fake timers after start-up:
  - no read before 1,260 ms and one read at it, with the light off again;
  - five reads for a Find over three lights, and none after;
  - none after a clear-all.
- `lighting-rig-actions.spec.ts` "The page shows each flash start and end by itself: Identify, then Find", on the stopped page clock with nothing else pressed: Back lit, then off after 1.3 s; then a Find over Key and Back, with Back lit, then off with Key lit, then Key back at 76 %.

The store case fails with the store as it was at `1911559` ("expected 0 to be 1"). So does the Playwright case, at the Identify flash's end: the light still reads 100 percent.

**2026-09-23 — a phones fader edit leaves the main fader alone** (branch `after-the-program-2026-09`; the finding recorded under `919047b`; decision 7). A channel's `fader` is its Main Out level, and `mix_levels` holds every output's. The console link and Sync write `fader` for `audio-mix-main` alone (`E/audio/console_link.rs`, `E/audio/sync.rs`). `update_audio_channel` (`E/audio/channels.rs`) wrote the level into `fader` for every mix target, so a fader edit on Phones 1 or 2 overwrote the channel's Main level, and what the main fader showed depended on which was written last. Now it writes `fader` only for a Main edit, and `mix_levels` for every edit, as before.

Guard: `audio::tests::a_phones_fader_edit_leaves_the_main_fader_alone`. A Phones 2 edit on playback 7/8 keeps `fader` at its Main level and stores the Phones 2 level; a Main edit then writes both. It fails on `channels.rs` as it was at `1911559` (`left: 0.3`, `right: 0.52`).

**2026-09-23 — the dynamics and send-mode edits take the audio state lock** (branch `after-the-program-2026-09`; the finding recorded under `919047b`; decision 7). Every channel edit reads the stored channel map, changes it and writes it back under `AUDIO_STATE_LOCK`, which the metering thread's console flush also holds while it writes what the desk reported (since `919047b`). `update_audio_channel_dynamics` and `update_audio_channel_send_mode` did not take it, so a flush committed between their read and their write was undone by the write. Both take it first now, as `update_audio_channel`, `update_audio_channel_eq` and `clear_all_audio_solo` do. Only the request dispatcher calls them, and it holds no audio lock, so nothing can deadlock.

Guard: `audio::tests::dynamics_and_send_mode_edits_wait_for_the_audio_state_lock`. The test holds the lock on its own thread and runs each edit on a second one: the edit must not finish within 300 ms, and must finish once the lock is dropped. It fails on `channels.rs` as it was at `1911559` ("the dynamics edit went ahead while the audio state lock was held"). With only the send-mode edit's lock taken out, it fails on that edit.

## Baseline refresh procedure

Refresh only after a slice lands and only for diffs the slice explains; the refresh is its own commit `Readiness S<N> (baselines): …`. Build first: `npm run build --workspace frontend/app && npm run frontend:storybook:build`. win32 (this workstation): copy the `*-diff.png` files out of `frontend/app/test-results` before the update run, then `cd frontend/app && npm exec playwright test visual-review.spec.ts storybook.spec.ts -- --update-snapshots` (narrow with `-g "<title words>"`; titles are joined by spaces, not `›`), inspect every changed PNG before `git add`. linux: from the CI `playwright-test-results` artifact of the branch push run (since S0 a push produces it; `frontend/app/tests/__visual__/README.md`), copy each `*-actual.png` over its `*-linux.png` baseline; the pull request itself is the operator's (D4). Take the pairs from the `frontend-e2e` job log, not from the file names: every failed snapshot case prints `Expected: tests/__visual__/…-linux.png` and, on the next line, `Received: test-results/…-actual.png`, and a long snapshot name is cut and hashed in the results folder (S13 refreshed 91 this way; its `baselines_map.py` also compared each capture with its win32 sibling after an 8× box reduction, which is a cheap way to see that a capture is the same board before looking at it — and looking at them is what found two that were one solid mask colour). Since S13 the results folders carry the project name (`…-default`). darwin: pending the next macOS host visit (listed in `docs/HANDOFF.md`). By the operator's ruling of 2026-09-18 only win32 is refreshed from here on; the linux and darwin captures stay, and nobody works on them. By the operator's decision of 2026-09-22 (the fixture double's health words), only the win32 captures at 2560×1440 are refreshed: a capture at another size, or a linux one, that a change moves keeps its old baseline, the lane that checks it fails on it, and the change's entry says which. By the operator's decision of 2026-09-23 (After the program), the 27 linux captures that decision left stale are refreshed once, and all ten CI checks are required from the landing on. So a change that moves a board refreshes its linux capture too, from the branch push run's artifact, before it merges. The win32 captures at other sizes still keep their old baselines. Never run Playwright and a qualification lane at the same time — both bind 4173.
